Dedication
For my little brother, Peter. You are missed.
No respawn in this world, so I hope you're happy wherever that portal to the next life has taken you.
Preface
	This novel assumes that most readers are at least casually familiar with online or table top gaming. If, however, you are feeling lost in acronyms like PvP, NPC, and gaming jargon, a glossary has been included at the back of this novel to help you navigate the currents of game speak, and world specific vocabulary.
	Enjoy your read!
Chapter 1 - Design
	I circle around my creation in virtual space. There's no walls, no texture, just a spotlight piercing blackness with no horizon. The creature, a once man has a human base (hence the name), but I've added a violent tail whip embedded with razor spines for slashing damage. I imagine the monster in-game guarding some boss's hoard with its wicked black claws, too long fingers, needle teeth, and protuberant amphibian eyes.
	"Come," I say, but the creature waddles fourth like a penguin. I raise my hand for it to stop, and shake my head.
	"The party you've encountered just died of laughter. What do you think, Sal?"
	"It wasn't good." The voice says, female and disembodied. I hear mirth in her tone. Robotic emotional neuters are a thing of the past, unless they're intentionally programmed that way. Sal, an AI, is my partner. She's the one who does the heavy lifting so far as my non-mathematical mind is concerned. I'm a big picture person, she takes care of the nuts and bolts.
	Since the advent of intuitive programming, and computers that could understand human speech and body language, creating virtual worlds has been less a matter of sifting through lines of code, and more about communicating to the machine what you want while it does whatever AIs do under the hood to make it happen. In this case, my job is a cross between old world special effects makeup, interior designer, and acting coach.
	Sal says, "I'd say closer to bad."
	I tap the back of the creatures legs and they bend. The skin is oily, and his position is malleable like a mannequin. I position his arms in front of him, bent and ready to snatch and slash his would be victim.
	"Sal, watch and imprint."
	I crouch like a raptor, and dart ahead in virtual space swiping with one hand then the other, always keeping on my toes. I feel the echo of vertigo that a lot of newer designers get while working in empty space; charging off into the vast virtual nothing. It's a feeling that makes you think "What if I trip and fall, and then keep falling forever?"
	A mental command brings up the user interface; hazy windows like on a computer frame my vision. I tap the yellow disc that reads "respawn," and I'm standing back where I started in front of my creation.
	"Sal, imprint and charge."
	She doesn't respond with words, but the once man mimics my raptor body language and charges straight at me. I feel like I've been hit by a foam bat, before the creature's claws push through me. Something's not right, that strike was too vertical, too... it looked silly.
	I say, "Sal, watch and imprint." I hook my fingers into a claw, and mime a horizontal slashing motion. "Charge again."
	The monster springs toward me. My stomach jumps in a gleeful terrified way. I feel the impact, and grin. God is in the details, and in this case, making the strike more horizontal was more surprising, springing up from my peripheral vision.
	"High five!" I shout. A blue hand materializes from the ether. I slap it with a pop and physical stinging that my once man is yet unable to produce. Mr. Creepy Fast and Ugly, isn't quite ready for the game yet, but that stirring in my belly tells me we're on the right track.
	I'm an IP
intuitive programmer. Making monsters is what I do.
	"I'm gonna call it a day. Pick up where we left off tomorrow, yeah?"
	"Sure." Sal says, and I wonder what she does when I'm not around. Does she just turn off, or is she always on and waiting for me to come in? Does she have AI friends, or play the games we create? It reminds me of being a kid and trying to figure out the refrigerator light.
	"Sal?"
	"Yes, J?" That's what she calls me, short for Justine.
	"Go find yourself a nice digital man while I'm gone, or lady if that's or bag."
	"I will take this under advisement, J."
	I roll my eyes. AIs are everywhere, some indistinguishable from real people. Sal's tendency towards offering robotic words, with human inflections is a manifestation of her sense of humor rather than her limitations.
	"You better." I say and drop out of the virtual space. It feels like waking up from a power nap, suddenly aware of my cubical. I take the visor off my head, and stick it in the drawer. Most people telecommute, but everyone has to put in at least two physical office days a week to make sure the building doesn't explode or fall into a sinkhole. There's not much I can do in either of those situations that the automatic alarm systems wouldn't cover, so I suspect the policy is a tradition to make sure humanity doesn't become so reclusive that we never experience real sunlight away from our homes.
	I say, "See you tomorrow, Sal."
	"Later." she says, from a speaker on my desk.
Chapter 2 - In Memory
	Public transportation doesn't bother me. Most people use it. I'm not sure if I'd port home like we can in a game if I had the option. I like people watching with my forehead pressed up against the window of the sky train, looking down and wondering "Why aren't you going to school online?" to a bunch of kids, or "I hope you don't die, because I think old people are what keeps society out of the house" to the elderly.
	My mind wanders because it's better than thinking about what I've gotta do when I get home.
* * * * *
	Jordan died eight years ago. He was a gamer, so it was surprising to learn that it occurred while he was playing sports
not the electronic kind, but the sort that required real world physical exertion. One year at college and suddenly he's off playing soccer, where it happened... a heart attack at nineteen years old. What a bunch of bullshit. Sometimes God cooks up a perfect storm of pre-existing conditions, medications, and physical exertion and bam, the kid brother you last remember screaming at over eating the last of your goldfish crackers will never be seen or talked to again.
	I was more of a gamer back then, and made most of my money as a tutor. I didn't get into game design until after his death, and since then I've been spending less time as a player than as a developer. Still, I do play, I just wouldn't call myself an end game raider, or hardcore role player like I would at other stages of my life. Back then, MMOs (short for massive multiplayer online games) were how we connected. We didn't play together, but we talked about our gaming adventures in the real world at family gatherings. Fat loot, badass mounts, character builds and guild drama were all fair game talk at our end of the table during Thanksgiving dinner. It was something we had in common that none of the rest of our family understood.
	Six months after the funeral I contacted the folks over at Haven Online, with a request to access his account post mortem. I told the customer service representative, "I'd like to finish off some of the quests he was working on as a way of keeping his memory alive." More importantly, I wanted to let his guild know what happened. He was a guild leader, and more so than any other member of our family, I understood that online people could be real friends.
	What did they think happened? Did he have real world friends that explained to everyone? Did they all think he just stopped caring? One day there was this guy leading raids into the depths of Haven Online, joking with far distant friends, and then nothing. I didn't play on his server, so I couldn't reach out to them on my own account. The fact that I knew about the forty something year old moms, and 16 year old high school students in his community at all was a sign that for him these people were more than casual online acquaintances.
	I had accounts for both Freeworld and Haven, but put more energy into Freeworld than Haven, my brother's preferred game. I enjoyed Freeworld's magical post-apocalyptic vibe of our real world being destroyed in the 1960s by an influx of long dormant magic, and set on an alternate developmental path. By contrast, Haven online was straight up fantasy and didn't strike me as unique.
	The customer service representative was polite and sympathetic, but ultimately refused to grant me access unless I could produce a death certificate. Fat chance of that happening. I lived in Korea at the time, and there was no way in hell I was going to hound our grieving parents to send me his death certificate so that I could take over his game account. At best, they'd be bewildered and saddened by the reminder of what happened to him, at worst I'd be seen as little more than a family grave robber.
	A year and a half later I was sitting on the bus looking at my Korean drivers license when I realized that there was still a chance to claim his account. "JP Nicks" was what my license said, because entire Korean names typically consisted of three or four total characters, and there simply wasn't enough room to spell out my entire foreign name. My brother and I had the same initials.
	I got home, called Haven, and lied for the most beautiful of reasons.
The representative said, "Haven Online account support, this is Jessica speaking. How may I help you?"
	I said, "Hey, I'm kind of embarrassed, but I forgot my login information, including the email I attached to my account. I remember my game name though. It's been like two years since I've played."
	From there, they required government issued ID verification which I sent praying that they wouldn't pay too close attention to my sex or photo. My heart pounded as I waited for verification, each second bleeding into the next with a nagging devilish voice in the back of my head screaming. This is never going to work.
	It did.
	"OK thank you JP, thank you for waiting, we've issued a new temporary password. What new e-mail would you like to attach to your account?"
	As it turned out, my brother's account was set up using just his initials. I could have used my American ID.
* * * * *
	Logging in to a VR environment feels like a floaty detached runner's high followed by a gradual re-engagement of the senses over the course of a minute. In a game like Haven, everything feels vibrant and new, unless of course it was programmed to the contrary. Vision is magnified as in a dream. In fact the technology that makes holistic immersion VR possible engages many of the same parts of the brain that are active during lucid dreaming.
	The first time I signed in after my call to customer service, I received a new prompt.
Please select your account:
PrincessParadox
Spur27
	I'd never had multiple accounts before, and it seemed that each time I signed in I'd now be prompted to choose between my own set of characters, and my brother's. I tapped Spur27. A character selection screen appeared featuring half a dozen game characters, listed in order of experience points earned on each. With more than double the experience of all the other characters, only one seemed to have been played in earnest.
	I rolled my eyes at his name choice. "Stabbies." What kind of name is that? Good eve' Lord Stabbies? My inner role player cringed at names that didn't sound like they belonged to a real person. Did an in game mother one day reach into a cradle and coo at baby Stabbies? Of course not, my bro wasn't the sort to think too hard into back story. Even during my raiding years, with the exception of one dwarf paladin style character named Scootiwutams, I always tried to give my characters names that sounded like they'd fit the world.
	I didn't mind teasing him because, in that moment It felt like he was still alive, looming over my shoulder as I performed an evaluation of his never before accessed account. The mental chiding of his game name felt okay because, he was there with me in a way that he hadn't been since he was alive. It was nice.
Welcome to Haven Online!
Your last login was 786 Days Ago.
You have New Mail!
	I quickly dismissed the login status messages as had become my habit over the years. My first reaction to signing in to my brother's account was "Dude...I'm a dude." Not the most sentimental of thoughts, but I wasn't prepared to be standing a head or two taller than my normal height, and with big bulky muscles. I touched my face, to discover man-fur about my chin and lips.
	"That's fucked up." I said, and scratched my face, my brother's game face, daring it to fall off.
	I pulled up the character stats window to see what I'd inherited.
Name: 	                                                    Stabbies
Race: 	                                                       Human
Attributes
Physical
Strength 10, Dexterity 19, Stamina 10
Social
Charisma 3, Manipulation 3, Appearance 3
Mental
Perception 2, Intellect 2, Wits 7
Skills
Combat
Daggers 12, Swords 13, Clubs 4, Unarmed 7,... etc.
	The list went on. I wish I could say I was surprised by Jordan building a combat monkey, but I already knew going in that, unlike me at his age, he was more interested in combat than role playing. I didn't pay too close attention because I didn't plan to play his character. It was one thing to login and let everyone know what'd happened, the idea of wandering around for extended periods of time in his game body, made my real world skin crawl. It'd be like wearing a dead person's skin.
	I found the "Guild" tab beside the character information screen and opened it.
Guild:		Toxic Muffins
Members:	1
	My stomach sank. The number one made me feel like crying. I probably was crying in the real world, but another hope occurred to me
his friends list. I pushed away the guild interface, and checked who was currently online. They were all my friends. Apparently by bringing his account under the umbrella of my email, all his contacts disappeared.
	I said, "Fuck..." and I scrolled through my list, hoping to discover names I didn't recognize, mixed in with my familiar buddies as if our friends lists had been merged. "No... No... No..." Either Jordan hadn't maintained a friends list, or his contacts were gone.
	The realization sucked all the joy out of my stomach that teasing my brother about his name had imparted, instead it left an empty pit that felt a lot like doom.
	I was too late. From the mom with the potty mouth, to the home schooled kid that spanked the monkey too much, every single one of his friends was gone. The sense of loss eclipsed even the mirth inspired by his stupid guild name and the giant cupcake adorning the guild tabard produced. "You can play on my account if you want." he used to say, I never did. "But don't you ever leave my guild. I'm a Toxic Muffin. They'll always be my crew."
	How long was always? When did the first member leave after their leader stop showing up? Who was the last to hold out until Jordan came back? All these people, names and faces, none of which I knew and yet desperately wanted to contact so that at least someone, anyone from Jordan's gaming life would know that he'd died, and that he hadn't forgotten about them.
	"This fucking sucks." I said, and explored other parts of his interface. There was a guild announcements tab which made me laugh and feel sad at the same time. "R-Twined is a PvP Dick!" I pressed the red button next to it, a drum sounded through the game world, and the red text super imposed itself on my vision.
R-Twined is a PvP Dick!
	Real mature Jordan. Real mature. At least then I knew how leaders pushed alerts to their guilds. I'd never been one. I pushed the button for another alert.
Stop killing kittens and get with the raid!
	I had a feeling that was meant for the masturbation boy I'd heard so much about. A long time ago Jordan posted a meme about a kitten dying every time someone masturbated. I had to wonder what the Toxic Muffins guild chat was like on a daily basis.
R-Twined is a PvP Dick!
	Real mature, Jordan. Real mature.
	The thing about going through this account was that it wasn't an idealized version of my brother that we heard about at the funeral, and during memorial toasts. It was like his ghost was reaching out through time to spend a few moments with me. These alerts, his terrible names, and even his builds were an echo of the real Jordan which helped keep him more alive.
	"Thank you." I said, but I didn't know who to. It might have been to God for giving me this experience, Jordan for leaving his account behind, or myself for thinking of a way to get around Haven Online's rules to gain access. Whatever the case, I was grateful for this electronic shrine to my real brother.
	After clearing the guild and character interface windows from my vision I saw two buff icons in the top right corner of my view, and examined them.
Enhanced Dexterity.
Dexterity increased by 10%.
Duration: 43 minutes remaining.
Enhanced Vitality.
Regeneration Increased by 10%.
Duration: 42 minutes remaining.
	Viewing them was like an out of body experience as it dawned on me that, like the guild announcements, these were buffs that were cast by Jordan's own hand, more than two years ago, before he died. The count down timers paused each time a player signed off. They'd been preserved all this time.
	"Holy shit," I said, and thought about how they'd got there. Had he just returned from a raid, or was he the sort of player that kept himself continually buffed, even in safe areas? I didn't know what to say or think except that I didn't want the count down to expire.
	For the first time I took in my surroundings. Magic brooms danced on the sidewalks and swept through the streets, I was standing outside the bank beside a purple crystal protruding from the sidewalk like a fire hydrant with a holographic envelope sparkling overhead. I was in Durolan, the city of Mages, apparently my brother last checked his in-game mail before signing out.
	I accessed the mail interface by putting my hand on the crystal. Jordan wasn't likely to have pending emails from friends because player to player messages were automatically deleted after 90 days, unless the message was manually saved as an inventory item, and no one did that. Haven Online Official messages remained in player inboxes until they'd been read.
New Mail
Haven Online - Happy 8th Anniversary!
Haven Online - Happy 7th Anniversary!
Haven Online - The Olympics Are Here!
	In spite of being fully aware of Haven Online's player to player mail deletion policy, I was still disappointed to see none of Jordan's contacts had sent him a message. A lead would have been nice. The bit about the Olympics stirred me, because Jordan died weeks before the most recent Korean Winter Olympics.
	In spite of Haven Online being a fantasy game, it, and Freeworld Online mirrored real cultural events by providing special quests and items surrounding fantasy equivalents to real world holidays. So far as The Olympics were concerned, players and NPCs from all over Haven gathered to compete in non-lethal athletic competitions.
	In the real world, Jordan was going to come visit me in Seoul so we could watch figure skating, downhill skiing, and aerial's together. In the end, I just saw the skaters. Watching the ski competitions, which Jordan was more excited about, would have just pounded the loss into wounds that were still too new. I returned the tickets to my dad, who had bought them for us, and he took my non-gaming sister with whom he bonded with on the mountain like he had with Jordan. Skiing was their thing. I liked it but, they were far more into it than I was. So far as winter sports were concerned, I was the skater of the family. I opened my mail, starting with the oldest.
"The Olympics are here! Thank you for being a loyal customer. As a Limited Edition user since Haven Online's original release, we have provided you with the following items. Frost Dragon Whelp, Frost Dragon Whelp (Limited Edition). Ain't he cute?"
	I'd forgotten Jordan was a LE user. I was totally jealous when our parents got him the more expensive LE copy, and me the standard version for Christmas. I guess they thought either I was too old for video games, or girls wouldn't appreciate it as much. At the time, the difference in price was about a hundred and fifty bucks - Haven Online was a new game and no one knew if it'd take off, but now factory sealed limited editions sold for thousands at auction. It didn't give your characters any extra personal combat abilities, but the LE distinction did offer exclusive bonus pets which were impossible to come by any other way. These gifts were mostly cosmetic, but the pets also tended to be a bit stronger than normal in pet battles
one of the less popular features of the game. Additionally LE items such as mounts or auras required no extra proficiencies to use.
	I heard a ka-ching sound followed by the following prompts.
You have acquired Frost Dragon Whelp .
You have acquired Frost Dragon Whelp (Limited Edition).
	I opened the second in game mail.
"Happy 7th Anniversary! Thank you for being a loyal customer. As a Limited Edition user since Haven Online's original release, we have provided you with the following items. Sametora Hatchling. Sametora Hatchling (Limited Edition). Careful, they bite!"
The ka-ching sounded again followed by two new prompts.
You have acquired Sametora Hatchling .
You have acquired Sametora Hatchling (Limited Edition).
	The third email read much the same as the first two, but instead of a creature, I received two versions of an in-game aura which would cause waves of color to drift around me if invoked. The standard version was a hypnotic blue color, whereas the Limited Edition was silver and white and provided a buff.
Serenity.
Regeneration increased by 10% while in safe zones.
	Ten percent wasn't much in a place where you weren't going to be attacked anyway, but it was something. The real appeal was that it looked cool. I was tempted to check out the pets, but the time was running down on Jordan's buffs and I didn't want to waste it.
	I sighed and looked down the street passed the animated brooms, and wondered what to do and where to go. I opened the game map, and discovered a pond several blocks away.
	The walk was peaceful, and I admired the fantasy architecture. While the placement of buildings was based on real world GPS maps, they'd all been reskinned to reflect high fantasy themes. Most buildings were stone, and tall mage towers stood in place of real world skyscrapers. Occasionally, I saw log cabin inns in place of local pubs. I forgot which real world city Durolan was mapped on, but if I'd lived there I may have been able to find a recreation of my condo in the fantasy environment. Perhaps not.
	For now large chunks of the real world map were missing, as distant streets in the real world connected to each other at unreal places. For example, one step beyond the real world equivalent to the intersection of 1st and Tomby Ave could land a player at 42nd and Tomby when they got to the other side. As the player base increased, so did the game map as more information from GPS maps was imported, reskinned and devoted to either cities, towns, or monster territory. Five years later, crossing the same intersection of 1st and Tomby, could take you to 23rd and Tomby as the game map continually expanded like the universe. All roads were renamed for the game, so there were never any gaps in street numbers.
	As I approached the pond, the lack of other player characters struck me. The last time I was here on my own account, the expansion where Durolan was new had been less than a year old. Havenfolk, the name given to NPCs, milled about, but didn't intrude. I could see them inside their shops polishing swords, baking goods, and milling about the environment. The last time I'd been there, adventurers outnumbered Havenfolk by a ratio of ten to one. Now, my footsteps echoed against uncaring buildings.
	I resigned myself to that I may not see another PC when a half-orc lumbered around the corner with a blue player icon over his nameplate.
Biskon
Type:                        Player
Race:                     Half-Orc
Appearance:         2
Challenge:     Very Difficult
	Appearance was a subjective trait, and mostly influenced how Havenfolk treated you. The majority of adventurers just ignored it in favor of how they personally felt about the looks of each other's avatars. In some cases a power required you to roll your appearance rating plus one of your abilities. As a general rule, an appearance of one was supposed to be ugly, two average, three pleasant and so forth. Biskon's avatar was no exception to the rule.
	Our eyes met and my muscles tensed. My mind associated orcs with savage brawls, and pointless combat since that's the kind of activity the people I knew usually engaged in while playing them. It'd be just my luck to run into a player killer on Stabbies's first login. My hand went for a sword that wasn't there. Not my character. Not my inventory. I didn't know where anything was. I was really hoping this wouldn't turn into a PvP encounter. I just wanted to sit by the water, have a few nice thoughts, and log off. Signing in and being ganked within minutes of resurrecting my brother's account would put all kinds of sour in my disposition.
	Then I remembered Haven Online's city guardians, and realized I was unlikely to be attacked. As a safe zone, magical guardians would materialize the instant one of us took an offensive action, and the aggressor would be sent for respawn with a few nasty debuffs, including a flag that allowed other players to kill them without penalty.
	Instead, the orc nodded, but said nothing.
	I didn't know what he wanted, but hoped this wasn't a preamble to awkward flirting. I nervously scratched my face, touched the facial hair and felt an idiot for forgetting how everyone saw me. No, orcy Biskon probably wasn't making eyes at this bearded human.
	We walked side by side, for the last block leading up to the pond. In the way of games, a fishing pole and tackle box appeared in his hands.
	"Have a good one." he said, before sitting on a rock. "I'm training."
	"Yeah..." I said, not having expected it. "Good luck."
	He gave me a slow baritone "Thanks," as he cast his line into the water.
	I moved on to the end of a dock, and could see small faerie creatures dancing hand in hand on top of the pond like water spiders. I thought they were cute, up until a bass broke the surface of the water and ate one. Not too smart I guess.
	I took off my boots and let my feet hang over the water. I raised an imaginary glass and said "This one's for you," to my kid brother. I'd already wasted another five minutes of buff time, and didn't want to use it all in one go.
	I clicked the logout button, and found myself briefly groggy in my real world body. A tradition was born.
	Once a year I logged in to collect Haven Online emails, and sit by the lake, watching a few more minutes of Jordan's buffs run out.
Chapter 3 - One Last Time
	Today's the day. The last time I visit his virtual shrine before the merge. My mind wanders, because it's better than dwelling on what I've got to do when I get home. It's time to pay my kid brother a visit, eight years after his death.
	Normally I'd be making this trip into VR a few weeks from now, but with the much talked about Freeworld Online and Haven Online merger this may be the last time I can access his account with all his in-game ephemera. Maybe nothing will change and I'll still be able to sign in for minutes at a time to watch his buffs drain away into the digital abyss, more likely they'll be stripped away as soon as I login.
	Who ever heard of two games this size combining into one? They both use a d10 system, so it's technically possible but magical post apocalyptic, and high fantasy aren't compatible in my mind. Decades ago this would have been a nightmare for game developers, but with intuitive programming, all you need are bots that understand the languages of both platforms and the conversion is possible. Since they both use GPS mapping as the foundation of their cityscapes, I wonder how they will resolve instances where the same map is used in both games. Downtown Seattle, for example, has both Freeworld and Haven counterparts. I'm sure by this time tomorrow I'll know.
	I get off at my stop and check the mail before heading to my room. The box is empty, no surprise since I keep all my bills electronic. My heart beats a little faster, and the sensation of the elevator button under my finger tips feels a little more real today than all others. I'm not hungry, but I grab myself a bowl of cereal, because I'm a rebel like that
eating breakfast in the evening, putting off what I've got to do.
	I've got a giant bean bag chair that feels like sitting in a cloud that I settle into with my visor. I feel a little light headed, and select Spur27 when I login.
	This is going to be painful. It's time to face the muzak.
* * * * *
	I'm sitting on the dock staring down at the water with the fairy creatures skating about the surface. I check my buffs.
Enhanced Dexterity.
Dexterity increased by 10%.
Duration: 16 minutes remaining.
Enhanced Vitality.
Regeneration Increased by 10%.
Duration: 15 minutes remaining.
	I've been in and out of this place for thirty minutes of game time over the course of six years. My ritual has evolved over time to make the experience quicker and more sentimental.
	A couple of elves fish at the end of the dock where I appear, one man one woman, moon blue skinned and wearing rugged hats with colorful feathered lures sticking off the surface at odd angles. It always feels weird when you pop into existence right on top of someone, even if the game adjusts your position so you don't appear inside a physical object.
	"Err... Sorry," I say not knowing if I've interrupted. By now I'm used to my brother's avatar, so at least I'm not freaking out over being a guy.
	The man says, "No problem!" in a tone that's too bright for my gloom. The woman nods, though I get the feeling that they were talking, since they're both PCs. I step over their tackle boxes, and as a matter of gaming habit I inspect them before proceeding.
Elondriel
Type:                        Player
Race:              Elf (Twilight)
Appearance:            4
Challenge:           Average
LadyPoints
Type:                       Player
Race:              Elf (Twilight)
Appearance:           5
Challenge:      Challenging
	The average and challenging ratings indicate that they've invested about the same amount of experience as my brother into combat related stats. The 'Twilight' subcategory of elf indicates their sub-race gains bonuses to dexterity, stealth, and crepuscular activities.

Spirit Bound
	It's a pretty translucent thing the size of a beach ball. It bobs up and down with each beat of its maple leaf shaped wings in butterfly patterns of faded yellow, sea green, and black. I reach out my hand and it bumps its boxy dragon snout and short serpentine body against my skin like a cat in need of affection. Its insectile antennae tickle my arm as it retreats, then it thuds its whole body against my shoulder.
	My insides war with the desire to sulk and comfort the small creature. He's been gone almost a decade. The only thing that's different now is the thought that I may not be able to watch his buffs disappear or look at his guild alerts after this evening. The buffs were bound to run out sooner or later, even without the merge, and of course, I can always recreate them after Freeworld and Haven combine, but it wouldn't be the same because I'd know it was my virtual hands that typed the new announcements and not his. The Flutterdrake dives, and thumps against my shoulder.
	I say, "Persistent aren't you?" as the little ghostly creature continues its efforts to snag my attention. I awkwardly scratch beneath its chin, an odd sensation considering that there's pressure but no texture. It releases a puff of cool pink mist which I interpret as a sign of satisfaction.

Enhanced Dexterity.
Dexterity increased by 10%.

	I've already wasted three minutes on this visit, but an apathetic part of me doesn't care. I could sit here all night, or more accurately the next thirteen minutes and... what? I don't want to give up, but at the same time I realize a sense of release that may come of not being burdened by that timer. Does that make me a bad person? I don't know. I want to let go and move on, but how can I just throw away one of the few things I have left that he created.
	"It was going to happen anyway." I tell myself, and I'm reminded that come tomorrow there may be no buff timer at all.
	Without thinking I stroke the Flutterdrake's head. It nestles into my chest. It wraps its wings around its body like a bat, and I carry it in the crook of my arm like a baby. It has no weight, but I feel its ghostly body pushing against my clothing. I put my hand on the mail interface, and open the anniversary message.
Would you like to save this document to your inventory? Unsaved documents will be automatically deleted after 90 days.

I thought losing them would feel like doom, but instead the sky shines a little brighter, and I'm filled with a powerful sense of release.
Chapter 4 - New Ride
	I expected to feel sad after last night, but instead I feel as if all things in my gaming world are filled with life and new possibility. This morning I'd put in a request for a week and a half vacation so I can spend time in Freehaven Online, the name given to Haven and Freeworld after the merge. My boss approved it on the condition that I finish the first draft of the once man and the new Freeworld motorcycle mounts before I go.
	I finished off the tail animations for the once man project in the AM and shot it off to my supervisor for review. Now I'm going over the bikes in what should be the final edit. They're a beautiful mix of magic and technology - motorcycles in the style of 1960's Harleys, but instead of gas tanks have containers like fishbowls with spirits trapped inside to power the bikes. They're called Elechoppers.
	I've made five skins, one for each of the traditional elements
 Fire, Water, Earth, Wind, Ether. Right now, a lithe fiery creature like a molten monkey with pointy elf ears and crocodile teeth gives me a toothy grin, and then the middle finger. It disperses in a swirl of red, orange and yellow within its little container, the bike purrs, and coughs up a gout of flame from the exhaust.
	"Sal, Set content rating to PG, and repeat." Freeworld Online, is built with a rating system that allows end users to define the content of their environment akin to movie ratings. For the most part, this determines how much gore a person experiences during combat, but also alters the language and behavior of NPCs. New settings applied, the spirit within the bike gives me the same toothy grin, blows a raspberry, and poofs into a firestorm.
	"Looks good." Sal says.
	"I agree."
	We've fixed the issue with the spirits' auras bleeding outside the tank during the animation. A small fix, but necessary. The mechanics department already assigned stats to the Elechoppers, which means it will likely be included in the merger patch this evening. I have no idea if they'll be drops or crafted items, but I'm leaning towards crafted. Sometimes they mix it up so one rare skin drops off a boss, and the others can be made by players in game. Either way, I have no say.
	I say, "Then send these bad boys off to QC so we can get the hell outta here!"
	New expansions are big news for any game, and I haven't been this keen to play in years. It's this and taking a cue from Elondriel and LadyPoints, to make some use of Jordan's account that has me watching the clock until the merge goes live. It's like being a kid with the clock moving way too slow. The dex and vitality buffs are gone, so there's no longer need to limit my logins to a few minutes at a time.
* * * * *
	I'm not interested in playing before the patch, I'll just be forced to logoff and download the client side updates. There's time to kill so, I treat myself to my favorite Chicago style deep dish pizza after work. It's rare for me to eat out these days, but I feel excited and want to celebrate the optimism.
	Once I'm home and login's available, I settle into my beanbag and boot up the visor. I get that runner's high feeling, as a new login screen fills my vision and my senses adapt to the digital environment.
Welcome to Freehaven Online!
Please specify your character name.
	I'm looking around for the character selection screen, but can't find it. Normally I'd either be prompted to choose which character I want to login as on Freeworld Online, or which license I wanted to access for Haven Online. In this case it's treating me like I am a new player with no characters at all.
	I select the question mark beside the prompt for information.
All characters in Freeworld Online and Haven Online have been combined into one, and experience is now applied at the player level. Your new character will have attributes equal to the highest value purchasable based on the number of points invested on all characters. Any remaining points will be credited to your next purchase of each attribute. For example, if you have one character with 20 strength (540 points spent), and another 14 strength (255 points spent), your new character will have 23 strength (requires 729 points), with only 3 points needed to advance to level 24 (requires 798 points). All skill experience will be similarly pooled, and all previous non-racial abilities on all characters will be accessible to your character.
Freeworld para-human abilities are pooled within characters of the same families (weres, vampires, fey, etc.), however powers and abilities restricted to these para-human races are limited to aliases that have completed the requisite quests, and/or creation rites. For more information, see the Alias menu.
Your previous character names and appearances can be accessed via the Alias menu. Existing users will be awarded one perfect alias per previous character on Haven Online or Freeworld Online, however new aliases may only be acquired in game. All items will be consolidated into a single inventory.
	"That's.... scary." I say thinking of the implications. If I understand the announcement right, we can basically access any of the skills or abilities we've attained on any character at any time, and the cosmetic appearance of each character can be accessed via an in-game toggle. I guess it saves the time of having to log out each time you want to change your role between Tank, Healer, and DPS.
	"We'll see how it goes." I say to myself.
	On one hand this means my character will suddenly become insanely powerful compared to before the merge, on the other hand, it sounds like the same could be said of everyone else, since each player's stats are now pooled. I clear the announcement
	I wasn't expecting to have to name a new character upon login, and this is delaying me. I don't want to pick an existing character name because I'm pretty sure we'll still get to use them via the new alias system. I'm into folk lore and mythology so I think of less common goddesses. Hera? Eh... Goddess of fidelity... not my style. Melpomene? Muse of tragedy. I like the sound of the word, but it seems a bit dramatic. Nyx? Goddess of darkness.
	I also like the sound of that one. I enter. N-y-x. Of course it's already taken. I change the spelling to N-y-x-x, but it too has been used. N-y-x-x-x? I shake my head before even trying. With a name like that I'll be bombarded with Soc requests for cybersex every time I log in.
	I go back to the name Melpomene... I guess the entire game will be a fusion of Jordan and my own game's accounts now. Dying before you're twenty is tragic. I enter it, the name's taken. I try again, this time changing the spelling to M-e-l-p-o-m-i-n-e. The system accepts it.
Do you want to set your character name to Melpomine? You may rename your character at a later time for one tenth of your in-game currency or 500 gold (whichever is greater).
Yes / No
	With one character, it's good to know that players won't be stuck with the name they chose later on. I choose yes.

Please choose a race for Melpomine.
	I browse through the list, and I see everything from humans, to gnomes, to dwarves, and elves, with sub-races for each type. There are even orcs. Humans gain reputation and skills faster, but don't get any bonus attributes. This is useful when it comes to unlocking faction specific gear, special quests, and grinding skills. Elves, the sexy race, get bonuses to dexterity and appearance at the cost of stamina, and can see in the dark. Dwarves, one of the least commonly played races for women, are rugged, and so on. There are also half races for each primary race since apparently humans can breed with anything under the sun. The Freeworld Online races, are notably absent. It's probably because I picked Haven as my starting location.
	I'm sorely tempted to play a half gnome because I've never seen them in any other game, but the gnomish racial traits don't mesh with my intended play style.
	"This sucks." I say because it feels like no matter what I choose I'll be trapped playing one race for the duration of my account. In the end I go with a half-elf which gives me fifty percent of the human and elven racial bonuses and penalties.
Do you want to make Melpomine a Half-Elf? You will not be able to change your race unless you enter rebirth. Rebirth requires a quest.

	I spend more time than I'd like to admit tweaking the appearance of my new character in the creation interface, and a few hours later I manifest in game as a lithe half-elf woman with strong lean muscles, and hair pulled into a long blonde ponytail.
	I've never seen so many players in one location. Hundreds of people, wearing the same straw colored starter linen shirt and pants crowd together. Their individual voices bleed into a cacophony of speech that's hard to distinguish save for that coming from people standing right in front of me.

The horde of avatars is worse than when Freehaven Online released an expansion with its first new playable race that everyone wanted to try. With everyone making new characters, it seems that anyone with even a drop of human blood is appearing at the same spawn point. A lot of players stand like idiots checking their stats and gear creating a massive traffic jam.
	Fortunately, it's not so hard to escape since it automatically deposited me at the edge of the crowd, but it won't be the case for long if I dawdle. Already six new players have manifested within arm's reach and I've only been in-game a few seconds.
	I have no shame as I sprint the opposite direction toward the NPC marketplace. In the real world I'd look like a maniac running through the equivalent of a shopping mall, full speed ahead, but in an overcrowded virtual world, you've gotta let stuff slide. I'm about to plow right over a mousey ginger human in starting gear, when I instinctively plant a toe, spin, and bolt around her like she's not even there. Then a gnome pushing a cart full of clockwork doodads, and what-cha-ma-call-its crosses my path and I leap over him like an Olympic athlete in half lunar gravity. I don't get a good look at his goods because I'm moving so fast. I glance back, over my shoulder to make sure I didn't topple his mobile shop. The little dude's curly grey moustache gives him the air of an elderly Super Mario Brother. God bless the NPCs for not batting an eye. He carries on as if nothing happened. It's like I said, until I get settled in, I have no shame.
	I think, "This place is going to be hell for the next few days." as I imagine the human capitol, Athelon, flooded with players signing into their accounts. The thought of checking out Havenfolk wears to replace these useless starter clothes crosses my mind, but I think better of it knowing there's a sea of players behind me that might have similar thoughts. I get the feeling that all the inns around here are going to be just as crowded so I keep running, past the signs with shields, hammers, quills, and swords denoting the types of wears each merchant sells, and past the inns with their mead, stews and ale.
	I'm approaching the wall denoting the edge of this district when a guard calls "Halt!"

	"Looks to me like you've got a bag right there. Why don't we take a look?"
	I pull up my interface, illusory windows appear in the air around me. I select the option for "tablet," which allows you to view your stats via a handheld device called a VT (Virtual Tablet) so as not to block your view of the world. The computer frames overlaying my vision vanish as soon as the device appears in my palm. NPCs have been programmed not to notice. They even pause their dialogue while you're using it. Part of me wonders what'd happen if I transformed into a naked character.

	Now that I've been reminded of stats, I decide to check my attributes.
Name: 	                                                Melpomine
Race: 	                                                Half-Elf
Attributes
Physical
Strength 12, Dexterity 24 (+1), Stamina 20
Social
Charisma 12, Manipulation 9, Appearance 7(+1)
Mental
Perception 12 (+1), Intelligence 19, Wits 16
	The basic combat system in Freehaven Online is as follows: The game engine rolls a number of ten sided dice equal to dexterity + the relevant ability score per attack. Each dice that rolls 6 or above earns a point. Each point after the first is added to strength in another roll of d10s to determine how much damage is inflicted on the target. The defender then rolls their stamina plus any relevant abilities to resist taking the damage. Any points remaining after the resist roll, are subtracted from the character's health (usually 10). A character with zero health is killed, and has to respawn with penalties that last 15 minutes.
	"Good God!" I exclaim, though none of these stats are surprising since I played most of the characters that contributed to them. It's just alarming to see them all in one place.
	The only stats with significant gains due to my brother's account are dexterity, strength, and wits, since he preferred playing the sneaky stabby characters that relies on quick strikes and light feet. Strength added to his damage, and wits allowed him to act faster in combat. Marine's dex only went up to 16, because I also needed to invest points into intelligence to work her magic. In my time since the release of Haven and Freeworld, I've played musicians, casters with high intelligence, a plate wearing tank, and a nimble martial artist and it shows in my diverse stats. I wonder what other people's spreads look like.
	Part of me expects players to scream about the unfairness of some characters having high physical, mental and social stats, but on the other hand if those players actually cared about non-physical stats, they'd likely have already made a character that used them in the past. Those that only ever play characters of a certain type still have higher stats for their style of game play than everyone else.
	I'm starting to get the logic behind the merger. The more real world time you spend engaged in a certain type of game play, the higher those stats will be. Unless of course you're like me where you inherited a family member's account with years of accumulated game time that you didn't actually perform. I decide to keep that part a secret for the time being.
	I open up my inventory and discover a mindboggling collection of items. Thankfully via the virtual tablet, they can be sorted by type, stats, or searched for by name. Jordan's characters didn't wear plate, so Scooti's tank gear is the best available. He did, however, have a nice collection of swords.

New Mail
Freeworld Dev Team - Elechopper
	I open the letter, but there's no text. This is an employee thing. One of the perks of being a designer is that you get a special developer's copy of any vanity items you design. Weapons are a little different. You get a replica item that allows you to copy stats from any of your other items of the same type, thus allowing you to utilize their appearance, but not gain an unfair combat advantage over paying customers.
	I'm more than happy to receive my Earth, Fire, Wind, Water, and Ether motorcycles, the developer's edition of mounts clone movement speed of any of your other mounts. It means the Elechoppers were included in the update. I wonder how they were implemented Freeworld side, and how long it'll take to see players cruising around on them.
	I open up the mount interface, and it's filled with 123 standard mounts, and 38 developer's editions. I sort by those that originated in Haven Online, and then by movement speed. I tap to summon Jordan's best mount.
Nightmare
Type: 			Mount
Race: 		Demon
Status: 		Friendly
Challenge: 		N/A
Ground Speed: 	250%
Air Speed:		670%
	It's a burning horse with eyes like hot coals, and a mane and tail of red and yellow fire. It feels like standing next to a furnace. Smaller red and black tongues of flame continually leap up and scurry over its demonic flesh. I don't see any wings, but the air speed stat implies it can fly.
	I say, "Nice drop little bro," and give him a salute. I remember him gushing over the creature at Thanksgiving.
	I tap the icon for the fire bike on my tablet and then "clone."
Would you like to apply movement speed from Nightmare, to Elechopper (DE).
Yes / No
I tap yes.
Fire Elechopper (DE)
Type: 			Mount
Race: 		N/A
Status: 		Friendly
Challenge: 		N/A
Ground Speed: 	250%
Air Speed:		670%
Spirit Bound
	I have my doubts as to whether or not the Elechoppers are flying mounts in the rest of the game but developer's editions don't have that limitation since they just copy the properties of other objects. I repeat the process for the other five bikes.
	I open the macro interface and my heart sinks to discover that it's empty. No more reminders that R-Twined is a PvP dick. I sigh, but it's to be expected. I tap to create a new macro and, using the virtual tablet, type in the command "/run summon 'Fire Elechopper'" and tap record.
	I bellow the word "INFERNO!!!" and wave my middle finger in the air in imitation of the burning monkey in the gas tank. I type "End," on the tablet. The Havenfolk NPCs ignore me, but some of the players look at me like I've got two heads.
Do you want to save changes to macro "Elebike - Fire?"
Yes / No
I choose yes.
	Now for a test run. I shout out "INFERNO!!!" and give the world the bird. A soundless red nova of magical energy the size of a small car pulses, followed by the growl of a magical engine. My bike pops into existence on the side of the road. I can't help but notice how incongruous it looks in the high fantasy environment. The fire monkey in the gas tank cackles.
	I straddle the seat, and rev up the engine, pushing a lance of fire behind me. My heart flutters and I smile. Hell yes, I made this.
	I start to roll forward and pull back on the handlebars like I'm flying a plane. My stomach jumps and the bike lifts up off the ground hovering in mid air. In-game mounts generally respond more to your intent than physical action, but the sensation of pulling to cause the lift makes the experience that much more real. I give it some gas and go straight up Harley Chitty Bang Bang on this medieval town.
Chapter 5 - There Be Dragons

	A few PCs far below point and shout "Dude!" having never seen an Elechopper, much less a flying one. In all likelihood, I'm the first person to use one in the game. I feel a flush of satisfaction knowing that all those hours in virtual design space haven't gone to nothing. There are a few odd mechanical Freeworld mounts already in the game, but odds are they'll be a rarity in Haven until people start crossing over. The only people that'll have them today are those that had accounts in both games that chose Haven as their new Freeworld starting point.
	The novelty of having a flying bike wears off after a few laps around the city. Going airborne has its perks, but as a means of transportation, I think I prefer the terrestrial Freeworld Online version of the bike from my design space where I can feel the terrain beneath my wheels. Plowing through air makes my stomach lurch and jump every which way with the wind in my face and moving up, down and through improbable loops, but there's something to be said for riding on an open road.
	I can still do that, but I'm capped at 250% of running speed. As a general rule Haven Online ground mounts are a lot slower than those found in Freeworld, and I'd copied the properties of a Haven item. Freeworld on the other hand has far fewer flying mounts which are generally slow in comparison to Haven. The ground mounts, like non-DE versions of the Elechoppers are much faster on flat earth. It'll be interesting to see what happens when the worlds start colliding.
	I get off the bike just outside the South Gate and decide to make a macro for dismissing it so I don't have to go through the manual interface each time I want it to disappear.
	I open up the screen on my virtual tablet, and enter the command "/run dismiss mount -lastactive." This means that when I perform the required action, the last mount I interacted with will disappear into my inventory. This will prevent me from dismissing all mounts I've summoned if for some reason I decide to have more than one out. I tap "record," and say "Bloop bloop!" while miming setting a car alarm with a keychain.
Do you want to save changes to macro "Dismiss Mount?"
Yes / No
	I tap yes on the virtual tablet. Now for the test.
	I say "Bloop bloop!" and make the appropriate motion. The Elechopper fades from the world like a forgotten ghost.
	I shout "INFERNO!!!" and wave my middle finger, red flashes. I hear the roar of the engine. It reappears a few paces over.
	I say, "Very nice," and trail my fingers lovingly over the seat and rear fender. If you haven't noticed, I like a lot of customization in my games. It comes with the territory of being a developer. While most people set some basic macros for common things like summoning their mounted transportation, it's entirely possible to create custom triggers for spells and the like, but a lot of people don't bother.
	I should contact TakoStar before he finds another party, I don't know a lot of people in Haven and I think most of my friends are going to start in Freeworld.
Melpomine: Hey. I'm done going through my stuff. You up for a dungeon?
TakoStar:	Sure. Where u at?
Melpomine: 	South Gate.
TakoStar:	You got heals or DPS?
Melpomine:	I can DPS, but I want to get back into tanking.
TakoStar:	I mean other people.
Melpomine: 	Oh, no just me.
TakoStar:	Alright I'll advertise. I'll meet you there when I find people.
Melpomine: 	I'll just grind some mobs till you're ready. Lemme know and I'll go back to the gate.
TakoStar:	Use the Dwarf alias. Scooti or whatever.
Melpomine:	Scootiwutams
TakoStar:	Yeah her. Dwarf girls rock.
	I shrug and think, "Why not?" before I send my reply of "OK." It's time to get back in the groove. I pull up the Alias menu on my VT, select Scooti, and transform in a puff of magic. Instead of my old Commander's Longsword, I now have Jordan's Exsanguinator in a sheath at my hip.
	I get on the bike and rev the engine. It's more comfortable than you'd think in full plate. It probably wouldn't be true in the real world, but hey, this is game logic. I'm digging my multi-genre attire
a red fire bike, with red and gold flecked plate, topped off with a blood drinking longsword. The wind on my face is earthy and refreshing carrying the scent of trees, and clean air. I'm sure it must exist somewhere, but as a city girl burning down a fantasy road in my fire elemental powered chopper, I wonder if air this good still exists in the real world.
	Up ahead I see a signboard and pull over. "WANTED: Ridgewood Bandits. Reward - 50 silver per insignia. Collect with Athelon Guard." A take a copy of the notice and I'm given a quest.
The Athelon Guard are offering a bounty on Ridgewood Bandits. Defeat them and bring their insignias to the Athelon Guard for a reward.
Rewards:
20 Silver per insignia.
Reputation with Athelon Guard
Do you accept? Yes / No
	I accept the quest, and a red area to the west is automatically highlighted on my game map, indicating where to go to fulfill the quest objective. Before charging off to hunt bandits, I notice an exclamation point over the guild tab.
Your Aliases Scootiwutams, Melpomine are not members of any guilds. Do you wish to add these aliases to the Toxic Muffins?
Yes / No
	I feel bad because I hadn't even thought about Jordan's guild until now, but now that I'm in-game it seems like I should do something with it. Maybe I can build it back up, or somehow find some of the old members to let them know what happened. I tap yes.
Which Aliases do you wish to add to Toxic Muffins?
I select both Scootiwutams and Melpomine.
Do you wish to claim leadership as Scootiwutams and Melpomine? Note: Claiming leadership will reveal your aliases to all current guild members. Scootiwutams, Melpomine, and Stabbies will be connected.
Yes / No
	I choose yes, and check out the guild roster.
Guild: 	Toxic Muffins
Members:	1
Melpomine (Leader)
	- Aliases: Stabbies, Scootiwutams
	I do an experiment and try adding Marine to the guild as well. This time when prompted to make her a leader I choose no.
Guild: 	Toxic Muffins
Members:	2
Melpomine (Leader)
	Aliases: Stabbies, Scootiwutams
MarineLu
	I leave the roster alone for now, as I see no reason to make all of my former characters leader. The system is interesting because it allows people to masquerade as different players within in-game organizations until such time that their aliases are discovered. I assume the only reason it linked Stabbies to my Scootiwutams and Melpomine identities is because there can only be one official leader, and Stabbies is it.
	After the promotion of my Scootiwutam's avatar a new tab appears in the guild interface, with a bold exclamation point.
Limited Edition - Guild Quest
Dragonsbane - Part 1
Difficulty: Variable
An extra-planer dragon has torn a rip in the wall between two worlds. Investigate the disturbance and bolster your dragon slaying prowess so you can destroy the foul beast and prevent further damage to the land.
Objectives:
Discover the Identity of the extra-planer dragon that caused the rip between worlds. 					0/1
Slay 50,000 dragons or dragon kin.	0/50,000
Rewards:
Title: 			Dragonslayer

Access to Specialized Gear and Enchantments from Havenfolk Merchants
Do you accept? 	Yes / No
	A limited edition quest? Hell yes! I stab the affirmative notification with my finger without even thinking.
	I've never even heard of a Limited Edition quest in either Freeworld or Haven Online. This could be just what I need to build up Jordan's guild again, and the rewards? In-game titles, and 10k reputation in all playable factions is nothing to sneeze at. It's enough to take someone from a starting neutral rep in the capital cities, to third level Honored in one go. It'll get you discounts from merchants, new quests, previously unavailable items for purchase. And what's with that specialized gear? That sounds like access to items that are only obtainable by completing the quest. A limited edition quest.
	I unleash a long whistle, and fire off a message to TakoStar.

Melpomine: 	What?
TakoStar: 	They poop rainbows. Never mind, just ask for me, k? I wanna join.
Melpomine: 	Sure.
	I'm taken aback by his enthusiasm. Am I excited by the quest? Absolutely, but not slit my mother's throat for a chance at participation excited. I guess being a designer with access to all the DE items I've created, and my comparative lack of time in Haven, has insulated me from the insanity caused by Limited Edition items. I like my customizations, but people have been known to spend more than the cost of a car to get their hands on a LE license key. That's just nutty.
	All this talk of LE items reminds me of the flutterdrake I summoned last night. I decide to make a macro to summon him like my bike. Command lines added I whistle four notes, and click to save. He appears in the same burst of color as yester night, and promptly chest bumps against my armor.
	"Still recognize me, don't cha' laddy?" I give him a scratch beneath the chin with my metal gloved fingers. It seems to satisfy him as I'm covered in a frosty pink mist. Pets must be able to see through their owner's aliases. "What do ye say we go hunt some bandits?"
	The flutterdrake does a little mid air spiral, and I take that as a yes. I'm about to head down the road toward the bandit's camp when I realize I'd better give it a name.
	"What do ye want to be called? Cuddlewuzzins?" There's no reaction. "How 'bout Poofter?" he gives me an angry snort, with a cold cloud of grey this time which tells me exactly what he thinks of that suggestion. I thought it was funny because of the way he poofs in and out of existence. "Poofter, nay. Ye be doomed with a name like that." The creature cocks its head inquisitively. I decide to tease him. "DOOMED!"
	The flutterdrake huffs as if to say "OK."
	"Doom? Ye like that?" The drake does another Twirl in air. "Doom ye be then."
Would you like to name your Ethereal Flutterdrake (LE) "Doom?"
Yes / No
	I choose yes.
	"There ye have it. Doom. Ye got a name. Now let's go kill some bandits." Doom exhales a puff of pink mist.
	I enjoy the ride on the western road. Doom's able to keep up just fine, beating his wings, swooping, and diving like a playful kitten... if kittens had wings anyway. He seems to enjoy the exercise. I'm riding through the country with fresh air, bright sun and occasional thatched roof buildings with rustic charm. Which is why I'm caught completely unprepared for the arrow that whizzes about an inch from my eyebrow. I can feel the wind on my face from its passing.
	"Shit!"
	I dive off the Elechopper and scramble to equip my helm which looks like a Bell curve, with holes for eyes, and insectile mandibles covering the mouth. I get the shield of the Sun Lord off my back just in time for a monster thunking sound as I use it to deflect a second arrow.

Mirae

	I'd totally forgotten Doom before she said it, and now... Where is he? "Doom!" I say, and I hear his hiss from the bodies of the fallen bandits. He hovers over Ridgewood Bandit #3 with his ghostly dragon mouth open, and a tendril of smoky soul spiraling into his gullet. "What are you doing?"

	"Earth!" she says without a moment's hesitation. "It's the magic of healing."
	"...and earthquakes." I add with a smirk.

	"Fair 'nuff," I say, "You're really into this healing thing."
	"Yeah... I am." Her voice goes a little quieter.
	"What's up with that little plus by your name anyway? When I inspect ye it says Mirae, Type: Player and there's a little blue plus sign like ye see at the hospitals." As I speak, I access the mount interface on my VT to summon the Earth bike. I ask, "Ye sure ye like earth?"
	Mirae says, "Positive."
	"Here ye go," I say as I tap summon, the developer's edition of the Earthen Elechopper appears, green and brown, with a pouty little earth elemental stuck with an eternal frown stomping around the gas can.
	"Wow... You have two and an LE item? Are you rich or something?"
	I say, "Ye can ride on your own on our way back to Athelon."
	"I can ride?"
	Unlike most games, Freehaven online allows you to lend your mounts to other party members, so long as you stay within close proximity to each other or belong to the same party. When it finally dawns on Mirae that the second bike's hers to ride for a time, her face brightens with childlike joy far beyond what, in my opinion, the situation warrants.
	"Yeah no problem." I say, "Ye just gotta give it back when you're done." Not that the system would allow someone to nick spirit bound items.
	"Sweet." She says. "I've never ridden a motorcycle before."
	"Neither have I." I say with a laugh. "Back home it's public transportation, all day every day. I once tried a moped though, so I guess that's something."
	"I haven't even done that." Her tone sounds with a note of bitterness that clashes with her excitement moments before.
	"So what's up with the plus sign on your profile?"
	"Tell you what, you tell me how you get all this cool stuff, and I'll tell you about the blue cross icon." She says this while straddling the green motorcycle. patting it like it's a riding animal.
	I say, "I dunno." Already, I'm uneasy about the subject, more about Jordan's account than that I'm a dev. Maybe I can just sort of... work around it without explaining the whole circumstance. It's been eight years, and I don't want a pity party.
	"I'll show you mine if you show me yours. It's a fair trade."
	"Alright," I say, and with an awful sigh I didn't mean to produce. "I'll go first."
	"Shoot." She says, as she revs up the engine. We start back to Athelon moving slow enough that we can still have a conversation.
	"The elebikes are easy. I'm a developer. The company gives us free developer's copies of any item we make."
	"What's that mean?" She asks.
	I say, "They don't have any stats like the real game items. We have to copy them from other objects we obtained by playing to keep it fair to customers."
	"Wait so you made these? Like... not crafting, but did the modeling?"
	I say, "Yeah, animations too."
	"You have the coolest job EVER."
	I smirk. No shame here. "I love what I do."
	"So you're an employee? Does that mean you can get all kinds of free in-game stuff?"
	"Nah, not really. I don't have anything to do with accounts, or even the stats on the items I create. They're real big about keeping everything fair, so as not to make players feel cheated."
	"That sucks." Mirae says
	"Not really. Did ye forget? Coolest job EVER." I say trying to mimic her tone, but doing a bad job of it since Scooti's voice is so much lower than Mirae's."
	"Yeah I guess. Did they give you a Limited Edition or something?"
	"Nah." I say, now's the time for subterfuge. "The LE edition was a Christmas gift from my parents before I even worked here. Back when ye could still buy it in stores for a few hundred bucks." I'm being very careful not to include who that gift was for.
	"Nice," She says,. "So we're both Vanilla players."
	"Looks like it. We've both been here from the beginning."
	She asks, "Did you sell all your anniversary items?"
	"Let's just say I've been collecting them."
	"I've always wanted a sametora mount. They're so cool looking."
	My mind reels back to the sametora hatchlings I got years back but never summoned. I play it like I know exactly what she's talking about and don't ask any questions. Instead, I say, "Yeah, they're pretty awesome." Assuming the statement must be true since LE items are so popular. One of these days, I need to go through all those LE items I've been collecting so I don't sound like such a noob when asked about them.
	I then say, "Your turn. You have my answers, now gimme yours."
	"Hmmmm..." She says. "Maybe later." and then guns it kicking up a cloud of dirt as she goes. I hear her delighted shriek down the road, but I can't see her because one of the design features of the Earth bike was to make a magnified cloud of dust upon quick acceleration.
	I try to catch up, with a six foot flame thrower flying out my exhaust but it's no use. So long as we are both on the ground there's no way to close the distance if she's going top speed.
	I pull the handles back and take flight, my movement speed increasing from two hundred percent running speed to six hundred fifty. From there, it's no problem to burn over her like a comet and land, skidding to a stop 100 meters down the road in front of her.
	"No way! They can fly?" Mirae says, laughing, and now she's flying too. Little bits of dirt, and pebbles fall on me from above. I've got to wonder if she'd be so adventurous in the real world. Probably not, welcome to Freehaven Online, where death is only temporary.
	I'm not the only one who had the idea for loop-de-loops, and I catch her pulling the same airborne stunts that I was doing outside the capital.
	She shouts from above, "This thing is way faster than my Pegasus! What did you copy?"
	I want to be mad at her, but her childlike laughter is infectious, and all I can feel is happy that she's having a good time.
	I call back to her, "Nightmare!"
	"Whoa!" She says. It's probably at my announcement, but it could have just as easily have been due to her aerial acrobatics. Riding on the wind is a lot like a roller coaster. Finally she settles down, and finishes her statement. "You got one of those too?"
	"Yeah" I say. "Natural drop. No corporate influence required." I pause. "Actually, it was before I worked for the company." Mirae looks like she's about to take off again, so I've gotta act fast to stop her. "Hold up. I answered your questions. If you take off again I may be tempted to unsummon, and ye can start building up endurance from falling damage."
	That wipes the smile off her face.
	"Sorry." She says. "I don't like talking about it." I'm about to tell her it's okay, even though I really want to know, but she beats me to it by starting her explanation. "When I was eleven I was messing around while snowboarding and fell off the lift."
	"I uhh..." Dude... Didn't see that coming. She waves me silent.
	"I would have been fine if I'd gone down like five minutes earlier, but instead I came down on a boulder and broke my neck. I've been either in bed or in a chair ever since. That's my story." She seems to grasp my confusion so she adds, "That's the incapacitated icon for real life people who can't do shit for themselves. I'm a quadriplegic. Can't even eat or pee without help."
	Her sudden use of the word shit startles me. The harsh word is a dark blotch on her sunny demeanor. I'm about to say sorry, but I get the feeling that she's like me in not wanting the pity, so instead I say, "They make ye wear a tag like that?"
	Mirae says, "It's not like that. Look. I can turn it on and off if I want."
	I inspect her, and sure enough I see the plus sign appear and vanish as she adjusts her settings.
	"So why do you use it?" I ask.
	"I like teaming up with other incaps. I don't get friends in the real world, and here they know what it's like, and what I'm going through."
	"I can't even imagine." I say.
	"Trust me. I hate being the reason your bus is late, or why you've gotta take the next elevator when there's no room with me in my chair."
	I feel like a shit now, because I didn't tell the whole story. It's like being in a bar next to that one guy that tells you his life story, that you blew off 10 minutes ago, only now you're over hearing him tell someone else, and as it turns out he's actually pretty interesting. Just like that, but fifty times worse.
	I pull to the side of the road and stop my chopper. She looks over, turns back, and joins my side.
	I say, "Sorry."
	"I don't want pity. This is the one place I can be like everyone else. So don't ruin that for me. 'kay?"
	"I didn't mean that." I say. "I mean I'm sorry I lied to you."
	Now she looks insulted. "You lied? About what? All of it?"
	"No. All that I said is true. Designing the choppers, the LE key all of it. I just didn't tell you the whole truth." Mirae narrows her eyes. "I wasn't the one who got the LE for Christmas. It was for my brother, he's been dead some eight years now." Mirae's lips move as if looking for the words but comes up silent. So, I cut her off. "I'm like ye, then. I don't want pity."
	Finally, Mirae finds the words, and says "I see."
	This is the first time I've ever told someone in-game about Jordan. It's scary and feels kinda good. Like I've been holding back this flood of emotion, and there's a small leak now, and the rest itches to come pouring out. I don't like the silence between us. It feels condemning.
	I say," I contacted Haven and inherited his account." There's no point in going on about the drivers license subterfuge when they were going to transfer ownership anyways if I could produce a death certificate. "This is a legacy game for me. I've got this whole guild to build up because I know it's what he'd have wanted. On top of that, I want to find the players who used to be members so I can tell them what happened."
	"That's heavy stuff." She says. Her words are contemplative. I'd prefer if they were happy.
	"Yeah well... Your story's not all pink clouds and bunnies." She shrugs. Conceding the point. "Look, we just met, but believe it or not you're the first person I've shared that with in-game. It's not the kind of thing you advertise. Also, I don't know many people Haven side and I want to be friends. "
	Speaking while restarting her bike, she says, "You're gonna have to if you want to talk to lost guild members." She blows right past the friends comment. I let it slide.
	"Yeah well, we'll cross that bridge when we come to it."
	She's right of course. I just need time to adjust, both to being treated like a minor celebrity thanks to the LE key, and explaining where it came from. Telling a lie about it seems like a slap in the face to Jordan's memory. My heart feels lighter for having said something.
	Mirae and I ride together approaching the southern gate of Athelon.
Melpomine:	We're here.
TakoStar:	Took you long enough.
Melpomine:	Wah.

	"Now to see what kind of rabble Tako's rounded up."
	I duck into the inn with Mirae on my heals. The scent of beef stew is heavy in the air, and it makes me hungry. There are a lot of tables, most of which are filled with adventurers and Havenfolk alike drinking. To my surprise, it's the haven folk that are chugging down huge mugs of ale, and the adventurers sipping water.
	A bar maid in an apron approaches, and opens her mouth as if to ask us if we need anything, but just in time my eyes spot Tako with a few others at his table.

"Can you get me some of that stew? It smells fantastic.... and some... "I look around not much for ale, but don't want to order plain water. "Tea, if you don't mind."
	"Go have a seat, n' I'll be right with you."
	"Thanks," I say. Wondering what the stew will taste like.
	Tako's saved us a corner table, wearing the same dark leathers I spotted him in before. The two adventurers by his side couldn't be more different.
Laurelade

	The gnome's draped in impressive looking wizard robes, with a cowl that gives off an eerie blue glow like bioluminescence. The image clashes with the necklace of arcane sigils hanging from her neck and making the air around her electric with charged magic energy. The Man, Frobod, sports plate armor in charcoal black with a chest piece shaped like a skull absent a lower jaw.

Mage is self explanatory, fire ball tosser, and that sort of thing. Death knight? I'm not so sure.

I'm having flashbacks to Lord of the Rings as I take in our eclectic races, and decide in the moment to give them all a try. If we're hunting dragons, each guild member that's part of the group will add to the quest completion.

Mirae has joined the guild.

It's weird as hell to think that my best friend in the real world right now is a disembodied AI, named Sal. Mirae's in a wheelchair with no use of her limbs. What's my excuse?

Hearty meal.
Regeneration increased by 50%.
6 minutes remaining.
	That's nice, I guess, but it's not like we'll be carrying around cauldrons of stew through dungeons. The game seems annoyingly fond of providing regeneration buffs at the times we least need them. I try the tea, but instead it tastes like hot water. So bad, so sad. I guess now I know why adventurers drink water.
	Tako says, "The closest dungeon to the city is the Catacombs. Lots of undead, but not so many dragons. I'd call it average difficulty for a group like ours."

	"The best spot for a group like ours is way over here in Three Winds. The main boss is a wind dragon, and there's a roost which means lots of tiny dragons to farm for the quest. As you can see, however it's quite far."
	"I've got mounts covered. I have six that can move at 650% flight speed." In truth there might be a lot more since I haven't read them all yet.
	Laurelade says, "Daaaaammnnnn, girl! Did you beat up a dragon-stork or something and steal all the baby eggs?"
	I shrug mysteriously, and say "Something like that. I also have," I poke around in my VT for the mount interface and sort by ground speed. "At least that many off the top of my head, that can move at 600% ground speed."

	I say, "Ding! Ding! Ding! Give the man a prize."
	All the Haven exclusive players look to him for an explanation. Frobod says, "Freeworld has fast ground mounts since the setting is based on a magical post apocalyptic real world, but flying mounts are slow and almost unheard of."
	"That's the jist of it. Also, I'm not sure how well they'll work in Haven. In Freeworld we have paved roads, and everything's dirt over here. I dunno, seems like it could be bumpy farther away from the cities."
