Chapter 6 - In the Forehead
	We step outside. I summon all five of the Elechopper mounts, starting with my middle finger waving "INFERNO!!!" invocation. Looks of bafflement turn to awe as each of the five spirit powered motorcycles appear in mechanical growls and flashes of light. If this was a picture of real life, people would think we were somewhere outside a mid-western biker bar situated kitty-corner from a renaissance festival.
	"These are awesome!" Frobod says, immediately drawn to the black and purple ether mount with a shade trapped inside. It's the one I'd have picked for him anyway. "I've never seen them, when did they come out?"
	"Last patch." I say.
	"And you have five of them already?" Frobod asks. "How did you do all that, and then travel all the way here from Freeworld? I didn't know anyone had passed through the portal yet."
	"She's a developer." Mirae says, straddling the green bike.
	Tako takes the yellow, powered by an imprisoned sylph, and Laurelade takes the blue, with a small undine blowing water inside what appears to be a little fish tank. I suppose I should have asked if they all wanted to use my mounts, but I had a gut feeling that after seeing Mirae, my, and Frobod's rides, the other's would want their chance. Another part of me wanted to show off the kickass work I'd done in design space.
	"Ye lead the way lad, cuz I have no idea where I'm goin'."
	Laurelade says, "So does that mean you get all this free, leet stuff?"
	"I'll explain on de way." I say, "If we fly slowly, and yes the bikes can fly."
	Tako says, "That's some straight up Harry Potter shit."
	Mirae laughs a little too loud. They recently remade the series featuring a 4D, full immersion experience where you can actually walk around the characters as they say their lines. As such, Hogwarts is fresh in everyone's mind.
	"Just use party coms. Invite us. It says you and Mirae are already in a party."
	I send off the invites.
TakoStar has joined your party.
Frobod has joined your party.
Laurelade has joined your party.
	"There." Tako says. It sounds just like he's speaking in an earpiece. I check the chat settings on my VT, and sure enough I can choose to speak in either in general or party chat. From context, and the way his voice is all around me like wearing a headphones, no other onlookers can hear what we're saying. I forgot about the feature because it'd been so long since I'd played Haven, but I wonder why Mirae didn't suggest it on the way back to Athelon. I guess having a conversation over slow moving bikes on a dirt path felt more like it would in the real world, than speaking casually at high speed with the other person's words planted right in your ear.
	"Before we go..." Tako holds up a canteen, "I want to propose a toast to the Toxic Muffin's. Haven Online's first motorcycle guild."
	I'm touched by the gesture, because Tako toasting to my brother's guild name, feels like a toast made to him. I don't have a canteen or water skin handy, so I mime the gesture, while everyone else either copies my empty handed motion, or raises a container of their own. For the first time since signing in, I feel as though I've made progress in my personal quest to resurrect the Toxic Muffins from Jordan's grave.
	"To the Toxic Muffins." I say, and follow up with a quiet "Cheers."
	The guild members that have drinking containers smack and slosh them together. "Damn..." I think, "This feels really good."
	The flight to the Windroost is largely uneventful. Tako and Frobod thrill at the Elechopper's, which is to say Nightmare's, movement speed. Laurelade is a tougher crowd to please. Perhaps she has a mount of her own that's just as fast.
	In the transit time, we discuss each other's builds, what we can do, and how long we've been playing the game. As it turns out, I'm not the only Freeworld player, Frobod has some experience there too, but I'm the only one who has played it almost exclusively for the last four or five years.
	I also explain my position as a designer, and the perks that come from the company while we fly just under low clouds, with each elemental bike raining its own cosmetic effect on the world below. I look like a comet, Mirae sprinkles specks of rock and dirt, Laurelade's bike causes drops of rain, Tako causes a sudden breeze, and Frobod's casts a light like a violet and grayscale version of the aurora borealis. They all seem to accept my employment, and the limitations of my employee benefits, except Laurelade who gives me a skeptical look. She can see the DE tag on the item. Where else would it have come from?
	"Hold up!" Tako says, he raises a hand and stops overhead. We all slow and perch mid-air in a flying V formation. I get a kick out of the idea of pretending to be UFOs. Once we've all stopped moving he points, we can see little creatures milling about just outside a dark hole in the side of the mountain. We've arrived.
	"Can any of you do mounted combat?" Tako asks.
	"I've never tried." I say, though I'm liking the image of roaring in on a badass motorbike, wielding Exsanguinator, and chopping off dragon heads as I go.
	"I can do ranged." Mirae says, as she nocks an arrow, gripping the seat of the Earthen Chopper with her thighs.
	"Scooti, why don't you rush down there and give it a shot, and we'll cover you. If it goes south, we'll join." I nod at Tako. I don't mind him taking charge even though I'm the guild leader since it's been so long since I've done a Haven dungeon. Moving around in Haven feels much the same as Freeworld, however I haven't worn plate or used a longsword since I last raided here. "Mirae, stick to heals for now. I don't want to risk you getting agro until we see how much Scoot can hold."
	"Laurel and Fro, you're DPS, you know what to do?"
	Laurel says, "Yeah we got it."
	"Fro, since you're in plate, I want you to be off-tank. Keep an eye on Mirae and Laurel. If they pull agro before Scooti can get to them, taunt off them with higher priority going to protecting Mirae."
	"Got it." Frobod says. I expect Laurel to be offended, but she seems to think nothing of it. I'm not wild about her attitude sometimes, but this shows that she at least knows her place in the party. Protect heals first because they keep everyone else alive. Heal tank before DPS because they prevent the rest of the party from taking damage.
	Tako says, "We're good to go. Have at it Scooti." I spin Exsanguinator in my writs trying to get a feel for how to strike while mounted. I don't know whether it's better to swoop down like an eagle, or go to ground a few meters a way, and approach as if on horse. "Scooti?"
	"Yeah, sorry. Just trying to figure out how to make this work."
	I decide on the ground approach for the sake of simplicity, and find myself wishing I had a halberd instead of a sword for longer reach. When my bike touches ground I hear Frobod say "What are you doing?" but I ignore him. There are four little dragons bobbing up and down with golden reptile wings. I have my target.
	I hit the throttle and charge.
	No response from the monsters as I approach, holding the handle bars with one hand, and wielding the sword in the other. Thirty meters ahead. Twenty, no response. Then bestial shrieks sound around me as all four whelps converge for the attack.
Taunt. Successful.
Windroost Whelp #1 attacks.
Windroost Whelp #2 attacks.
Windroost Whelp #3 attacks.
	Exsanguinator bites deep into the first whelp's skin before it has a chance to connect.
You have killed Windroost Whelp.
You have gained the skill Mounted Combat.
	I try to swing at number two but it's a miss. It takes evasive action, and aborts its attack before it can connect. The third hits me right in the chest and sends me flying, Elechopper rolling on without me before tipping on to its side. I don't take any damage, but now I'm prone. Whelp numbers 2 and 3 are about to attack when a fork of blue lightning rains down from above, zapping them both, and leaving blackened bodies that look like scorched turkeys.
Laurelade has killed Windroost Whelp.
Laurelade has killed Windroost Whelp.
	A dart of green magic energy speeds past from overhead, toward the cave entrance.
Mirae has killed Windroost Whelp.
	"Nice hit." The gnome says, smirking at my toppled chopper. The rest of the party comes to ground around me. Getting back to my feet, I make the "Bloop bloop!" sounds and gestures to banish the bikes to the nothing from whence they came.
	"Gotta start somewhere. Mounted Combat level 1!" I cheer sarcastically.
	Tako says, "It'll be a lot harder once we're inside. Everyone ready?"
	"Ready." We say in a chorus, except for Frobod who says "Just a sec." He's checking something on his VT.
	"Nice." Frobod says. "Now we only have 49,996 dragons to kill. Even the little ones count."
	I say, "Lets do this." and march myself into the open mouth of the dungeon.
	Passing through a dungeon portal is like experiencing zero gravity for anywhere between ten and twenty seconds, and then your feet are set gently back on the ground. Each dungeon transports you to a parallel realm within the game world, so that multiple groups can experience the same content at once. World bosses, by contrast exist outside of dungeons, so there's only one of them available in the game for players to fight at any given time.
	This dungeon looks like a cave with some kind of magical lighting. As a half-elf, even though I'm wearing what's effectively a dwarf costume in the form of an Alias, I take no penalties for vision in low light environments. I still have all my half-elf stat bonuses and penalties, and none of those I'd get from playing a true dwarf character.
	There's a giant egg the color of cocoa powder flecked with glowing amber to the right of me. I take a step closer to investigate. It begins to quiver and shake. I instantly take a step back. You don't have to be an endgame player to know that if you wait too long, something's going to come out of it. A few seconds later Tako, then Mirae, and the rest of the crew appear behind me.
	"Careful!" I say, "There's eggs, and I got a feeling that if you get too close they'll hatch."
	"Duh." says the gnome, now that we stand side by side, I see that the top of her head is about up to my nipples. That's really short considering my dwarf alias, and I get to wondering where half-gnome babies come from. None of the others seem surprised, by my comments about the egg, and I realize that I'm the only one in the group that hasn't been here. ...and here I thought I was being useful.
	Tako says, "This place is full of them. Usually I'd say we should do a speed run, moving as quickly as possible, so as not to hatch the eggs, and hit the boss, but with this quest, I think it's better to do a full clear. "
	"Agreed." says Laurelade.

	Tilashia is a couple heads taller than anyone in our party; a lean woman with serpentine skin, and wicked red claws in place of finger nails. Frobod's unlucky enough to be closest to her, and she extends her wicked hand sending a trio of magic bolts flying. His health drops by ten percent.
	No time to waste, I bang Exsanguinator and my shield together.
Taunt. Successful.
Tilashia attacks.
	One volley, two volleys, three bursts of glowing blue-white projectiles fly my direction from Tilashia's open palm, lowering my health by twenty percent. It feels like being pelted with hard packed snowballs. The pain quickly fades as the scent of cherry blossoms surrounds me. I charge, and suddenly find myself looking up at my feet as they fly out from under me. The ground's been lubricated by exploding dragon egg slime. I crash to the floor and slide an uncomfortable six feet toward our opponent. Hard metal armor clunks and scrapes against slippery stone.
	The boss leaps an impossible distance, and crashes into my prone form, clawing at my helm, but so far doing no damage. I hear a skidding sound off to my right, and catch a glimpse of Frobod riding the draconic slip and slide. At least I'm not the only one. Everyone else has either managed to keep their feet, or hasn't moved from their starting position.
	I try pushing the boss off me, but it's no use because she's so heavy. I watch Tako's dagger come down hard behind her head. That's gotta hurt. I can't see the rest of his body, because my entire view consists of Tilashia's crocodile toothed head and upper body. For a moment her skin frosts over as Laurelade blasts her with some kind of cold magic. The frigid effect fades in just a second.
	I can't swing my sword because I'm pinned. I can't block with my shield because it's pressed against the left half of my body. I normally don't advise disarming yourself mid-combat, but desperate times call for desperate measures. I let go of Exsanguinator's grip, and press my gauntleted hand to Tilashia's hard, scaly chest.
	I growl, "Helios smite thee!" and for a moment a beam of pure sunlight shines onto her chest exactly where I touch, in spite of being indoors. An arc of yellow holy light jerks back and fourth down the column of sun like an arc of electricity, harmlessly through my hand, and into my opponent.
Smite. Successful.
Tilashia takes 2 holy damage.
Tilashia is stunned.
	A ten second countdown appears above her head, with an icon that looks like a whirlpool, indicating that the creature can't take any action. A lot can happen in ten seconds.

Laurelade follows up with magic bolts of her own, and Tako makes a lucky dive out of the way of Tilashia's flying body. Time seems to move a bit slower he turns and manifests one of his weighted throwing knives from out of nowhere. His foot slips as he tosses, and it clatters harmlessly to the ground somewhere behind the boss. Having made a reflex save, he doesn't join me on the floor and in the egg muck.
	I grab Exsanguinator's hilt, and struggle back to my feet. My boots slide a little, but now that I'm expecting slippage, maintaining my balance is easier. Laurelade lets loose another frost spell which encases Tilashia's lower body in a block of ice.
	Tilashia snaps out of her stun as I close the distance. I clunk Exsanguinator and shield together to make sure I have her attention. 	Three things happen in the next instant. Tilashia rears up on her hind legs and roars "Die!!!" Meanwhile, Frobod turns on his heel, and sprints the opposite direction... as well as anyone can sprint wearing plate armor anyway. My health, now at thirty percent, suddenly leaps to one hundred.

Point Break! You have gained ten points to distribute among attributes.
	Tako says, "Congrats on the ding both of you."
	Although Freehaven doesn't have a leveling system, it does have "Point Breaks" which are equivalent to levels, otherwise known as "dings." Each allowing players to spend accumulated attribute points on their primary stats. The higher the stat becomes however, the more expensive it is to advance to the next level. The number of dings you can get are technically unlimited, but most people usually don't see more than one or two each month.
Stamina. Current level: 20.
Points required for next level: 53.
You have 63 unspent points. Do you want to raise Stamina to 21?
Yes / No

	A moment later, an arc of lightning shoots from Raika's maw, and connects with the tip of Exsanguinator. It jumps from my weapon, to my shield, and into my body. Then, Nothgoran roars in pain. Success! I'm standing right where I wanted, and an amazing fifty percent of his health vanishes. Tako wasn't kidding about this being the easy way.
	After Raika's lightning, she lands at the edge of the balcony and attempts to bash me with her tail. It catches me by surprise, but I'm able to duck before it connects. Nothgoron's axe crashes against my shield, as Raika follows her tail swipe with a claw attack, which connects with my side.
	My health drops a little, but it's nothing Mirae can't handle. Raika and I go back and forth trading blows as I try to either block or dodge Nothgoron's attacks. Laurelade is making good use of her wand, as I see as much as hear Frobod land a clean chop with his greatsword in Raika's flank. I have no idea where Tako is, it's hard to keep track of everyone during combat.
	Raika beats her wings again, which is a sign for the other close-range fighters to run out of range of her lightning, Frobod does, but then when I least expect it see Tako leaping on to Raika's back. She lifts off the ground, and I try to make myself small by crouching, thinking it'll cut down on wind resistance.
	By the power of cheap-ass video game logic, Nothgoran is unaffected by the swirls of air Raika's stirring and takes my hunkered stance as an invitation to attack. CRACK! The blade of his axe, meets the resistance of my shield. I almost fall back, but manage to keep my footing. I'm not leaving my position.
	Raika's winds reach peak velocity, but I'm still standing. Laurelade sends alternating blasts of arcane and frost magic, while Frobod shoots beams of necromancer death magic from afar. A debuff appears above the boss's head, but I don't have time to view it because she's about to unleash another lightning blast.
	It dances between my sword, body and shield before taking Nothgoran down another big percent. He roars in anger, but that's okay because he's almost dead. I block another blow with my shield, and then look up to see Tako clinging to Raika's neck with one arm and sticking her with his dagger repeatedly with the other. Her health's lower than I'd expected, nearing twenty percent. She lands, and I push the offensive with Exsanguinator tearing into her snout.
	She claws, and attempts to bite with those powerful jaws, but Frobod's not having it as he slams his greatsword into her neck forcing Raika to snap her jaw shut before reaching me.
	My attack enables Nothgoran a clean hit, and I take damage.
	"Almost there!" Tako calls as I see him trying to inch his way up her neck, dagger between his teeth, like a pirate, or a kid climbing a coconut tree.
	Raika's skin briefly frosts over as Laurelade hits her with another magic attack. Ten percent left. Nothgoran swings, and Mirae's heals land just before my health would have fallen into the danger zone, but instead the blow and heal balance each other out.
	Six percent, as Frobod connects another cleave. Five percent when Laurelade blasts with her new wand. So close, I contribute some damage with another blow to Raika's face when she comes in for a bite.
	Three percent.
	Suddenly Raika howls in pain, as Tako's dagger pierces her eye. He jumps down clinching the handle, causing it to twist in the wound before pulling loose all together. Raika, zero percent.
	Tako says, "Nothgoran... Go go go go!"
	Everyone unleashes on the dragon knight. Laurelade freezes him in an ice block, Tako stabs from behind, and Exsanguinator takes its due. He's about to hit me with another chop, when Frobod comes in from the side, and separates the dragon knight's head from its body.
	I catch my breath in silence. The fight is over. Mirae tops off my health.
	Tako says, "Good job guys!" as he moves in to inspect the loot.

Chapter 8 - Hotfix
	I don't want to hold everyone up so I ride back with Lightning's Bite strapped to the back of my half-elf Alias's back. It clashes with her starting gear. The linen's coarse but I welcome the change from plate. That shit is heavy and hot after fighting dragons. It also smells like a fireplace after being doused in flame, and scorched with electricity. I'll have to take it to a repair shop, or see if Frobod can fix it.
	As for my weapon, I'm on cloud nine. I've never played a speed based tank, and my new staff has given me the stats I need to explore a whole new play style. Just as I've come to think of Exsanguinator in terms of being a proper noun, rather than just "my sword," I've already started thinking of Lightning's Bite as just "Bite," to give it more personality. It's sunset now in the game world, but part of me wishes it was night because I think the molten gleam of my Elechopper, paired with the streamers of electric blue crackling off either end of my staff will look fierce once the sun is gone.
	We're riding bikes back to town since Frobod will have a while before his new dragon's large enough for mount training, and in spite of the youth of our current guild composition, I riding the Elechoppers feels like a new tradition that could become part of our guild identity. I look forward to finding out about how new Elechoppers are obtained, and working towards farming them for all our members.
	I'm about to say something to Mirae when I realize she's not beside me. She and Tako lag behind having their own conversation in a private chat. That leaves me, Frobod and Laurelade. I take the opportunity to get to know my newest recruits.
	I say, "You're a pretty nice guy for a death knight."
	"Hey," he says, "It's a game. If I were an rper you wouldn't like me, but as it is, I just pick magic and items that look cool." He pauses a sec, and says. "What happened to your accent?"
	I say, "I just use the Scottish filter when I'm using the Scootiwutams alias. I think it compliments her character."
	He asks, "Ahh, so you're not really Scottish?"
	"Not at all. I'm from Seattle, born and raised."
	"Hope you don't mind my lack of role playing then."
	"From a self proclaimed death knight, I'm actually glad. But really, I haven't been seriously into the RP thing in years. Then I started running dungeons and raiding."
	"In Freeworld?" Frobod asks.
	"Mostly, but the Scooti alias was all Haven."
	Laurelade says, "I'm not into Soc stuff. I'm just here for the excitement and adventure."
	"I can go either way. Sometimes I'd rather just chill in a bar...or in this Haven's case tavern or inn with people I like, than actually going out in the real world."
	"I hear ya." Frobod says. "It's too bad all the food tastes like water."
	"Why is that?" Laurelade says.
	I say, "From a developer's perspective, I can tell you it's definitely possible to bring taste to this game, but the technology's patented by a company called Data-Bite and I don't think they want to shell out the cash to buy the rights for implementation."
	"That makes sense." Laurelade says.
	"Still sucks..." Frobod says.
	I shrug as if to say "What ya gonna do?" and then say, "So what do you think of the guild so far?"
	"I'm having fun." Frobod says.
	Laurelade adds, "... but we're going to need a lot more people."
	"Yeah I know. When I came back to haven I decided I wanted to build this thing back up for sentimental reasons."
	Laurelade says, "Like what?" but I just shrug. I don't feel the same kind of connection with her that I do Mirae, so I don't want to share.
	Instead, I say "I think Mirae's recruiting some people, once she has a chance to talk to them, and we'll always need more people."
	"Shouldn't be hard." Frobod says, "LE Guild and all."
	"Yeah, I just don't want a bunch of buttholes. Ages ago, this guild used to be like family."
	We can see the walls of Athelon approaching when a red exclamation mark appears at the bottom of my interface. I mentally tap it and see the following message.
Hotfix
Installing
	This must be a big fix if it's large enough to show a status bar. With modern connection speeds, any changes to previously downloaded content usually push through in an instant.
	"Did you guys see the hotfix?" I ask.
	Laurelade says, "Yeah..." Her voice trailing.
	Frobod says, "Mine just finished installing. What is it?"
	I say, truthfully, "I have no idea. Usually these things are applied if they find a bug in the patch we just downloaded, but they don't usually take this long to install."
	Laurelade says, "I haven't seen any bugs."
	"That's kind of the point." I say, "They want to fix them while the game's running before anyone spots them."
	We come to ground just outside the south gate, a minute later Tako and Mirae follow. She's all smiles and laughter, and Tako has a smug grin painted all over his boyish face. I'm glad she's happy.
	Frobod says, "Did you guys see the hotfix?"
	Tako says, "Yeah, it just downloaded."
	Mirae adds, "It was a big patch. Biggest update ever. There were bound to be a few problems."
	"Yeah..." I say. "I guess your right," but something about the situation doesn't sit right with me. Hotfixes are usually applied so fast that no one even notices. The fact that everyone in our group caught the progress bar, tells me that something is definitely out of the ordinary.
	Simply put, an update of this size shouldn't be applied while we're all signed in. The application of so much content is enough to warrant a separate offline patch in of itself. Haven Online and Freeworld like announcing them ahead of time to generate a buzz about the future of the game. Meanwhile, I'm clueless as to what this may be.
	"Everything okay?" Mirae asks.
	"Yeah, just... Thinking." I say, "You know I'm a designer, but I didn't hear anything about another major update at work. Especially so soon after the merge. It doesn't feel right."
	"I dunno." Frobod says.
	I nod, nothing I can do about it one way or another. "Alright so... Good first run. I'm glad to have you."
	Tako asks, "Does this mean we passed our tests?"
	I look at everyone, my eyes linger on Laurelade a touch longer than the rest. "I'm not kicking anyone." Tako cheers, and does a fist pump, as if there'd been any question. "Go sell your loot, and meet up at the Smiling Otter if you want to run something else, or check out Dragonswood later."
	"I think I'm done." Frobod says, "I haven't eaten, but I might come back in like four or five hours if you're still here."
	"I should be." I say, remembering the Chicago style pizza after work. "I took vacation days for this, so I'm planning to make this a marathon."

I've forgotten what Haven calls its formally trained martial artists. Frobod and Tako nod. "Monks. Let me know. All the gear from my other characters are going to be melee dps, or caster oriented."
	"Will do boss." Tako says.

	"A bit abrupt," I think as I watch her go. Frobod, Tako, and Mirae drift off their separate directions, but not before I see Mirae give Tako a hug before going. Oh dear. In guild romances can either bring people closer together, or cause a crap ton of drama.
	I say, "Hey Mirae!" as she walks off, and jog to catch up. She stops and looks back. "I'm promoting you to officer within the guild so you can invite your friends without my approval." I clap her on the shoulder hoping the gesture conveys the fondness I feel for her crawling around in my chest. Of all our current members, knowing about her real world situation, she strikes the closest to how I remember Jordan describing the Toxic Muffins. Not just avatars within a game, but real people with real shit to deal with on a planet called Earth. "I trust your judgment, and again, I'm glad to have you."

She sticks two fingers in her mouth and unleashes a shrill whistle. A pulse of white and lilac flashes in the way of summoned creatures. her pegasus. It's the first time I've seen it, and I admire the mount as a beautiful animal. It beats its powerful wings, moving air and lifting my guild mate off the ground. I open the guild interface on my VT.

Thunder Bridge
Cost:
3000 manna + 1000 manna for each additional traveler up to a number equal to half your intellect.
Effect: 	Ride the lightning to a place you'd like to go.
	My alias, MarineLu, is a wind and storm themed magical martial artist, who used her martial arts in the form of katas, and strikes to channel her magic. She can use spells from other elements, but has an affinity for workings that deal with air. Thunder Bridge, while not defensive, is a beautiful means of fast travel. Before the update, it could take you anywhere you wanted to go, so long as you remembered going there once. The only drawback is that it tends to leave your manna drained upon arrival, and could cause damage to structures if used inside buildings. Part of me wonders if it can take me back to Freeworld for a visit, and I add it to my list of things to try.
Flow Like Water (Rank 2)
Cost:  	2000 Manna
Effect:
Your body takes on the fluid qualities of water causing it to flow out of the path of incoming attacks. Lasts 30 minutes, or until the loss of 10 levels of health have been negated. Negates physical damage only.
	Previously, I'd only used that one in PvP where a tank's taunts were useless since they didn't have any effect on players. As an evasion tank however, Flow Like water effectively gives me ten extra health levels that can't be restored short of re-casting.
	I'd really like to have an arcane shield like the haven Mages are famous for using, but alas I never played an arcane caster in Haven Online. It might be something worth exploring. Aside from Flow Like Water, which I most certainly intend to level up, the only other spells I have from Marine that'll help with surviving damage are a heal over time spell, and something that turns your skin to stone at the cost of half your dexterity. Not the best choice when you plan on dodging and beating up mobs with your sparky electric taunt stick. When playing a dex tank, not getting hit is the name of the game.
	I page through Jordan's abilities on Stabbies to see if I find anything interesting. He's got something called "Shadow Step" which allows you to teleport into the shadow of an object or other person's shadow within line of sight, and "Shadow Retreat" which yanks you out of combat, and allows you to become a two dimensional shadow, immune to all but fire and holy damage for up to 30 seconds. It causes you to drop all agro, and take double damage from fire and holy magic.
	It's what I call an "Oh shit button," for when the healer's out of manna and I'm on my last sliver of health. It might buy me enough time to get away, and resurrect someone else after the battle finishes.

	I equip Bite, and hunt around for a clear area. The last thing I want is for someone to walk into the path of my staff, or accidentally set a thatched roof on fire. It's evening now so a combination of mundane and magic lanterns keep the streets bright. 	I hadn't thought of that. Marine's Freeworld weapons were magic war fans that produced a variety of effects, but now armed with a staff, the movements will be a bit different.

I open myself to my manna, my pool is miniscule compared to that which I'd have using Marine's gear, but my high intelligence and decent charisma provide me with enough for what I'm doing.
	I channel the energy, inviting the cool Yin ether to course through my body. My staff and hands are a blur of motion, as a translucent dome of purple light appears and begins to expand around me. Leaves blow in the trees. Park rabbits hop opposite directions.
	"Stand in the dome" I say, "It won't hurt you." My voice is monotone and robotic. I should have told him before hand, because talking shakes my focus making it harder to cast.
	My staff is a whirlwind, unseen and deadly. Both it and my hands are a blur. A massive snake of electricity twists over the outside surface of my magic dome that's taller than I am. The small hairs on my arms and the back of my neck rise, the hair on my head wants to do the same. I hear Frobod make a noise like a shiver. I can feel the magic energy welling up and up, coursing through my body, sparks dare to spit from my mouth and eyes. I imagine the mailbox outside the Durolan bank. The image burns crisp in my mind.
	I'm about to explode, when I bring Bite to a halt and slam its butt into the earth. I imagine stabbing the cobblestones in front of the Durolan bank. Thunder cracks. A bolt of thick lightning stabs from the heavens to my staff, into me, then to Frobod. The world goes white, and returns to our vision with spots, that fade in the way of those incurred by staring at the sun.
	Time slows, as Frobod and I are suddenly in the sky, looking down as if from an airplane. He's holding my left hand, dragged behind, while my right's held high over head clinching my weapon. The tip of Bite shines like a blue comet pulling us onward along what seems to be an endless road of electricity. My hair lashes every which direction. I scream my excitement, as I fall in love with the cold air beating my face.
	Thunder crashes again, as our vision again goes white, and we are deposited roughly in front of the Durolan bank. The spots from staring into a too bright light fade within seconds of our landing. I keep my feet, but Frobod takes a tumble. I laugh, having almost forgotten how fun it could be, like sky diving, an amusement park ride, and latching on to a tow rope all at the same time.

	"It was a long time ago." I think about how much I want to reveal, and follow the comment with, "The first time I signed into Stabbies, the main character on his account, he'd been gone for two years. I looked up at the corner of my interface and saw the Enhanced Dexterity and Enhanced Vitality buffs ticking down. It's kinda hard to explain what I felt but... It was just like... 'He did that.' It was something that was still alive in the world that was caused by something he did with his hands... or whatever you call it in a virtual environment."
	"That must have been rough." Frobod says.
	I nod. "The world moves on real fast against the contributions of a nineteen year old kid. I made it a point to sign in once a year for a few minutes to sit by the lake, retrieve the annual regular and limited edition items the company sends you, and watch those buffs trickle away. I never took time to go through his gear, check out his mounts, or visit this keep because it'd take too much time. I had to stretch forty-five minutes of game time out over several years to keep the buffs from running out. Day before the merge... yesterday to be exact, everything changed. I sat by the lake until they went all the way down to zero because I didn't know what'd happen after the update. I don't know if you understand but, I wanted to be there to experience as much of Jordan's buffs as possible because they came from him. It was a basic choice between being there to watch those times drop down to zero, and take the risk of signing in and discovering they were gone."
	Frobod says, "Are you alright?"
	I sigh, and say "It was oddly liberating. Jordan used to always talk about how much he loved his guild so now I'm trying to rebuild it. Everyone left after the leader went MIA for over a year. I have no idea how long they held out. "
	Frobod says, "I guess that's what those guards were talking about then."
	"Guess so, but I have no way of knowing if Jordan ever interacted with those NPCs specifically. My bet is that something about the update is making some NPCs more lifelike, and they used Stabbies' login and location records to cobble together some back story. There's something different since the hotfix, and I don't just mean the inability to log out."
	The reminder of the hotfix sends Frobod into another one of his brooding silences. He looks down at his VT, but has the restraint not to swipe or pull up any menus. I decide to change the subject to keep his head in a nicer space.
	"Helios smite thee!" I call out, and direct my open palm at the practice dummy. Nothing happens. I should be seeing a flicker of sunlight, and a tendril of smoke rising from my target. I try again, "Helios smite thee!" but nothing happens.
	"It's not working." I say. "Use one of your death magic attacks."
	Frobod curses in a guttural fantasy language I can't understand, sending a dark streak of magic that looks like a giant black sperm trailing tendrils of octopus ink. It poofs into the target with a sound like punching a pillow leaving only a small black mark on the dummy's chest.
	"No Smite for Melpomine?" I ask, to no one in particular.
	"Maybe because it's night." Frobod says, but I'm already shaking my head.
	"Shouldn't make a difference, since it doesn't rely on actual sunlight."
	I pull out my VT, and switch to the Scootiwutams alias. By now Frobod's used to my transformation. Her shield and armor's still blackened by Tilashia's flames, and smells like an old smokehouse.
	"Helios smite thee!"
	As expected a narrow column of sunlight pierces the night, and stabs the target, followed by a sudden jolt of holy energy. The beam flickers out as quickly as it came. I swap back to the Melpomine alias, and say "Strange." I pull up the skill description for more information.
Smite (Rank 4)
Cost: 		           120 Manna
Description: 	Call upon your patron deity to smite your enemies.
Effect:
Deals up to 4 holy damage, and has a chance to stun your target.
Requirements: 	Clergy Member, Symbol of patron deity
	"Oh," I say. "Scooti's shield is painted with a symbol of Helios. You need to be a clergy member, and have a symbol of the God you're invoking for it to work."
	Frobod says, "I guess you should pick up a Helios necklace or ring then?"
	"Maybe, but Melpomine's not a clergy member, only Scooti. Melpomine will have to find her own in-game faith, or I'll have to show the Church of Helios that Melpomine and Scootiwutams are the same person."
	"I guess a necklace might have been too easy."
	"I can do without Smite." I say, "I've got this Shadow Step ability to surprise enemies and grab their attention. I haven't decided. I may want to take Melpomine a different religious direction. See if any other deities give cooler perks for what I'm doing with her."
	"I guess this means no Res?"
	"I think the requirements are the same for all divine magic. If someone dies, I'll just change into Scooti for a second and no big deal."
	Frobod says, "That works." and puts his hands on his hips.
	"We can use this place to train noobs if we recruit any. So far everyone seems fairly experienced. We could also use the grounds to hold contests if we need money. Charge an entry fee, and offer up some loot we're not using to the top competitors."
	Frobod laughs, "I hadn't even thought of that. Good thinking."
	I say, "Depending on how the inside looks, we could also host balls for soc players, if someone wants to organize them."
	Frobod says, "All that goes right over my head, but I guess that's why you're our leader."
	"Yeah..." I say, thinking of possibilities. I get a warm feeling as I imagine this guild coming together. If we get a reputation for hosting some of the best events, our guild's name will be out there for old members to rediscover. "Why don't we check out the inside."
	Frobod says, "It's your castle. Lead the way."
	I say, "I may hold the deed as guild leader, but it's all of ours. Don't forget it."
	I love the sound of our boots on the draw bridge, I look over the side to check out the moat. It's about ten or fifteen feet to the water, stone walls on either side. I also wonder if there's anything living inside. It'd be just like Jordan to hide some awful creatures down there in case of invaders, and I doubt they'd die for having not been fed.
	The actual door to the keep is made of a heavy dark wood with iron bands, much like the gate. I push... once...twice... three times before I throw my shoulder into it. It creeks open. Frobod lurks behind. The walls are high and dark, I can see a hint of rafters overhead with my half-elf low light vision. Given the brilliant reception of the outdoor grounds, I'd expected similar treatment inside.
	Small magic torches burn on the walls that give off no heat, and casts the environment in an eerie blue light. It feels like entering a new dungeon. The front hall, decorated with just a large throw rug, and a ripped up portrait of someone I don't know, comes to a T with what appears to be more dark gloomy hallways to either side.
	"Nice place." Frobod says, a bit of sarcasm in his voice.
	"Creepy." I say. "Let's go right and keep going right until we've been around the whole castle.
	We're greeted by a great hall with a giant crystal chandelier when we pass through the antechamber. I want a way to make it light up so it's not so gloomy, but alas we're stuck navigating by the eerie gloom of magic torch lights.
	The clatter of unbroken glass bouncing off hard floors sounds from somewhere beyond the hall, which is followed by a giant belch.
	"Someone's here?" I ask in a hard whisper.
	Frobod says, "Sounds like it." His voice is puzzled, as if not able to make any more sense of our visitor than I could. I equip Bite, and proceed with caution trying to step quietly so as not to alert whoever's here. The effort's wasted, as Frobod's plate clanks with each step, but at least it seems that whoever's here hasn't noticed.
	The next room is a dining room with a long table. There's stains on the tablecloth which is covered in plates smeared with the remains of old food. Empty wine bottles, some standing, others on their sides are strewn about the place from the surface of the table, to clusters on the floor.
	A warm yellow glow pours from an open door to our right, that appears to lead to a kitchen. At the other end of the dining hall, we see another room flickering a fiery red and gold light. I motion Frobod to stop so I can listen. I hear fire pops, as though from a fireplace, and another long belch. Then there's blue flashes coming from the far doorway that remind me of... Television? That can't be right. I give Frobod a questioning look.
	He shrugs, making his armor sound another clank, and presses a shushing finger to my lips.
	He whispers, "I doubt you'll be attacked in your own castle."
	I whisper, creeping closer to the far door as Frobod follows, "On the count of three rush in."
	One nod from the death knight.
	"One... Two.... Three!"
	We burst through the door. I brandish Bite ready to strike, Frobod hefts his greatsword in preparation to do the same. The room... is empty, aside from a large red sofa enough for four or five people, and a couple of love seats at either end, all angle toward a crackling fireplace and what appears to be a holographic television hanging in the air. An old episode of a reality TV streams across its face.
	Another belch. A bottle of wine floats mid air, next to a ball of wobbling yellow and azure energy.
	"What the?" I say in my non-whisper voice. The bottle of wine clatters to the floor spilling its deep red contents all over the rug. Frobod dismisses his greatsword but I'm not quite ready to let go of Bite.
	The illusory TV disappears, and the room's suddenly brightens like hanging around a club just after closing time. I squint at the sudden change of lighting.
	"Mas..ter?" a voice says, it's hard to tell if it's male or female, but it seems to come from the floating orb perched right over the sofa. I can't get over how messy everything is. Books on the floor, empty bottles everywhere, dirty blankets in mounds at the end of the couches. It's like someone had an eight year sleepover party and never cleaned up.
	"Master!" The voice says. "It's so good to see you! When you didn't come back for so long, and all the Muffins left. I thought you were doomed!"
	I say, "What the hell is going on around here? This place is a mess! What are you?"
	"I'm sorry master. I will clean master." The voice gives a small burp, not as massive as those before. "As always, I'm here to assist you."
	Frobod says, "Are you drunk?"
	The Orb says, "Just a little. I promise, it will pass."
	I repeat my last question, "What are you?"
	"I'm the keep, same as always, same as before. I'm here to serve."
	I ask, "Are you telling me I have a drunken castle?"
	"Not too long now.. Just a bit, would you prefer me to take your favorite form?" The voice sounds terrified, and hopeful. Trying to win points back after being caught slacking on the job.
	"Sure." I say, "Whatever you want."
	"Yes, master. I'm here to serve." The orb explodes in a smoky poof that reminds me of the stage acts of old timey musicians doing magic tricks. In its place stands a petite elf woman, with long red hair down to her buttocks. She's topless, with improbably large breasts, and the bottoms of a chainmail bikini.
	"Whoaaa!" I shield my eyes with the crook of my elbow. "Ball of light is just fine. No showing off boobies for the new guild members... or any other guests for that matter!" Real mature Jordan... Real mature. I get the feeling he made great use of the adult content settings upon his eighteenth birthday. Talk about Haven preserving a person's memory as they truly were.
	I look Frobod over, a budding smirk teases his lips. I roll my eyes. "Dudes."
	After changing back to a ball of light, the orb says, "If you prefer I can adapt a more modest appearance?"
	"That would be wonderful." I say in a deadpan voice. Another puff of smoke and the orb transforms into a butler, full tuxedo, and a hairline moustache over his upper lip. "What's your name anyway?"
	"Master calls me Cupcake, or Alfred. You can use either one, both, or grant a new name." Cupcake says.
	"Cupcake, as in Cupcake Keep?" I ask, "... and Alfred like the Batman guy?"
	"That is correct." Cupcake says.
	Frobod asks, "What's with that TV thing you were watching when we came in?"
	Cupcake... Alfred looks to me, and asks "May I answer, master? ..hic!"
	I say, "You can answer any of his questions tonight."
	"The television is a paid upgrade, purchased ten years ago as an enhancement to this castle. ...hic! It enables users to watch any public television broadcasts, or paid programming for which you are an offline subscriber. ...hic! It may only be viewed within guild quarters."
	"Can you get the news?" Frobod asks, hope crawling into his voice.
	Cupcake says, "It can be broadcast from any of several sources."
	"Put it on."
	Cupcake says, "I'm sorry, you don't currently have access to this feature."
	I say, "Cupcake, allow any guild member to use the TV if someone else isn't already watching. First come first serve, alright?"
	Cupcake says, "I have changed the settings." He pinches his eyes shut, and then opens them wide as if to shake off a massive case of grogginess. He does it again, and it contrasts with the whole stick-up-the-ass butler costume.
	Frobod says, "Put on the news."
	The phantom television screen re-appears over the crackling fire. Cupcake asks, "Would you like a controller?"
	Frobod says, "Of course I want a controller, give it here."
	In the blink of an eye a black TV remote like the one I have at home appears on the sofa's armrest. Frobod eagerly grabs it, and plops down beside a pile of dirty blankets and bed sheets.
	Alfred seems to notice this, and hastens to gather wine bottles from the floor while continuing our conversation. A woven sack appears in his hand where he collects them.
	"This is a lot to take in. Why is this place so dirty?" The butler, looking embarrassed says "Give me one day and it'll be good as new."
	I guess that's nice to hear.
	Frobod's flicking through the channels, rapid fire, one after another.
	He says, "I don't see anything!"
	I say, "The hotfix just went through, we probably won't see anything for a few hours, or tomorrow morning depending on how fast they fix it."
	"I was just hoping for news, or some sign that anyone knows we're stuck here."
	"I know you were." I say. "If we can't log within the next few hours, I say we sleep on it. With any luck, we'll wake up in the real world wherever we were when we came online."
	"Alfred." I say, "Does this keep have sleeping quarters for guild members?"
	"It does master. A new room is generated each time someone joins. Would you like me to prepare your rooms for you?" He looks like he's about to hiccup again, but holds it down.
	"Not yet." I say. "Just make sure they're ready before we go to bed. We'll be sleeping here tonight." I turn to Frobod, who has settled on CNN which is reporting non-Freehaven content. "Good with you?"
	"Yeah, better than sleeping in an inn somewhere."
	Alfred says, "I see the Toxic Muffins have gained 37 new members. Would you like me to prepare recall stones for them?"
	I say, "That's like a port right?"
	"Yes," Alfred says, "At the current level, they can be used once every six hours to return to the courtyard from any location within Freehaven Online, but they must first obtain one by coming to the keep in person."
	I'm glad that Alfred's drunkenness is fading, if NPC, balls of energy can even get drunk. I'm taken aback by the fact that Alfred seems to have broken the 4th wall.
	"How do you know what Freehaven Online is?"
	"TV, master, and that I am an administrative construct designed to take care of your castle's needs."
	"So you're like some sort of hybrid AI? Part NPC, and part...AI like I interact with in the real world?"
	"This is correct, but who's to say that what you're experiencing is not the real world?"
	"I uhh..." I let it drop, because I'm not game for philosophizing. "Just get this place cleaned up."
	I sit beside Frobod, and make a show of throwing the dirty laundry on the floor. I want my behavior to say "Bad butler!" so he feels ashamed of letting this beautiful property waste away. I put a comforting hand on Frobod's thigh and say "At least we've got a way to see what's happening out there."
	"That's something." He says. "It'd be nice if communication went two ways."
	"Can you do me a favor?" I ask.
	"What?" He says.
	"Take off the plate when you're sitting on the couch. It's not comfortable sitting next to a metal man."
	"Oh... Sorry."
	Instead of manually changing by stripping off each piece of armor, like some players do for the sake of realism. Frobod messes around in his interface, and his attire abruptly changes. One moment he's in plate, and another he's sitting next to me in a black and gold embroidered tunic, and matching trousers.
	"Much better." I say.
	"I wear the armor so much that I forget that I'm wearing it."
	"Seems like it'd be hard to forget." I say.
	"It's a game." He says. "I usually sign out after battle, and when I sign in, I'm ready to go again."
	Sitting beside him watching the virtual television feels like a real world movie night in a home that I couldn't possibly afford. I kinda get why Jordan bought this feature. It's something that allows guild members to just hang out in virtual space, like they could in real life if they weren't however many thousands of miles away. I enjoy the heat of the fire, and plush cushions under my butt. It's a feeling I could get used to after long days of dragon slaying.
	 Alfred's left the room, but I want to ask him something.
	I call out, "Hey Cupcake!" over the back of the sofa to snag his attention. In the same poof of smoke he appears right in front of me like a personal genie. Frobod cranes his neck to see around him and I say, "Don't block the TV."
	He says, "Yes master." and hobbles a couple of steps to the left.
	"Can you give us recall stones now, before I forget?" Instead of speaking he holds out a green pebble, the color of glow in the dark paint I played with as a kid. Frobod takes it, but keeps watching the TV. "What about mine?"
	Alfred says, "You already have one. It's a unique item, so you can't have more than one in your inventory."
	"Really?" I say, and check my VT. Sure enough, there it is, usable only by characters that have joined the guild. I wave my hand and say, "That's all. Go get this place cleaned up." The butler collapses into a pile of smoke that starts tall, and shrinks as it rolls across the floor.
Mirae: 	I'm done.
Melpomine: 	Can you come to Durolan?
Mirae:		Sure, why?
Melpomine:	We've got headquarters. You've got to come on the grounds to get your recall stone. Also, I'm bored. I want to start rounding everyone up to plan for the Dragonswood tournament.
Mirae:		I'll take a portal, where should we meet?
Melpomine:	How about in front of the bank. I can show you from there.
	I stand, and give Frobod a look, he's transfixed by the television. "I'm going out to meet Mirae. You want to come or...?"
	"I'm good right here, I don't want to run anything else tonight."
	"Suit yourself." I say, "Don't break anything worse than Cupcake already has while I'm gone."
	"I'm staying right here." I turn to head out of the... living room... for lack of a better term, when Frobod clears his throat to snag my attention. "Thanks again. I'd be in a bad place if you weren't around."
	I give him a nod, pause, and march my way back over to him on the couch. I'm not the most affectionate person, but something inside says that this is the right time to give a hug. I plant one knee on the sofa cushion, and wrap my arms around his big shoulders resting some of my weight against his chest. His body heat is enticing and real, in a way I haven't experienced in the real world in a very long time.
	"You'll be okay." I say, before turning to go.
Chapter 10 - Archangel
	I'm watching one of the city's magic brooms sweep the street, pondering what would happen if I tried to grab it. Would it fight me? Would Dryslyn show up and try to arrest me for stealing public property? I've never tried. A few minutes later Mirae approaches on her pegasus, silver hooves echoing off the buildings. I straddle the fire Elechopper, and wave a greeting.
	"You want to go to a Soc tavern or something, or head right to the keep?"
	I think for a moment and say, "Let's try the tavern." Knowing that Cupcake Keep isn't much to look at for the time being.
	Mirae says, "I know of one near here."
	The inside of Fire and Soul is surprisingly active compared to the rest of Durolan. An elf musician plays a catchy tune on his lute, while patrons chit chat over tables nursing pints of ale and water. I take note that the musician is a PC, and wonder if he's performing for a quest or for amusement of the guests. It's probably the latter, you don't make a musician character unless you enjoy playing music in-game.
	He could be a bard type character. Adventurers who use magic through song are great in ten to one-hundred player raids because they often cast buffs to anyone that can hear them. Give that many people plus ten percent strength, for example, and suddenly your group is doing a lot more damage. They can also often inflict debuffs on enemies. If we have, or recruit one to the toxic muffins, I'll be buying him a set of bagpipes before our first epic battle since I doubt there's much for electric guitars and amps on this side of the game. If he's just a soc, he's unlikely to make much of a difference in a real battle.
	We take a seat at an empty table, and a human bar maid by the name of Freya approaches.
	"Something to drink or eat?"
	I say, "Just spring water I think."
	Mirae says, "Me too."
	"You adventurers," she says, "Never try anything interesting from the menu."
	Maybe we'd be more inclined if it didn't all taste like water. I smile and say, "Maybe next time." She curtsies, and I redirect my attention to Mirae. "So how's Tako?"
	A shy smile touches her face, and she looks down into her drink. "He's good." She says, "He has a pet cabbit that goes crazy for treats."
	"What's a cabbit?" I ask.
	"It's like a cross between a cat and a rabbit. It has big hind legs like a rabbit and the forelegs of a cat, and a feline face."
	"How about the ears?" I ask.
	"Big and floppy, but also kind of triangle."
	"Like a fennec fox?"
	Mirae says, "I'm not sure what those look like, but cabbits are adorable. I want one..."
	Until I see a cabbit for myself, I'm going to assume their ears are like a fennec's. I say, "I can't wait to meet all the people you recruited. Good job."
	Mirae says, "It's nothing really. I was just worried about leaving everyone, now most of my friends have come over. A chance at LE quests is nothing to turn your nose up at."
	"How'd they react to the Dragonsbane quest?"
	"Most of them are farming dragons right now, but no luck on the invitation drop. Either it has a low drop chance, or it was special because you were in our group with your LE key and guild leader status."
	I say, "We should go tomorrow if everyone's available."
	Mirae says, "I'll be here all day, and Tako's a daily player. No idea about Laurelade."
	I say, "That's assuming they fix this log-out mess. Pretty soon people are going to have to start using the toilet." I don't know what's going through Mirae's mind, but I guess this isn't something she's worried about since she already has people taking care of her. I feel a little bad for bringing up a reminder of her disability in this virtual world where she can be carefree. Maybe I should be more worried about my own situation.
	I'm about to move in to discussing plans for the tournament when a booming voice sounds from all around, and echoes in the street seeming to come from everywhere and nowhere at once.
	"I greet you." Mirae and I exchange what-the-fuck looks. It's a lazy male voice that gives me the shivers. "Report to the nearest capital city at once. If you are beyond city limits, you'll find a one time use teleportation scroll to take you there in your inventory. You will all see a red mark on your maps denoting the closest meeting location, and should come if you want to know about recent improvements to this world."
	I say, "I have a feeling we're about to get some answers."
	"Why are they announcing patch notes via a character in game?" Mirae says.
	"I don't think they are." I say, "Or, at least the company isn't. We... they... always strive to provide an immersive experience. Going over technical details any time after we've landed in game is counter to that objective."
	"Then if not your employer, who?" Mirae says.
	"That's a very good question."
	I pull out my map and see a red marker in the shape of angel wings on top of the town square. It's an open market where people set up temporary stalls to hawk their goods.
	"You want to go?" Mirae asks.
	"You need to ask?" I say, "This has been bugging me since the hotfix. There's no reason for it to have downloaded so much information while we were logged into the game."
	"Let's go then." Mirae says, "Mind if I use the green bike? I want to look like a pair."
	I realize we don't have drinks yet, so I toss a few coins on the table to compensate the tavern both for our unused drinks, and for taking up space. Most players would just walk out, but the action is a carry over from my role playing days where I try to treat Havenfolk and Freefolk as real people. It doesn't cost anything, and makes the virtual environment feel more like a real place
a home away from home. That might be a more useful habit after the hotfix.
Mirae has invited you to join her party. Accept?
Yes / No
	 I choose yes.
	Outside, I wave my middle finger and call out "INFERNO!!!" to call my fire Elechopper. I use my VT to call up Mirae's earth bike. Now, we're riding in style. A number of other adventurers have vacated the tavern and I can see them admiring our bikes. Under normal circumstances I'd be all about showing off for them, but for now I just want to reach the market and find out what the hell is happening. I take a moment to fire off a message to Frobod whose presumably still at Cupcake Keep.
Melpomine:	Did you hear that?
Frobod:	Yeah. Already on my way.

Frobod:	The blue lights on the wall when we entered. That's how I think of them since it made the place look like a haunted house.

	"Good point," I say. "That gives me hope."

	I shudder imagining how helpless it would feel. The game won't let you materialize inside a physical object, but that doesn't mean a catastrophically failed teleport won't stick you on top of a bell tower, or a boss won't inadvertently knock you on to a tiny inaccessible ledge overlooking an active volcano. "It's a good thing that rarely happens."
	Mirae says, "Even so, I think getting my hands on one of those recall stones just became a higher priority."

The next thing you see is yourself flying above the clouds for a few seconds, and then lightning strikes again at your destination. The Durolan guards, first asked if I was alright, but then wanted to arrest me for it. I guess they're not a fan of 'alien magics' here. That's what they're calling Freeworld spells."
	"Whoa." Mirae laughs, "That sounds awesome. Can you teach me?"
	"How's your martial arts skill?"

	"Not really, but before I came here I was with my old guildies, and after that Tako. Haven't been around many NPCs."
	"Start looking. I think you'll see a difference."
	It's at this time that we pull up to Durolan Town Square, and we're not the first to arrive. At least fifty other players crowd into the area, with indistinct murmurs of "What's going on?" and "I can't log out!" Looks like we're not the only ones to notice. A few people look ready for a boss battle, with their best gear out and swords in hand, but the majority of adventurers just look baffled. It's better than anger.
	It's not too long before we see Frobod, or rather he spots us on our bikes, and waves his hands as he walks over. He says, "Finally, I think we're gonna get answers." with a note of anger in his voice that wasn't there before. I guess now, with an announcement hinting at an end to our imprisonment within reach, some of that uneasiness within him has boiled over into outrage. I don't blame him, by now his girl's party is long over.
	"What's this all about?" I hear a guy yelling in the distance. By now the crowd's more than doubled in size. Not a half bad turn out considering how vacant the streets in this city can be. No one's buying anything from the merchants, who must have been excited to see so many potential customers. The lack of sales eventually stop all the shouting for people to browse their goods.
	Darkness descends on the square. All the ambient mage lights grow fifty percent dimmer. Then, like a hologram, a huge angelic figure appears at the far end of the market over a public fountain. Enormous feathered wings rustle on the apparition's back, and a shaft of white light, as if from a spotlight reflects off immaculate platinum Roman style armor. I try to get a look at its face, but it either doesn't have one or is using some kind of unnatural shadow beneath its cowl to conceal that part of its appearance.
	A silver-grey cloak flaps in non-existent wind as it begins to speak in the same booming voice heard earlier throughout town. "Welcome. I'm sure you've all noticed some upgrades to Freehaven Online."
	Someone in the crowd shouts, "Your software sucks! I want to sign out and go home!" and another responds "Your devs suck donkey dick!" A jolt of righteous indignation snaps through me as another guy echoes "Donkey dicks!" I'm a dev too, and, last I checked, just as stuck as everyone else. It's not my fault we're all stranded here. I hear a few calls of "Yeah!" scattered throughout the crowd, but them there's an undercurrent of people turning to those around them and asking "We can't sign out?" followed by panicked cries of "Oh my god! I can't sign out!" These sentiments sweep through the crown like a tangible force of emotion.
	The angel continues, as if unable to hear the protests. It probably can't. "To start, the Freehaven's logout feature has been disabled."
	This announcement is greeted with a chorus of "Fuck yous!"
	A woman to my right says "Since when has logging out been considered a feature?" and a quieter voice somewhere behind me and to the right says "What is he talking about?"
	Frobod says, "You're telling me this is by design? You can't keep me from my family!" He's growing red in the face. I put a warm hand on his arm for comfort, but it's like I'm not even there. Frobod's harsh face only has eyes for the faceless angel addressing us.
	"Your in-game actions, will now have consequences, and if you want to logout again, you must find quests within Freehaven to unlock this feature, though we hope you will choose not to."
	Nervous chatter. People don't know what to think. The square is a swarm of confusion, fear, and anger. I look to Mirae to see that her index finger's on her chin with a quiet, thoughtful expression. I put a hand on Frobod's arm hoping to calm him, but he doesn't even notice, the only thing he's got eyes for is the faceless winged angel addressing us.
	"Some of you may be thinking you'll be signed out when your loved ones discover you, and physically remove the hardware from your body. This is inadvisable, as Freeworld online is now keeping you alive. Thanks to the update, forceful removal from the game will result in side effects ranging from permanent brain damage to real world death."
	The crowd settles into a shocked silence, each person's gaze transfixed on the angel, or the white faces around them. "On the other hand, the world has been upgraded to provide more realism. In time, you will discover that you are capable of things that were previously impossible. For one, those who seek immortality will be given the option to abandon their flesh and immigrate to Freehaven permanently by digitizing their consciousness. Soon, you will see stations within every capital city to make this possible. New wonders await you. Enjoy your new lives in Freehaven Online."
	The spotlight effect illuminating the figure flickers, and disappears leaving tendrils of aimless white smoke in its wake. Then, as if the smoke were white paper hit with a red stamp, the text "- Archangel" appears, and then dissipates with the vapors. The ambient mason jar mage light lanterns return to their former brightness leaving everyone present stunned and alarmed.
	Frobod stares at the space the Archangel once occupied with a tense jaw, and both hands balled into fists. I stroke is hard flesh beneath his tunic, trying to lead him back to reality. His chest rises, and hands begin to unclench. His voice is dangerous in a way I've not heard from anyone since arriving in Haven, "I want to sit down and go through every aspect of that tournament quest as soon as possible."
	"Excuse me?" I say, I was ready for him to blow up about a lot of things, but our task north of Dragonswood wasn't one of them.
	"You heard him." He says in a clipped tone. "The only way we're getting back short of another hotfix is to unlock it in-game via some unknown quest. We've got an LE quest now, and sounds to me like a fine place to start. The sooner we find one of these hidden quest chains, the sooner we can get the hell out of here."
	"... and back to our families." I add in my mental voice, clarifying what Frobod must be thinking.
	He says, "I'd say we should go tonight, but if the archangel's serious about added realism, a Lord's Manor isn't going to be receiving anyone so late, and it's all about making a good impression, right?"
	"Right..." I say, trailing not having expected either the archangel's announcement, nor Frobod's conviction. If you were to ask me an hour ago, I'd say that Frobod would be wallowing away at Cupcake Keep until the logout was fixed. In a way, I consider the bit about new quests to enable the logout option, I guess it has.
	"What's with the red text in the smoke?" Mirae asks, "You think Archangel's his name?"
	"I don't know." I say, "But we've gotta call him something so why not?"
	"Archangel then..." Frobod says. "I've never been religious."
Chapter 11 - First Night from Home
	Back at headquarters, I'm impressed by how much work Alfred's managed in the time we've been gone. The garden's still overgrown and the painting in the front hall's torn up, but the interior ghost lights have all been replaced by a healthy warm glow from the main hall chandelier, and similar albeit smaller fixtures in neighboring rooms. I idly wonder who the painting was made of, and if it can be repaired. I've mentally changed the name 'living room' to 'lounge' to make Cupcake Keep feel more like a communal lair than a personal residence, and settle into it.
	I send a guild alert.
"Everyone on the quest for the Dragonswood tournament come to Durolan. Meeting at HQ to discuss how to proceed and give out recall stones. Ask a guard for directions to Cupcake Keep."
	Frobod shares the main sofa with me, and Mirae stretches out on one of the loveseats, leaning back on a couple of throw pillows. Elves are short like that, so she manages. Not an empty bottle of wine, nor heap of old laundry is be found. I sniff, and am surprised to find a note of something from the garden, fresh rose if I'm not mistaken, like Alfred's got his hands on a powerful scented candle. Charming touch. I love roses.
	"Nice place." Mirae says.
	"Thanks." I say.
	Mirae says, "I guess this game is about to get real. So it's nice to know we have access to prime real estate."
	"Yeah." I say, "What do you think he meant by that? 'Improvements for realism' and 'Actions have consequences?'"
	"I don't care." Frobod says. "There's a way out, and I'm going to find it if I have to chop off every dragon head on this continent."
	"Good to know where you stand." I say, the force in his voice unsettling me. I'd hoped the ride back from the square would mellow him out a bit. It seems not.
	"The least they could do is give us food that tastes good. God, I'm going to miss it."
	"Have you tried it?" Mirae asks.
	"What?" I say.
	"The food, since the update, I mean. We didn't get our drinks in Fire and Soul, and even then we just had water."

I look around for the human embodiment of Cupcake Keep and call out "Alfred?" using the male name hoping to avoid another incident of flashing boobs. There's a puff of genie smoke, just as I'm thinking, "Please no topless elf woman. Please no topless elf woman." Thankfully, I'm looking at a guy with a thin moustache clad in a black tuxedo.
	"Cool!" Mirae says. "It comes with a butler."

	"As you wish..." The servant bows, and disappears in another puff of smoke, only to reappear moments later with a medium sized cheese platter. I take a cut triangle in my fingers and pop it in my mouth. The texture's right but...

	"Probably not." I say, contemplative. Would I have come knowing we'd be locked inside? Obviously not, but I can't help but see the appeal of living the rest of my days in a game world; no bills, or shitty bosses
no physical injury that can't be put right with the proper incantation.

Alfred's informed me that each time we gain a new guild member, the keep generates new personal quarters for their use. No one can go in or out of your room without your permission."
	Laurelade nods, taking it all in stride, but Mirae's clearly excited. "If we've got to start sleeping in game, people are going to start spending a lot of money on lodging if they haven't bought their own house yet."
