	I confirm, "Yes." Feeling smaller than I've been all day. It's easier to set those feelings aside when I'm surrounded by the guild. The bedroom's quiet, and I hear nothing rustling around in the near empty halls of my castle. It's an absence that threatens to overwhelm me. I turn from the AI and move over to the bed, the fluffy comforter is as soft as a cabbit's coat, and half the weight.
	"For what it's worth," Alfred surprises me, breaking the silence. "I'm sorry for your loss. If you need anything, on castle property, you need only call."
	"Thanks Alfred..." I say, wondering at the sophistication of his AI. Is he as complex as Sal, and if so, for how long?
	He seems aged in a way that I'd not expect for a construct granted awareness on this day, but then Dryslyn and Jenda both acted as if they'd known Jordan long ago. With all these changes, I'm finding it hard to separate authentic learned experience from Havenfolk behavior reacting to data points in my personal log of in-game activities. How are they different from actual memories?
	These are the thoughts stirring in my head as I strip off my adventuring gear, and settle under the covers in nothing but my undergarments. I remove everything manually, rather than with my VT because it feels more real. The mattress is every bit as soft as my beanbag chair back home, which is to say, it makes me feel weightless.
You are in bed.
Would you like to sleep? Yes / No
	It's a prompt I've never seen before. So much for insomnia. I choose yes, and let the world fall to the black of dreamless sleep.
* * * * *
	I wake up, smoothing my hand against the mattress under the covers, and groaning in the way I always do upon awakening. Red canopy, too large bed, an amazing comforter. I'm buried in luxury. I feel at my slightly pointed ears... It's all too real. Sun from an open window greets my face, and while I appreciate the sent of fresh morning air and the sensation of sunlight on my cheeks, part of me hoped that I'd wake up, in my shitty apartment, and the whole mess with archangel would be a dream. Sorry Frobod. So much for automatic logouts. I feel no urge to pee like I usually do in the morning. I wonder if my beanbag's already soaked.
	I groggily get out of bed, a poof of smoke, and Alfred's standing beside me in his tuxedo. "Hey dude. Ball of light form while I'm still in my undies."
	"Master..?"
	"Just do it." There's a poof of smoke and he's back to looking like the bobbing ball of blue energy I first encountered hovering over the sofa.
	"Master, I've already seen you in this state as of last night."
	"I wasn't thinking about that when I went to bed." I say, "You're all creepy and quiet. It's easy to forget you're there." I hastily gather my gear, sniffing my leather top before donning it. It doesn't stink. I'm glad the game doesn't seem to pack the scent of human sweat into our robes and armor. I finish securing my pants, and say, "Okay. You can be Alfred the butler again."
	A puff of smoke, and he's standing beside me with a silver trey in hand, a couple of vials of swirling blue sitting on top of it. "Good morning. I've brought manna potions, master."
	I look them over and wave my hand, "I'm good on manna. Already topped up after the night's sleep." I'm not much of a coffee drinker, tastes like dirty water, but I find myself wishing for a giant mug of Zip Cocoa, a milk-chocolaty drink with enough caffeine per sachet to compare to a triple shot of espresso.
	Alfred says, "Manna potions have a stimulating effect when consumed at full manna."
	"Huh..." I say, "Didn't know that. Hand em' over." I pull the corks off both and chug them down in a single gulp, each vial containing not that much flavorless liquid. I pinch my eyes shut as I feel a bite as they go down. They still taste like water, but it's brisk in a way that sends lances of alert energy through me. "Wow... better than coffee, and my Zip Cocoa."
	"So I've been told, master."
	I slap my cheeks with both hands to make sure I'm wholly awake. "I feel good." I say. "Better than I thought I would."
	I hear Alfred saying "Master..." as I march into the bathroom to get a good look at my face. No groggy goop in the eye, and miraculously my hair doesn't look like it's been hit with a Midwestern tornado. Sleeping in Freehaven rocks. Alfred continues, "I thought I should remind you that last night you wanted to come back to the topic of Lord Zarentu and his tournament."
	"Oh right..." I say, and flop back down on the bed. "What do you know about it?"
	"About the tournament, master?"
	"Anything," I say "About the tourney or Lord Zarentu."
	"I have no additional information, beyond what I've overheard you discussing regarding the tournament."
	"Bummer." I say, "What about the dragon?"
	"Lord Zarentu is a light dragon that is often seen in human form. He's known for being fond of mortals, and is generally seen as a force of good."
	"Good to know." I say, glad that every dragon we encounter won't be trying to eat us. "So..." I hesitate, not knowing how to breach the topic without sounding like a total noob. Then I remember, I'm talking to an NPC; his lifelike demeanor having confused my newly awakened mind. My eyes go to the door, it's closed, and I'm confident our words are private. "With the exception of a few years playing as a dwarven tank, almost all my experience is on the Freeworld side. I'm going to need you to tell me things that most people in Haven take for granted."
	Alfred says, "Yes master." The two word response feels somehow inadequate.
	"Okay... then... Tell me about dragons. Obviously I've fought a few, but I don't know anything about their society. All the one's I've seen in dungeons have been of the kill first, as questions later sort. Makes sense, since it's a dungeon, but you say Zarentu is a force of good. Is he going to be pissed off at us for slaying his cousins?" Alfred opens his mouth to speak, but before he makes a sound I throw in another lightning fast question before I forget, "He is a dude, correct? Lord being gender neutral in Haven, and all that."
	Alfred says, "Lord Zarentu is in fact male."
	I say, "Thought so." and wave my hand for him to go on. Zarentu sounds like it has a penis.
	"Dragons as a race are almost as varied as adventurers," Alfred says, "Most are intelligent, but some are little more than animals."
	I nod, having guessed as much on my own. "Get to the good stuff. Society, diplomacy, and all that jazz."
	"Light and silver dragons are most commonly associated with humans, and often use magic to shape shift into a human form. They are social creatures, who enjoy the company of their own kind in addition to other races."
	"What about the part about holding grudges for killing other dragons."
	"I don't foresee this being a problem. There's no alliance between Zarentu and the dragons of the Windroost, or any other adventurer dungeon that I know of."
	I ask, "So why did we find the invitations there?" The obvious answer is, 'because that's where the devs put it,' but I'm hoping to discover a reason for it within Haven lore.
	"Lord Zarentu often hosts parties and tournaments that are open to all dragons that have neutral or greater reputations with him, as well as mortals who have invitations. Most do not attend, as many dragons are selfish, and dread spending too much time away from their hoards."
	"Any enemies I should know about?"
	"As a light dragon, Zarentu sets himself against most dark dragons, as well as those that are openly evil."
	The immediate statement that comes to mind is 'define evil,' but I think better of asking since the Toxic Muffins have got places to be, and I'm willing to risk the assumption that evil in this world comes down to bad guys that like hurting people, and making the land that much crappier for everyone else.
	Instead I say, "So this makes him an enemy of this extra-planar dragon then?"
	A thoughtful expression crosses Alfred's face before he answers. "Enemy is a strong word. It would be more accurate to say that Zarentu openly opposes the tear manifesting between Haven and the place you call Freeworld. As an extra-planer entity, it's alien to this world, and thus other dragons have no preconceptions of its nature."
	"So, the 'extra planar dragon' in the quest, is a creature from another reality or universe?"
	"Correct." Alfred says.
	I'm not sure what to make of that, since Freeworld doesn't have dragons in the way that Haven does. Shape changing were-lizards yes, but nothing the likes of Raika and Tilashia. I wonder if the devs are planning a smaller third world between Haven and Freeworld to bridge the two spaces.
	I say, "How are the other dragons reacting?"
	Alfred responds, "It's varied, though most agree that anything ripping a hole in the universe itself is undesirable. A few see it as an opportunity for new conquest, and a chance to add unknown treasures their hoards. Fewer still welcome the tear on the off chance that it might destroy our world."
	"Who would want that?" I say, genuinely shocked. "If the world dies, so do they. Right?"
	"Death cultists in service to dark gods. They think bringing about the end of the world will bring them favor in the afterlife. Haven has many religions."
	"Wackos." I say, and mentally remind myself that it's just a game. Something about going to sleep, and waking up a little over eight hours later makes this place more real. If Freehaven's not home, it certainly feels more like a genuine place knowing that I can't just log out at any moment. It means, that before too long, non-adventurers are going to start seeming a whole lot more like real people.
	"You could say that." Alfred says.
	As cool as I think it would be to obtain the Dragonslayer title, part of me worries that it'll have a negative effect on the good dragons. I begin spinning ways to rationalize the title in the back of my head if it somehow comes up with Zarentu. Given our current predicament, I plan to say it's a reward for keeping the world safe from hostile dragons. He has to know how bloodthirsty his kin can be.
	"Thanks for the info." I say. "This is helpful."
	I stand and check myself in the mirror once more, still good hair, and no eye gunk, and wonder if I'll have the chance to grow nostalgic for bed head. Sure, it's a pain to deal with, but can be pretty funny on other people. I'm about to leave the master bedchamber when Alfred clears his throat.
	"One more thing, Master." I quirk an eyebrow in invitation for him to go on. "Do mind your manners. While Lord Zarentu is known as one of the good dragons, he's just as proud as any of the others and will expect to be treated respectfully. I would hate to see any of you disqualified from the competition before it begins."
	"That can happen?" I ask, having never heard of an NPC discontinuing a quest over bad manners.
	Alfred says, "Not yet but... after the update, it's a possibility."
	"Noted." I say, instantly thinking of TakoStar's antics, and the way Laurelade rubs people the wrong way. "I'll be sure to tell everyone."
Chapter 12 - 18,631 and North of Dragonswood
	I find everyone in the lounge. It doesn't take long because the sound of television carries against the hard floors and stone walls. It seems I'm the last person out of bed.
	"You didn't tell me this place had TV." Mirae says with mock scolding and amusement in her voice.
	"Yeah." I said, "It's a paid upgrade that lets you view content that's available on public networks, and streaming websites."
	Frobod shushes us as the screen cuts to an anchorwoman in a black blouse sitting behind a news desk. In the top right corner of the screen is the new logo for Freehaven Online.
	"So far, six individuals are confirmed dead after family members manually attempted to remove Freehaven game hardware from their bodies. We implore you not to remove any of the game's equipment from a person who is signed in, as it may result in death or brain damage. As we continue to get updates on Archangel and Freehaven Online, we now know that as many as 18,631 individuals worldwide may have been trapped in the popular virtual reality game."
	Frobod's eyes are glued to the screen, but Tako, Laurelade, and I exchange troubled looks. I also make eye contact with Mirae, but she's less concerned than everyone else. I think, "Does she want to die?" and then consider that so long as someone's taking care of her body, she's probably happy for the excuse to stay here. I, for one, don't have that luxury, but at least I know no one will be trying to remove my visor.
	"If one of your friends or family is currently logged in, we urge you to call the number at the bottom of your screen and report them. Locations are being set up for emergency support, and trained professionals will be sent for their care and/or transport to medical facilities."
	"That's a lot of people." I say.
	"No shit..." Laurelade says.
	"At least they're on top of this." I say. "I'd hate to find out they just started rallying support when people were dying."
	"People are dying." Laurelade says.
	I say, "I meant from starvation or dehydration, but yeah... that might have been what kicked their butts into gear."
	The newscaster says, "A hacking organization known as Archangel is taking credit. Authorities are following leads to uncover the identities of its members...Next up, trouble in the White House as the President.."
	Frobod clicks the remote, and the holographic TV vanishes. He says, "They're not making any progress. It's been the same all morning."
	Tako says, "18,631...." his voice is more serious than normal.
	"I agree, it's a lot." I say, "...but at the same time not that much. That's less than a quarter of the people that'd fit inside a stadium."
	"I guess..." Tako says.
	"We're all still here, and that's what matters. We're alive, and with all the news coverage, we're likely to stay that way." I pause, taking in everyone's sullen expressions, and add "I'll bet they have people and AIs calling every phone number associated with your account to give warning."
	"I just..." Laurelade says, "I can't even. This is supposed to be fun. Not a struggle against real world death."
	"It still is fun." Mirae says. "All that's changed, that we know of, is that we can't sign out."
	"She's right." I say. "... and in any case there's nothing we can do here to change anything."
	"You're wrong." Frobod says.
	"Huh?" Mirae asks.
	"Melpomine's wrong." Frobod says, "There is something we can do from here."
	"What's that?" Laurelade says with a quiver in her voice. It's hard to believe she's the same arrogant gnome we recruited to farm the Windroost. That quiver paired with the way she tucks her hands into her sleeves, and holds her arms close to her body, tells me she's taking this the hardest.
	"Archangel said there are quests to be discovered," he pauses for dramatic effect, and then gestures toward the front of the castle, "Out there, to re-enable the logout function. We can either stay here and wait for people in the real world to find a solution, or we can go out there," He motions again. "and find our own."
	Laurelade sighs, but speaks no words.
	I nod and say, "I couldn't have said it better myself." Tako nods along with me.
	"There's another option." Mirae said. "Archangel also said that we can stay here permanently, via digitization."
	"Fuck you for even thinking that." Frobod says.
	My skin grows cold, I don't like the harsh language coming out of his mouth. It puts a bad stain, on Mr. Frobod's nice guy demeanor. Mirae's eyes widen as if afraid of being attacked.
	In a less confident voice she says, "It's an option."
	"It's an option" Frobod says, "An option that leaves all your friends, and your whole family alone forever, without even a chance to say goodbye."
	"To be honest," Mirae says, "My friends are all already here."
	"Then your family." Frobod says, "Don't be so fucking selfish."
	Something about the word "selfish" pushes Mirae's lips into a thin tight line. It's an argument I don't want to be caught up in, because I can see both sides. Having lost my brother, I know first hand what it's like to grieve for a family member, on the other... whose being more selfish? Mirae for wanting to take on a digital existence leaving the physical world behind, or her family keeping her trapped in a broken body, when there might be an option where her mind can thrive. I mean... it's not really death if the person's consciousness survives, is it?
	"I.. uh.." I break in, not knowing how to de-escalate. "Yeah, well we don't even know if that's possible. I've never heard of someone's mind being uploaded into a game before."
	"The idea and technology's been around for ages, but it hasn't been released to the public." Mirae says. "Trust me, I know."
	I think, "She would."
	Mirae continues, "Everything Archangel's said has been true so far, so I have no reason to doubt him. Just look at the news. It shouldn't be possible to kill someone by removing a visor, or whatever device you use to connect, but already six people have died. That's gotta tell you something."
	Laurelade shifts uncomfortably, "I'm not quite ready to give upon the real world."
	"I think we all aren't." Tako says.
	Frobod says, "None of us should be."
	Mirae has a flustered look on her face as everyone gangs up on her. She says, "I'm just saying that it's possible. It's all possible, and thus digitization is an option."
	"Only if you don't give a shit about everyone around you." Frobod mutters.
	"Peace..." I say raising placating hands to both of them. "Let's just worry about the quest now. Frobod's right. Finding a logout quest is an option, and until those digitization stations start popping up, the only one we can pursue."
	"Fine." Frobod says as if annoyed that there's even a need for peace.
	I say, "I only sort of know the way."
	"I know how to get there." Frobod says.
	"Me too." says Mirae.
	"Then I'll follow you."
	Outside we discover a beautiful day filled with the scents of clean air, and flowers from the overgrown garden. I wonder how long it'll take Alfred to reign in. We all stand at the foot of the drawbridge as if waiting for something to happen. On one side there are peaks of yellows, reds, and impossible blues all tangled in wild snarls of green. On the other we have grey stone, against the earthen tones of training; combat dummies in burlap sacks. I imagine how it'll all look once restored. The atmosphere is... too nice, with so much possibility. How can we worry about death and virtual imprisonment, on a day where the very air smells of life and freedom?
	"INFERNO!!!" I wave my middle finger in the air. The fire Elechopper pops into existence with its satisfying monster purr. I ask, "Everyone else want a bike?" but the question's rhetorical because I've got my VT in hand and am already summoning. The bikes still feel like a symbol of our guild so long as we're the only ones that have them, and now more than ever, our guild will serve as a surrogate family until we're back in the real world. For that reason, it feels good to see everyone claiming their ride without asking which is theirs. Each person straddles the same bike they took to our first guild dungeon.
	It seems I'm the only one that's shaky on directions, because instead of Mirae or Frobod, Tako takes point and lifts off. We fly in a triangular formation like racked up pool balls at the start of a game with our bikes sprinkling harmless bits of their elements on the ground beneath us. There's scarcely a time you can feel more alive, than when you're moving at six hundred and fifty percent running speed with whips of wind hammering your face.
	Mirae's all smiles, and I can tell that we have this in common. In another life, before the dawn of virtual reality, she and I'd have made kick ass race car drivers in the physical world. I can't see Tako's face, because he's in front of me, but the gnome is gloomy, blas
 and impervious to the excitement caused by our means of travel.
	"You excited?" I say, using party chat, so the others can hear. I'm looking at Frobod, and he looks like a hero in his black plate, and greatsword strapped to his back. I want to put him on a movie poster.
	"Yeah." He says, in a lighter tone than he used at the keep. "It's good to be finally doing something."
	"That's what I like to hear." The outline of Dragonswood Keep looms on the horizon, and I find myself thankful for our flying mounts. We're all focused on getting to the next stage of our quest, and don't want to deal with killing pointless mobs in transit. I ask, "Do you think there will be other adventurers?"
	Frobod shrugs, and begins his descent behind Tako with the others. He says, "It says PvP and PvE, so who knows?"
	Our Elechoppers touch down on the dirt road, we're about fifty yards from the entrance and Zarentu's guards.
	"Hold up." I say. Tako stops, and looks over his shoulder. The others are looking at me as well. "With all the weird stuff happening after the update, I think we should behave."
	"What do you mean?" Tako says.
	"Just... Treat the Havenfolk like they're real people. They're acting different from before."
	"You mean like role-play n' shit?" Tako asks.
	"I..." I pause, at a momentary loss for words given that I in fact used to be a hardcore role player. "I don't think you need to do accents and all that. Just, treat people like you would in the real world. If someone looks important in Dragonswood Keep, treat them with the respect that you would treat a real person in a position of power."
	Tako rolls his eyes. I'd expected Laurelade to chime in with some comment to the effect of 'They're just NPCs" but she's still off her game, so she shrugs and says "Whatever."
	"I'm serious. I was talking to Alfred this morning, and he thinks there's a chance that we could get kicked off this quest if we misbehave. Dragons are proud, don't scratch their egos, and that kind of thing."
	"Never heard of getting thrown off a quest, for not role playing." Tako says.
	"I know." I say, "But now things are different."
	He says, "Can't argue with that."
	"Just... Don't be savages. Okay? I don't want to lose this quest."
	Tako bows deep, in an impression of Alfred from Cupcake Keep. "Yes, master. Don't lose the quest master."
	I give him a flat look. "You know what I mean."
	We approach the gate on our bikes looking like a handful of actors yanked off the set of Lord of the Rings, and making their way back to town for some grub. The Dragonswood guards, both named NPCs, Lyle and Archibald, move to intercept us, crossing wicked halberds in the road like a bladed parking gate. Archibald has bushy blonde eyebrows, and a walrus moustache more robust than Jardis back at the Smiling Otter. It's a common enough style amongst Havenfolk that I find myself picking up on subtle differences in facial hairstyles. Both guards have egg-shaped helmets that conceal the tops of their heads.
	"Halt!" Archibald says, with a comically pompous note in his voice. "State your business."
	Mirae says, "We're here for the tournament. Sir."
	"Are you dragons?" He asks, giving us skeptical looks from under his conical helm.
	"No?" I say, the word sounding more like a question than I'd intended.
	"We're not." Mirae says, in voice more confident than my own.
	"Invitations?" He says.
	We all fish them out of our inventories, and hold them out for him to receive. He takes Mirae's first, and unrolls it murmuring "Yes... yes... I see... Indeed." as he reads. It must be an act for God knows what reason, given the sheer number of these he must see.
	Lyle is eyeing our bikes, as if wondering what planet they came from. He's a wiry man with a round head and a clean shaven face. As Archibald finishes with Mirae's scroll, Lyle whispers "Their steeds sir." Though the tone of his voice makes it sound as though he'd asked "That's a steed?"
	Archibald waves him off, and says "Yes, yes.. I know. Everything is in order here."
	Lyle whines something that sounds like, "Alien magics."
	"But of course! These are seasoned adventurers. Of course they have treasures from far off lands. How else would they have invitations?"
	Does he protest too much?
	"I assure you," I say, "Our mounts are harmless. You're companion's right. They're from a far off land."
	Archibald says, "No one here's afraid of your ... your... mounta-wha-cha-dood-it."
	"They're called Elechoppers." I pause trying to think of a way to explain them to a high fantasy character. "Metal carts powered by elemental spirits."
	"I know that." Archibald snaps, and then turns to the uneasy looking Lyle. "They're just ele-chood-its."
	"Elechoppers." I say.
	"Yes, Elechoppers. Please come in."

	The courtyard beyond the gate is decorated for a festival. Merchants sell wedges of watermelon, and burly smiths show off portable racks covered in shields and arms. Somewhere ahead a minstrel plays a jaunty tune, and I see Havenfolk dancing, arm in arm with colored ribbons in their hair.
	"Where do we go?" I ask.
	Frobod points the opposite direction of the dancers. "Over there." There's a bright red and white banner with images of combatants sparring with shield and sword. I appreciate the way the game finds ways to convey meaning without the use of written or spoken language.
	"Registration?" I ask.
	Frobod says, "Looks like it."

	We respond, "Melpomine."
	"Frobod."
	"Mirae."
	"Tako."

Each invitation entitles you to join one solo event, and the melee. Winners take home a sack of gold, and may ask a favor of the keep."

	Mirae says, "Archery."

	"Looks like we might face off." I say to Tako, grinning. "No hard feelings when I kick your butt."

	I ask, "Augmentation spells?"
	"Buffs," Yrvril says, as if hating the word. "as you adventurers call them."
	"Ah..." I say, and make a point to go through Jordan's spell list to find those dexterity and vitality buffs that I've become so acquainted with.
	Yrvril says, "None of you will be killed while competing in the tourney, but you can be injured." For the first time he smiles. "With some of the spells I've seen over the years, you may find yourself wishing for death."
	As soon as he says it, a buff appears in the top right hand corner of my vision.
Champion
Cannot be reduced to zero health.
Duration:
Until the end of Lord Zarentu's tournament.
Only active on the grounds of Dragonswood Keep.
	We're all at full health, so we forego searching for the healers, but we do take the opportunity to scope out the grounds. My stomach growls. It surprises me so much that I stop walking. The rest of the group moves a few paces before realizing I'm lagging behind.
	"Dude..." I say. "My stomach's growling."
	Everyone else stops, and looks back to me.
	"I didn't notice before," Mirae says, "But now that you mention it I'm hungry too."
	Tako says, "Damn... Now I'm feeling it."
	"We better eat something." Frobod says. "If this game's really all about the realism now, I don't want to fight on an empty stomach."
	Laurelade says, "There's probably an inn."
	"Ugh! But the flavor. How much tasteless crap am I going to have to consume to feel full?"
	"I hate you." Mirae says, having been reminded of the shortcomings of Haven cuisine.
	"This is gonna suck." Tako says.
	Frobod says, "First lets find out how long we have until the first contest. I don't want to miss it. Then we'll find out where to eat."
	The rumble in my stomach makes eating seem like the higher priority, but I can't fault Frobod's logic. He's first in the joust, and I know I'd kick myself if I missed the chance to compete on account of stuffing my face if I were in his position. I see a human girl with bright red hair and a green dress, the contrast between the two colors reminds me of a Christmas tree.
	I try to snag her attention.
	"Excuse me, Miss?" Her name's Ava, according to her name plate. We're a few paces away and she points her finger at her chest as if to say "Who me?" when I address her. I give her a nod, which makes her light up with a smile as she approaches.
	"So nice to be talking to a Champion." She looks to Frobod and then to Tako, "...or dare I say a lot of them? How may I help you?"
	"I .. uhh.. errr..." The sudden celebrity treatment catches me off guard; the Champion buff having unintended consequences. "Thank you. We were just wondering how much time we have before the joust starts, and the other events after that."
	"The joust? Ye got some time. It's not for another couple of hours, and some minutes. After the joust, each events starts on the hour after that."
	"Thanks." I say, "Can you tell us where the contests take place?"
	"Oh." Ava says, "It's just over yonder." She waves her hand. "Ye can't miss it with all the banners. Just a couple minutes walk from here."
	"Thank you... Ava." I say, she doesn't seem to notice that I know her name without her having told me.
	She curtsies and says, "Glad to help."
	Tako says, "Where can we get some food? We're starving."
	Ava says, "Well, the feast won't be 'till after tourney, so if you're wanting some food before then you'll have to fend for yourself."
	"There's no inn?" Frobod asks.
	"I guess ye could go down into the village, but this keep is all Lord Zarentu's property. We don't have kitchens open to the public."
	"I see." Frobod says, his voice sounding stoic.
	"I've got a few recipes if we can get the ingredients. In fact," Mirae checks her VT, "I just need the meat. I can cook hares, hens, boar and pheasants."
	Ava says, "Ye should be able to find the birds and rabbits in the woods outside here. Normally, I'd suggest going to the kitchen, but I hear they're swamped preparing for the feast this evening."
	"Thanks..." I say. "We'll do that. You've helped a lot."
	Ava curtsies again, "Always glad to be of service to Champions like yourselves."
	I look to the others. "Looks like we're going hunting. Think you can manage?"
	Mirae says, "I think we'll manage."
	Frobod says, "Should be easy game."
Chapter 13 - Tastes Like Butt Crack
	Archibald says, "Enjoy your ride on your Elechopits!" as we head back out the gate.
	I mutter "Elechopper," but I don't think he hears me. In any case, we don't mount up because our destination is the forest which is within line of sight of the keep. 	Once upon a time, gamers let real life experiences pass them by as they stared at computer monitors and TV screens for hours at the time. It now strikes me as funny that online gaming now opens people up to quasi-physical experiences that they might not have otherwise had.
	We're moving along a dirt path that's wide enough for three of us to walk side by side. It winds its way through green trees that smell like damp earth and fallen leaves. It makes me feel calm and alert. I hear scattered birdsong and critters rustling above us.

	The stable is a curious place with at least a dozen waiting animals. Most are horses, but there's also demons like the Nightmare, riding tigers, a broom, and a crazy shark looking thing with four eyes, a dorsal fin, and powerful hairless legs that remind me of a great cat.

	 Frobod sits on the seat and asks, "What do I do?"
	"For now, nothing. Just sit." Aztram murmurs syllables in an indiscernible language, and tendrils of white magic rise from his finger tips. He moves around the bike as he chants, waving his glowing fingers all around it, much the same as I'd imagine a person in the real world trying to bathe in the smoke of incense sticks. Now, standing in front of the headlight, he makes a four fingered 'come here' motion with his hand. Each time he does, the bike grows a little taller. He checks from another angle, and realizes that Frobod's sitting a bit higher than the other mounts. He makes a pushing motion, and the bike gets smaller. 'Come here' and the height of the seat creeps up again.
	"There." The mage says. "How does that feel?"
	Frobod looks down, "Surprisingly good. I'm a lot farther off the ground, but the physical sensation is about the same as before."
	I say, "That's magic for you."
	"As it should be." Aztram says.
	Frobod asks, "What does it look like?"
	"Bigger wheels, bigger everything. It's strange to see." Looking down at the other mounts, I realize that while their heads may be at different heights, the saddles on each are all on a level.
	Frobod asks, "Will this change the way it handles?"
	Aztram shakes his head no. "It'll all feel exactly the same, because, so far as you're concerned, nothing has changed."
	Frobod says, "I don't get it."
	I say, "Dude... Sometimes you've gotta let shit slide. It all works out because..."
	Frobod finishes the sentence. "Magic."
	Aztram says, "Not many adventurers take the time to learn this kind of magic. Most of your lot are so concerned with blowing things up. There's far more to the art than destruction." He sighs, as if knowing his words are falling on deaf ears.
	"Well," I say, "That's usually what's most useful in dungeons, but don't give up. I have a feeling you're going to have a lot more students looking to expand their skill set in the coming months."
	"You're very kind." Aztram says, "But I'm old enough now that I won't believe it until I see it."
	"Are you a teacher?" I ask.
	"Oh, I suppose I could be persuaded to take on an apprentice, if the right individual came along."

Ashtara by contrast falls, and nearly hits the ground. By some miracle, her hand whips out in a wild grasp, and snatches the pummel just in time. A full shatter, the crowd cheers at the destruction but it's short lived, as the dragon leaves her saddle and plummets to the earth.
	Frobod tosses what remains of his lance aside, and pumps his fist in the air as the victor. Havenfolk chant, "Frobod! Frobod! Frobod!" in celebration. They're all excited save for a weasel faced man in a top hat pushing through to the exit.
	In party chat I say, "Sleeve's making a run for it." and then "Stop him!" in the local channel for all to hear. I point an accusing finger so that there can be no doubt of my meaning.
	Zarentu says, "Well done Frobod of the Toxic Muffins! You have fought well."
	Frobod remembering my advice about showing deference to important dragons takes the knee and says, "Thank you, m'lord. If I may ask one favor."
	Zarentu says, "Rewards will be granted at the feast."
	"Yes, m'lord, but it seems one of your subjects is breaking the law. As we speak, the human named Sleeve is making a mad dash away with all our money."
	"Is that so?" Zarentu says. "Do you have proof?"
	Frobod says, "If you follow him, I think you'll find it." Then, switching to party chat so Havenfolk can't hear he says, "You better be right. In case you didn't notice, when it comes to false accusations and Zarentu, they don't fuck around."
	Zarentu says, "As champion of this event, you are granted one boon. Should your accusations prove well founded, I offer my thanks, and then another." He doesn't say what'll happen if Frobod's accusation goes unfounded. I doubt it'll be pleasant.
	"I understand, Sir."
	The inclusion of 'Sir' strikes me as a bit military, but the NPCs don't note the change in vocabulary. The most important part of the interaction seems to be that an attempt was made at respect.
	"Very well." Zarentu says. "Dragonswood Guards, seize the gambler's tent, and bring Sleeve to me. Lord Frobod, you'll be summoned later with the results of the investigation. Please mount your Elechopper, and choose your partner."
	Frobod gets back on his bike, but has a confused expression on his face. Sten fast approaches from the side with a lance in one hand, and a circlet made of yellow rose blossoms and green leaves in the other. They exchange words that I can't make out, and Frobod dips the tip of his lance to Sten who hangs the wreath from its tip.
	In party chat I hear Frobod say, "This is so cheesy."
	I look to Mirae and say "What?" She has a smile on her face.
	 Frobod makes a slow lap around the perimeter with his lance in hand, his head turned to the audience, searching faces. Some ladies spread their arms, as if to catch a wedding bouquet. Their shoulders slump, and their faces grow pouty as the death knight passes.
	"Oh God..." I think I know what's happening. We're in the last stand from where he started, and Frobod doesn't pause until he's right in front of us. He makes a show of searching faces, my cheeks grow hot. This isn't what I had in mind.
	Frobod extends the lance so that the rose wreath is in my face.
	Herald Brime says, "Will she accept?"
	Dear God, this is mortifying.
	The crowd begins to chant, "Take it! Take it! Take it! Take it!"
	I'm frozen solid, so I don't initially accept.
	Brime says, "Does she reject?" and the crowd reacts with a resounding "Boooooooo!"
	I quickly snatch up the wreath before I can grow any redder, and smash it on my head. It's a shame it's not a hood to hide my embarrassment.
You have received Rose Ring of the Champion.

	As soon as it touches my head, the crowd erupts in gleeful applause and chatter. I may be Lord Melpomine, but for now I'm feeling like quite the Lady.
Chapter 15 - Eight Whole Chickens
	We've got some time before the next event, so we hit the tents that have been set up for the festival. Laurelade slips off to do Laurelade things, which leaves me, Mirae, Tako, and Frobod. I'm wearing the Rose Ring of the Champion because... Why not?
	"So what was all that about?" I ask, referring to the ring on my head.
	"There's a performance before the feast, where all the winners dance." Frobod says.
	"That explains the stats." I say.
	"The wreath has stats?" Frobod asks.
	"Nothing super. Just a bump to charisma and appearance, two levels of dancing, and a temporary rep bonus."
	"Still..." Frobod says, "It's more than I thought."
	"I want to cook some more food." Tako says.
	I ask, "Do you know how?"
	"Eh..." Tako says, "Not really unless they've added Microwaves."
	"Didn't think so." I say.
	"Hey! I could be a chef! You never know!" Tako says.
	"You just said that you weren't." I say.
	"Yeah, but I could be!" Tako says, "Don't judge a book by its cover, girl, not cool." He speaks with such intensity that I could believe he's serious, but then his lips break into a smile and he says. "I'm just fucking with ya. I wouldn't trust me with your food either."
	I say, "Maybe we should recruit one... A real world chef I mean. From what I've seen from your experiment, cooking the hare manually instead of using the cooking skill in the UI made it taste about like you'd expect under the circumstances. Maybe cooking like we do in the real world will result in real tasting food."
	"Like char broiled butt crack." Tako says.
	"Something like that." Mirae says. "Actually, I think I know a guy."
	"Oh?" I say.
	"Yeah, he's like me. One of the people I recruited. He said he was a professional chef a few years ago."
	Frobod asks, "Why did he stop?"
	Mirae says, "That's personal."
	Frobod says, "Whatever."
	I decide to shift the conversation away from Mirae and this other person's incapacitated status. "Can you send him a message?"
	Mirae, looking down at her VT, says "Already on it. His in game name is Pehrom by the way. He's one of the triplets" I raise and inquisitive eyebrow, and she clarifies. "You'll understand when you meet them. Three goblin characters that are almost identical."
	"A goblin chef." Tako says, "That's dangerous."
	Mirae says, "I don't even think he has the in-game cooking skill. Something about the lack of taste being an affront to his training."
	"Sounds like a chef to me." Frobod says. I snicker.
	"Oh, good." Mirae says. "He says they've been farming the Windroost, but after this run he'll head over to HQ and see what he can do."
	I say, "Tell him to tell Alfred that I said it's okay to raid the kitchen."
	"Dude." Tako says, "If this works out, our guild will be famous."
	Mirae's talk of the Windroost makes me wonder about our guild's progress on the Dragonsbane quest. While we five are over here working on one objective, it's easy to forget that we have a massive number of dragons to kill.
Limited Edition - Guild Quest

	"Nice!" I say, "Check out the Dragonsbane quest." They all poke around in their VTs. "Looks like the new guys are making some progress."
	Mirae says, "Most of the people I recruited have been farming. There are a lot of little dragons in that dungeon."
	"What we need is a dragon raid." Frobod says, "An epic battle with all kinds of opponents."
	"We might have enough people for a small one." Tako says. "But we should get over a hundred guild members for the kind of battle you're talking about."
	"For now," Mirae says. "I think the Windroost is enough."
	We're coming around a bend in the maze of Havenfolk vendors and tents when I see a collection of four guards standing around Sleeve's tent. The bookie's nowhere to be found, but the red "Closed for bets" sign is still in place.
	I say, "I'm going to go see about my winnings."
	Frobod says, "I'll join ya."
	Mirae says, "We'll be around, or at the arena since I'm in the next competition." She looks to Tako, "Let's go."
	As I approach, one of the guards, in the same plate and conical helms as Lyle and Archibald, says "Closed for bets. There won't be any more betting here this tourney."
	I say, "I'm not making a bet." and fish my receipt from my inventory. "I'm here to collect my winnings."
	The guard says, "Let me see that." and takes the paper from me. "Four thousand gold. That's some nice profit."
	Frobod says, "I have a bet too, but it's not ready to collect yet. Where's Sleeve?"
	"For that, you'll need to go to the keep proper, and speak with Lord Zarentu. We've been given instructions to send you his direction. Do you need an escort?"
	I say, "I think a mark on our maps will do fine, thank you."
	"Very well then." We both hand over our maps, and he speaks while making a red mark that magically appears with a touch of his finger. "You'll find the Lord over here. He'll be expecting you."
	"Thank you." I say, and look to Frobod. "Shall we go?"
	"You're most welcome, Lady Lord Melpomine, and might I say you're as lovely as a half elven lass ever was."
	"Err... Thanks." I say, not sure what to make of the compliment. I'm not a soc, so I identify more as a warrior. The guard beams in spite of my awkward response. It motivates me to break extra heads in the arena.
	We walk over a draw bridge, which is much like my own, and find a castle with abundant natural lighting, and bobbing white mage lights in crannies where the sun can't shine. Banners hang from the rafters in Zarentu's gold and white house colors. There are numerous tapestries on the wall depicting great battles, and great dragons. One in particular features a valiant white drake coiled around a woman in elven form. I wonder if the creature is Zarentu, and if so, whether the woman is a dragon. Who is she?
	We find Zarentu eating by himself at the head of a long banquet table in his dining hall, though two silent bodyguards are stationed in the corner. They're so still, and so quiet that they seem more like furniture than sentient beings. I count six whole chickens, and two decimated carcasses on ceramic plates. All for him. My God, dragons eat a lot.
	He says, "Lord Frobod, Lord Melpomine. Good of you to join me." He doesn't stand, in fact, he sucks juicy meat off a chicken bone in between his statements. "I've good news. Lord Frobod was correct in his accusations of the human Sleeve. My guards caught him, and his ogres hastily loading up a cart upon apprehension. So long as you can produce receipts for your bets that correlate with his ledger we shall consider the matter settled, and Lord Frobod will be granted another boon."
	I say, "Thank you, m'lord." We fish our receipts out from our inventories. He sucks chicken juice off his fingers, and motions one of the stoic, leather clad body guards from the corner to retrieve the documents. Given all the finger sucking, the lord of Dragonswood Keep is a lot more informal than I'd imagined.
	Zarentu looks the papers over on the table beside his plate without actually touching them, and nods. "We'll have these sums drawn from the coffers, as soon as you're ready."
	"What'll happen to Sleeve?" I ask.
	"That depends entirely on how much gold he has in those trunks." Zarentu says. "If it's enough to cover his bets, he'll be looking at a few months in the stockade. If he overextended himself, he'll be given to indentured servitude until he pays what he owes." Zarentu pauses, as he shuffles his current plate to his left, and brings a fresh whole roasted chicken in front of him. "I've been quite rude. May I offer some refreshment? A hen? A honey ham?"
	"We appreciate the offer," Frobod says, "But we've already eaten."
	The dragon shrugs, as if it's of no consequence to him. "At some point I'd like to discuss your marvelous mounts. Wherever did you get them?"
	Frobod and I exchange meaningful looks. I hesitate, spinning a mental tale of a land far, far away, but then stop and settle on the truth. He's going to find out some day, and I'd rather not be caught lying by omission when he does. "You're familiar with the Rip of Worlds, yes?"
	"I am." Zarentu says, his voice becoming dangerous. "What about it?"
	I say, "The Elechoppers are from the other side."
	"So you've crossed over." He says.
	"You could say that. I've spent more time over there than Frobod has."
	Zarentu says, "What do you know about the cause?"
	"Not much." I say, honestly, at least from the perspective of a person living in Freehaven, and not a dev. "There's a dragon from another world causing it, but it doesn't come from Freeworld."
	"Freeworld?" Zarentu asks.
	I say, "That's what they call the place on the other side of the rip, and the people who live there are the Freefolk."
	"What makes you so sure?" the dragon asks.
	I say, "Freeworld doesn't have dragons, or at least, not like you do here. There are people who can transform into hybrid creatures, a bit like Nothgoran, but there are no large dragons on par with mounts, much less the size of a castle."
	Outside, a horn blows.
	Frobod says, "Sounds like Archery's starting."
	Zarentu corrects, "Nothgoran is a black drake, that assumes a semi-human form. He is not human. As for the contest, we have a few minutes. "
	"Ah, right." I say, thinking I've needled his draconic pride. "I just mean that Freeworld has people who can change into animals, and hybrid human shapes."
	"Weres?" Zarentu asks.
	"Eh...Kind of..." I say, "That's what a lot of people call them, but they aren't like the weres here."
	"How so?" The dragon asks.
	Frobod says, "It's not a disease."
	"Yeah," I say, "...and they still have their minds when they transform. Most have a short temper, but they're not mindless killing machines like the ones here. They're born, with a few exceptions, and even have their own cultures."
	He polishes off another chicken, before reaching for the next. Amazing. I hadn't noticed him eating, or at least not enough to account for the consumption of an entire bird. It must be a dragon talent.
	Lord Zarentu says, "This is all very interesting, but alas we are short on time. Lord Melpomine, after the games, I'd like to speak with you more about this Freeworld and its marvels. I don't know if what is already torn can be mended, but either way, I want to face this new world with knowledge. Right now, you two are the only source of that knowledge I have."
	I say, "Some other adventurers may have information, but I agree. I'm likely the closest thing Haven has right now to a scholar."
	Zarentu stops chewing for a moment and narrows his eyes. His voice taking on the dangerous tone it had upon first mentioning the Rip of Worlds. "It would be most unfortunate to learn that any of my champions played a role in causing it."
	"We haven't." I'm quick to add. "We don't want to see Haven destroyed anymore than you do, and we're investigating the cause."
	"If this is true, you have nothing to fear, and I welcome you on my lands." Zarentu says, "Let us hope that it is so. If you'll excuse me, I'd like to quickly finish, and proceed to the arena." He motions with his hand, and the same bodyguard as before returns our gambling receipts. "Present these to my clerk and you'll be paid."
Chapter 16 - Single Combat
	I leave the keep with a spring in my step, it feels good to be paid. I don't even know what I want to spend the gold on, but it's good knowing that if I want it I can probably buy it. The archery and magic challenges, are less interesting than the joust, since they both basically come down to individuals trying to hit a target at range. I'd like to say Mirae and Laurelade brought home first place for the Toxic Muffins, but at the end of their challenges, Mirae places third, and Laurelade seventh. We're milling about the grounds waiting for single combat to start.
	"I'm bummed that I won't get a boon from Zarentu, though." Mirae says.
	Frobod asks, "What would you use it on if you had it?"

She did a fine job zapping, freezing, and blowing things up, but when it came to the part where she had to quickly melt specific candles, in a cluster of twelve without damaging any of the others, she fell apart. In the first attempt, two candles next to the target were destroyed, and in the next it took her so long to get the job done that she lost points.

	I get the feeling that if she had to choose between us, she'd prefer Tako, if for no other reason than to get a wreath, but I'm not complaining. I'm not going to get jealous of my officer's crush. I say, "Thanks." to Mirae, and then to Tako "Shall we?"
	"Let's." he says.
	We all kneel before Lord Zarentu, and promise to conduct ourselves honorably. Now that I know that he doesn't consider Freeworld content dishonorable, I have no reservations about accessing my full array of spells and abilities. After the opening ceremony, we're escorted to the Champion's Lounge where we're informed that dragons are not allowed to take their native shapes, and now is the time to make pre-battle preparations.
	It's a different feeling waiting in the lounge than in the stands because we can't tell what's happening. Of course we hear the crowd's "Ooohs!", crashes of steel, and "Ahhs!" but it doesn't tell us anything about our opponents' tactics. As for our surroundings, it's every bit as luxe as I'd expect for dragons, with tables of food... lots of food... platter after platter of cooked swine, hare, and fowl. We've got lush couches and half naked servants to cool us with fans crafted from peacock feathers and palm fronds. They don't seem like Zarentu's style, but I can see how other dragons go for that. The light dragon is a gracious host. I don't bother with the solids, but help myself to a sweating pitcher of ice water.
	Aztram, who joins us says, "If you want to cast any beneficial augmentation spells, this is your chance." The dragons must all already know the drill, because he's looking at me and Tako, the only adventurers in the tent.
	I've already set up a macro for Jordan's Enhanced Dexterity and Vitality buffs, which I enable by wiping my hands on each other. The Enhanced Dexterity makes a whistling wind sound on activation, and vitality sounds a thump-bump, thump-bump, thump-bump effect like a human heart. They're both instant effects, and take no preparation.

Dexterity increased by 10%.

Enhanced Vitality.

	It's the first time I've seen those words in my UI since the merge, and it makes me wonder what Jordan would say if he could see me now, cannibalizing his beloved character. I can only hope he tells me to kick some dragon ass, than bitch about using skills that I hadn't worked for. If he was still alive, it'd be the latter, but I like to think that as he is now, he'd want me to do his legacy proud.

	Explorer of Worlds? What's with that? As far as I know I haven't gained any new titles. Zarentu must have given Brime instruction to place Freeworld magic into context. I'm quietly thankful, so I don't start a riot when they start seeing the effects of Marine's spells.

	I say, "Thanks." and charge into the arena with Bite at my side. Unlike Frobod, I'm a peacock, and greet the audience with a series of handsprings and midair tumbles. I drink in applause, and it tastes like magic. There's a red spot in the dirt, where I await my opponent.
	Herald Brime says, "...and her challenger. Knight of Lord Rendak's court, hailing from Castle Deepwater, Sir Maadzon."
	The knight marches out of the Champion's tent with a heavy shield, a formidable sword, and matching bronze armor the color of a new penny. Each segmented piece of his plate is blackened around the edges as if scorched by fire. A long, red, feather dances in the wind from the top front of his helm. I bow to him, as he takes his spot on a red dot across from me, but he just nods. He's got a pale human face so far as I can see, but we're some fifteen paces away.

1.....
Begin!
	He twirls that longsword in his hand waiting for me to make the first move. It'll be a tank vs. tank battle with him blocking, and me slipping out of the way unless I can take him by surprise.
Shadow Step (Rank 5) - Success!
	I disappear from my current position, and reappear in his shadow standing right behind him. I sweep low with Bite, yanking one foot out from under him, he barely keeps his balance, but I follow up with a roundhouse kick, and then pound his plate with the end of my weapon. Electricity cracks and leaps from the end of Bite, into his armor. He goes sprawling, face against the ground, and body pressed into his shield.

Unlike adventurers with comparatively obscene stats, most non-combative Havenfolk will go down with just a swing of an adventurer's sword. To my right, a big eared boy scuttles into the stadium, with a long hook like they use to change mage lights in Durolan, and a green and orange wreath.

	One more event to go, till we finish this quest, and go back home. It'll be wonderful to collapse in bed.
Chapter 17 - Eye on the Prize
	We're given a chance to rest and recover which I'm thankful for. Though I've been at full health since the healers mended me, four back to back combats before a enormous crowd are mentally draining. I don't feel it as much when we're running dungeons because it's not only me fighting. Having other party members to help carry the load gives me a boost of mental stamina, it's a social experience compared to one on one against people I don't know and it's just me.
	We're all waiting in the Champion's tent, which Lord Zarentu has allowed us to procure until the next event owing to that we now have two contest winners, with bonus rep circlets for Dragonswood Keep. I've just refreshed my buffs when Frobod finally saunters in with a sack of gold clinking in his fist.
	He sings "It's my birthday."
	I say, "Really?"
	He says, "No, but I just got paid."
	I say, "Glad to see you're in a better mood. This morning at the joust it was all. 'I'm here to win.'" I imitate his deep voice.
	He says, "Yeah well, I already won that, and more than half our group has placed top three in their respective events. We're gonna crush it in the group battle."
	Tako bumps fists with him, and says "Word."
	He looks to me and says, "Thanks for the bonus rep head gear, if we win the big melee, maybe he'll offer the log out button."
	No one says anything to that, and I'm thinking it's not likely. I mean, what are the odds that the first quest we get after the hotfix (Did we even get this quest before the hotfix?) will be the one to take us offline? I remember the hopeless feeling so many felt after Archangel's announcement, and I've gotta believe it'll be a lot harder to accomplish than spending the day fighting in a pit.
	He says, "I've got a little girl to get back to."

Chapter 18 - The Rumble
	It's our last battle, and we're assembled in a V formation, with me on point, and Frobod and Tako standing just behind and to either side. The fights leading up to this one were smooth because we work well as a group. Mirae and Laurelade are in the back, as they both wear cloth, and are the most fragile members of the party. As soon as the match starts, they'll fall in behind the melee fighters. Mirae's got her bow, which is good, because if I were the enemy, I'd assume that Laurelade with her wand was the healer.
	I'm not eager to see Laurelade die, but when it comes to accomplishing group objectives, the tanks and healers are the lynchpins of any group. With both of us, we can eventually down most NPC enemies within our challenge range, but without someone to absorb damage, or restore health, you've got a recipe for failure.
	The stands are fuller than I've yet seen
this is the main event. Villagers cheer, and vanquished contenders sitting in the champions' section clap and rumble their support. To my surprise, warriors like Lady Lord Sedin, Lord Maadzan, Lady Ashtara, and all of Frobod's other competitors with one notable absence, wave white cotton banners with purple hand-painted cupcakes. I try to tell myself that they're just NPCs, and their show of support doesn't matter, but the greater part of me beams knowing we've claimed their respect.
	There are five enemy combatants, all Havenfolk, all new to me, every one of them wearing a human shape, and silver and blue tabards depicting a dragon's fang. Three are plate wearers. One in front wields a sword and shield. The guy to his right has a claymore, and one to the left hefts a two handed war axe. 	There's no cloth on the opposing team, just leather. A guy in the back spins a pair of short swords, and the other has no weapons that I can see. I'm pegging the allegedly unarmed one as heals, if they have any at all. NPC groups aren't always logical.

	"Toxic Muffins... the victors!" Herald Brime says, to the answer of uproarious applause. I pump Bite in the air, savoring our victory.
	I think the competition's over when Zarentu motions Brime over with a wave of his forefingers and speaks such that I can't hear.
	"Ahh..."Brime says, "There may be one more challenge." The crowd hushes, as the five of us exchange cautious looks. "Lord Melpomine. Lord Frobod. Step forward." 	Murmurs in the crowd. I look to Frobod, but his head's forward, eyes locked on Brime
focused as a hawk, cold, predatory.
	Brime continues, "Accept victory now, and you will both be Champions. Face off, for one last battle, and one of you will be named Grand Champion of the Dragonswood Tournament. You must both agree to continue the contest. If either of you declines, the Grand Champion title will be forfeit until next year."

Chapter 19 - Rewards

	The two dragons that follow are obviously marvelous because dragon's are sexy and good at everything when they take human form. She's in a blue dress, tight on top, free flowing on the bottom. 	"What the heck?" I look up and see four black dragons breathing shadowy breath over the crowd beating colossal black wings to hover in place. It doesn't burn, but I assume they're the source of the harmful effect. They have glowing green eyes, and shiny black scales. Their bodies are the size of semi trucks. High above them, is a much larger foe of similar colouring. It has a shadowy aura like the negative inversion of a halo in a renaissance painting. It swoops down and perches atop the roof of Lord Zarentu's castle. Its relative proximity to a known object helps me discern that its long neck and bulky body is nearly as long as the building. Dragonswood Keep's flat roof is a cat bed for its body.

	Red light flashes around everyone at the banquet. Magical black chains appear out of nowhere and constrain us, slapping my arms to my side, and pinning them to my body. When I struggle against them, they constrict like metal pythons.
	In party chat I say, "Don't move. If you fight, they get tighter." Sten, of course, doesn't hear me as an NPC and non-member of the party. I feel a little queasy as the boy's forearm lies motionless on the ground a few paces from me. He's just an NPC, but damn... I liked him.
	I yell, "Don't move!" hoping to save Havenfolk lives around me.

Wings sprout from his spine, the color of white gold, as his colossal tale grows longer and longer, pushing dancers aside and knocking over Meat Mountain. His human face elongates into a yellow eyed tyrannosaurus skull filled with glistening white teeth, as he takes to the air. Each pulse of his substantial wings, stir up currents of air around us until he's some forty feet overhead. He's still climbing, but we no longer feel the drafts from his wings. Looking up, I see that Zarentu dwarfs the black dragons that cast the binding spell, though he's a small body compared to the monster atop his keep.
	"Cimmerian! You dare do this now?" Lord Zarentu speaks in a voice of slow rolling thunder. "You come to my keep, and murder my guests. This is the Dragonswood Tournament. There are more than fifty dragons and eight champions on the premises."
	Cimmerian, the great dragon on the castle laughs, "Champions? Mere mortals."

Down the Gullet
Find a way out before you're digested!

For the count of two heartbeats our vision goes black, before we're flying in the air looking down on hills of sunset colored clouds. The wind beats my face.

GLOBAL ALERT
Pohrum is the first Freehaven adventurer to achieve digitization! Welcome to your new home.
For being the first, Pohrum as earned the unique golden title Pioneer. The next 300 adventurers to digitize their consciousness and become a permanent resident of the Freeworld universe will receive standard versions of the title. Additionally, all players who digitize within the next week will receive an imbursement to help them pay for their first Freeworld home.
	"Isn't that our chef?" I ask.

	Wind swirls around us as Lord Zarentu beats his wings, and touches down gently with his hind right claw; a feather trapped in a powerful dragon body.
