	A servant on the other side of the door says, "Sir?"
	Zarentu says, "Send Aztram to my study."
	The old half-elf has tired eyes and disheveled robes when he enters, no doubt exhausted from having to recast protective wards on the property. "You summoned, m'lord?"
	Lord Zarentu says, "Grand Champion Frobod has a favor which may require your expertise. Lady Lord Melpomine was just explaining the origin of adventurers, and how they'd like to return home."
	"Adventurers, sire?" Aztram asks.
	I bring him up to speed, and follow with, "Our presence was once temporary, but now we're trapped. Every time we departed this world our physical bodies faded like ghosts, but now... Ever since the event they're calling the Rip of Worlds we haven't been able to go home. We're stuck in Haven, and can no longer abandon these bodies."
	Aztram says, "You're telling me you'd just come and go as you pleased, and each time you left haven your bodies would disappear?"
	I say, "That's sounds about right."
	Aztram says, "It explains so much, like why adventurers are so careless with their lives, and why none of you seems to have a family tree."
	I shrug. Can't argue with that.
	Frobod asks, "Can you help us?"
	Lord Zarentu looks to Aztram expectantly. He clears his throat awkwardly. "I don't have anything immediately. It'll take research. This is all new and theoretical territory."
	Frobod says, "It's not a theory." The growl in his voice would make a dragon proud.
	"Yes... yes... I believe you." Aztram says, "What I mean is that branches of magic needed to address this problem are largely theoretical."
	Frobod's face contorts into an ugly frown. I put a calming hand on his arm and say, "Like quantum physics." The comment goes right over the heads of Haven folk.
	Lord Zarentu says, "It's settled then. As your first favor, Aztram and his team are tasked with researching ways for you to go home and sharing with you their findings."
	Frobod says, "It's okay to share what you find with the Toxic Muffins. We're all in this together." That last part makes me feel good because it shows that I'm not the only one thinking of us as a lasting team.
	 "... and your next request?"
	"I ahh..." Frobod says. "I hadn't thought of that. If you don't mind parting with some gear from your armory that'd be nice."
	Lord Zarentu says, "Done. I'll have the order sent to my quartermaster." He pauses and looks us all over, "And you Lord Melpomine? As the only other winner of two challenges. I'll hear yours next. You have two favors."
	I take a deep breath, "My first is a political matter. I want to forge an alliance between the Toxic Muffins and you and Dragonswood for the purpose of defeating Cimmerian and stopping the Rip of Worlds from spreading. After the threat's past, I'd like the opportunity to negotiate an ongoing alliance."
	Lord Zarentu looks contemplative. "Dragonswood has never aligned with a guild before, much less a guild of adventurers."
	I say, "Cimmerian and his death cult are a threat to us all, and we need to stand together."
	He holds up a finger. "I have not declined your request yet. What you ask is unusual, but then these are unusual times. The fact that you are adventurers, which I am now to understand are a whole new type of being, lands a point in your favor."
	I ask, "So you accept?"
	"No." Zarentu says, "Dragons are long lived, immortal if left to our natural deaths. The demise of Cimmerian could take centuries. I am not willing to commit Dragonswood to an alliance for so long over a mere victory in the contest, no matter how impressive your skills in battle."
	I deflate under his words, I'd been so certain he'd accept the offer. "..but-"
	He raises his halting hand, the same as when he stopped me to summon Aztram, "I propose a compromise. A one year alignment, at the end of which we will reconvene to determine if an extended relationship is to our favor. Is this fair?"
	I think about the possibilities. A world event shouldn't last more than a few months, so in a way Zarentu has inadvertently offered more than what I asked for though, of course, he couldn't know that. As for renewing the agreement, my gut's telling me that it'll be a reputation grind over the course of the next year to ensure the alliance keeps going. Either that, or winning the contest a second time. I can live with that.
	I say, "I agree, and it is."
	"Done." Zarentu says, "I'll have my scribe draw up the contract. You have one more favor."
	I say, "This is probably one for Aztram, but I'd like an enchantment either on my boots or staff. If you'd be willing to give me access to your enchanters I'd be appreciative."
	Zarentu motions to Aztram to answer my inquiry, "What kind of enchantment do you need?"
	"Damage, stamina, or dexterity." I say. "Something to help me negate armor."
	"... and for the boots?" He asks.
	"The same." I say "I have gloves that do bonus sonic and lightning damage when I punch, I was hoping you could work out something that could do the same for kicks."
	Aztram sounds intrigued, "That's an interesting proposition. I've never seen boots of kicking before. Given some time to study your gloves, I may be able to replicate the effect."
	"... and the staff?" I ask.
	"I have several enchantments that would suit your needs. The first two  that come to mind would add a either holy or light damage to your attacks. The other would have a small chance to heal some of your health and increase your strength on a successful hit."
	"Do I have to choose now?" I ask.
	Lord Zarentu says, "If an enchantment is what you want, I authorize Aztram to do the work for you. Return to Dragonswood Keep when you're ready."
	"It's what I want."
	Zarentu says, "Done. Now for the rest of you."
	The rest of the loot is distributed in much the same manner. Mirae gets to commission a bow, Tako a pair of daggers, and Laurelade gains the opportunity to learn a rare spell. With an alliance forged, and me not anticipating any PvP or single combat for a while, I decide to leave my Fists of the Thunderlord with Aztram to research. Of the two projects, he seemed more excited about creating a new kind of enchantment, so why not throw him a bone? The happier he is doing the work for me, the better chance I have at increasing personal reputation.
Chapter 20 - Risk and Burgers
	It's dark when we leave the keep. I've switched back to pants for the trip home. Archibald says, "Farewell champions." as we exit the gate. I wonder if he and Lyle ever sleep.
	I say, "Ready to ride the lightning?" teasing Frobod, but exiting Dragonswood keep has made him look glum. All the anger and fury of before deflated into a downcast gaze and dragging footsteps. He doesn't respond.
	I say, "It's not over." to remind him of his favor, and Aztram's research.
	Laurelade asks, "How many people can you take when you do that anyway?"
	I say, "Technically, there's no limit assuming you have unlimited intelligence, but with my current gear I only have enough manna for one extra person."
	Mirae says, "Let's take the bikes then."
	I'm about to ask if we should ride or fly, but then decide that flying is better. I pull at my VT and reset the fire and ether Elechoppers so that they're once again cloning the Nightmare's stats. Owing to the tradition of Elechopper rides for guild events, I set up a new voice and gesture macro to summon all the other choppers at once.
	I shout, "INFERNO!!!" and wave my middle finger, my fire bike appears in a pulse of red, and I continue "Let's ride!" while pumping my fist in the air. One after another, ether, wind, water, and earth Elechoppers appear in pops of color corresponding to their elements. They rumble to make their presence known. I say to Frobod, "We're flying since I don't want to hit anything nasty at night on the ground."
	Not much chatter on the way back, so I enjoy the trip imagining I'm riding on a path to the stars. Part of me wishes I had a flying carpet so I could lay back and stare at the sky forever. It burns with the same constellations as the real world, but flickers in a majestic potion of color and fleeting streaks long lost to the glare of concrete jungles of light and technology. We're moving so fast that I only hear wind in my ears, but if stopped I have a feeling I'd be listening to songs of owls, frogs and crickets in the trees below.
	Almost home.
	Durolan is a blue-white beacon in the night, suspended mage lights glimmer on every corner. I was planning to land right in front of Cupcake Keep, but a stone igloo the size of a McDonalds catches my eye. I've never seen it before. Two hooded figures with black angel wings stand guard with black spears with crystal heads. Marking the entrance are a pair of columns painted with blue icons featuring a human shape up top that fades into scattered pixels at the bottom.
	"A digitization station." I say.
	Surprising for Durolan, there's a crowd of about ten people surrounding the silent angels. They call to me as I drop down for a closer look.
	"Don't go in!" A golden haired elf says, he's wearing a fairly standard tunic and trousers for a social character.
	A brunette human woman shouts, "If you go in, there's no guarantee you'll come out."
	In party chat I say, "I'm going to check this out real quick. Won't be long. Head back to the castle if you want."
	Frobod says, "You're not going to..."
	"Relax." I say, "I'm not digitizing after just one day in game."
	Mirae says, "See you there." and the rest of the Muffins fly off to headquarters. I can tell she wants to look by the way she's eyeing the pillars and guardians, but I guess she doesn't want Frobod to make a stink in front of a bunch of protesters.
	Dropping out of party chat, I address the woman who shouted, another player by the name of Ashlynn. All of the protestors are PCs. "You can relax, I'm not entering. What's going on?"
	Ashlynn says, "People are dying."
	"Well... yeah..." I say, feeling uncomfortable about having this simple fact stated so bluntly. "I guess... that's part of the process."
	Ashlynn reiterates, "They're dying and not showing up in game."
	I glance at the angels upon this pronouncement, the elf, named Ceris, says "They don't move or say anything unless you attack or physically block the entrance, but do that, and they'll send you to respawn."
	"That can't be right." I pull out my VT and check the guild roster. Sure enough, Pohrum's there with a little gold star next to his name. All non-hostile player names appear in blue, but I notice that his and two other guild mates names appear in a slightly different shade
a little more royal and less cerulean. "It looks like a few of my guild mates have already er..." I struggle not knowing what to call it "Converted."
	"Then they're the lucky ones." Ashlynn says.
	"It's not everyone." Ceris says. "It's about a one in ten chance you won't come out."
	"Seriously?" I ask.
	"Yeah." Ceris says.
	"That's scary." I say.
	The elf says, "No fuckin' kidding." in a very un-elflike manner.
	"Did it happen to someone you know?"
	The elf shakes his head, "No one I was close to, but a couple of tavern regulars have gone 'offline' since the stations opened. Doesn't take a genius to figure out why."
	Ashlynn says, "I saw it happen. Like a lot of people, I watched the first players go in, and most came out with their title and new player tag color, but two hours later we're still waiting for a couple of them. It was just a few minutes for the others so, if it hasn't happened by now...."
	I finish, "You think it won't happen at all."
	"Exactly." Ashlynn says.
	I say, "Damn..." and scratch the back of my head thinking. I better warn Mirae.
	"Yeah, damn." Ceris says.
	"I'd better go and tell my guild. There are some other people who were considering..."
	Ashlynn says, "Don't let us stop you. The more people we can save the better."
	"Thanks." I say, and lift off the ground on my bike. I'm hovering about five feet off the street when I say, "Let's add each other as contacts. I've got the real world TV upgrade in my guild hall, and a castle guardian that loves watching it and talking about the real world. If I hear anything new, I'll let you know. If you don't mind doing the same, like... if the people you saw disappear come back... I'd appreciate it."
	A few seconds pass, and Ashlynn and Ceris appear as new friends on my contact list. "Thanks again." I say, and begin my ascent over roof tops in a direct line to Cupcake Keep. It only now occurs to me that I just experienced an entire conversation with a bunch of socs, without anyone commenting or even taking a second look at my rare-ass mount. This strikes me as a greater sign of concern than their words could ever be. Social gamers are all about the avatar customizations, and vanity gear.
	I land on the drawbridge, and dismiss my Elechopper with a "Bloop bloop!" sound. Alfred's waiting when I swing the door open.
	"Welcome back Master."
	"Hey." I say, "Where's everyone?"
	Alfred says, "Most are eating in the dining hall."
	As I walk that direction I ask, "Were you able to help Pehrom with his food?"
	"I've granted him access to the kitchen, and use of all our ingredients, but haven't helped with his cooking."
	I say, "That's what I meant." as I slink into the dining room. Frobod, Mirae, Tako, and Laurelade are sitting at the end of the long table closest to the kitchen with food on their plates, and full place settings.
	Tako speaks with a mouth full of food, "I take back everything bad I ever said about goblin chefs. I love this man. If I could have a food baby, I'd be all up in this burger like warm apple pie."
	"Gross." I say. Mirae hits Tako's shoulder with her napkin.
	"Not gross." Tako says, "It's just that good."
	Mirae concedes, "The burgers are tasty."
	"Wait..." I say, "You shag apple pies?"
	Tako takes another bite. His eyes close in pleasure, his jaw moves in exaggerated chewing motions, to show everyone exactly what he thinks of the food. "I'd consider it if they were this good."
	I'm about to respond with something stupid when the door to the kitchen swings open revealing a little, long fingered, green skinned man about a head taller than Laurelade. He's got a big nose, protruding chin, batty ears, and wears a black apron with a poofy toque blanche on his head like a five star chef. Seeing him gives me a clearer picture of Sten's heritage. Poor Sten... I wonder if he'll respawn.
	He gives me a once over and says, "All hail the guild master. Nice to finally meet ya." He's got an upstate New Jersey accent. "Thanks for lettin' me in the kitchen." His voice wells with unexpected emotion, "It means a lot."
	"Hey man," I say, wondering what happened in the real world to make him stop cooking. I'm sure it's every bit as bad as what Mirae experienced. "It's guild property. Use it as much as you like. You should thank Tako for coming up with the new style of cooking."
	Mirae says, "He has... Profusely."
	Pehrom says in an excited voice, "It's just burgers and steak for now, but with the proper herbs and ingredients, there's no tellin' what I could do here."
	I chuckle, "You look so excited. I'm happy for you."
	The goblin says, "There's been a hole in my heart ever since I couldn't cook for people. The craft skill in the game's a bunch of bullshit cuz everything you make tastes like water, and doesn't use any actual skill, but this... I'm one step closer to puttin' me on a plate again." He wipes the corner of his eye.
	Until now, the only VR taste programs have been virtual restaurants, where the 'food' was all generated by the program, and cooking classrooms that allow people to refine their craft for the real world. Neither one of which enable people to cook for their friends in a social setting.
	Frobod says, "What happened to make you stop cooking?"
	Pehrom says, "Fuckin' boats and sharks. Me and my wife were out diving when the fuckin' tour guys turned the engine back on and the propeller started spinning." He makes a 'shrick' sound and a cutting motion over his bicep. "If that ain't bad enough, you don't want to be in the water with blood and a bunch of bull sharks. Barely made it out alive."
	Frobod asks, "You lost your arm?"
	Pehrom says, "Both of em' and a big ole chunk out of my thigh."
	Frobod says, "That's rough. I'm sorry."
	Pehrom says, "I'll tell you one thing. I never used that dive company again." He laughs. "That's some gallows humor for ya." I smile appreciating the way he uses levity to transcend his condition. He looks to me and says, "Tonight boar burgers are on the menu, and fuck all else."
	Deciding that I like our chef, I tease "I'll try the salmon." sure to have a smirk on my face so he knows I'm kidding.
	The goblin shouts, "Fuck all else!"
	I respond, "I'll have a boar burger then."
	In a chipper voice he says, "I'll have it for ya in just a second." and turns to go back to the kitchen. He looks back in the doorway and says, "Nice meetin' ya."
	I help myself to a seat, and Mirae asks "So what's going on with the angels?" I'm not surprised she's the first to ask.
	I say, "The angels? Nothing, they just stand there looking scary and kill anyone who tries to block the way in. The protestors, on the other hand, say people are dying without coming back into the game."
	Mirae says, "How would they know?"
	I say, "The human chick said that she and a few others were watching since the first few went in, and a couple of people didn't come out."
	"Oh." She says, with a note of disappointment sounding in her voice. "I wonder if it hurts to be digitized."
	"Of course it hurts." Frobod says, "You're dying. Stopping your heart forever. It's not supposed to be comfortable."
	Mirae says, "You don't know that. The game can make us feel whatever it wants. That's what makes this whole world work, you know?"
	"Hey." I say, "No fighting at the table." I then look to Mirae, "... but seriously. Some people are just disappearing, without making it back to the game."
	"Is it happening to a lot of people?" She asks.
	"Best guess is ten to twenty percent." I say, padding the numbers so she'll take it more seriously.
	Frobod says, "That's a one in five chance you'll kill yourself for no reason." He pauses a moment to think, "I wonder if the news has more information."
	"Maybe." I say, "With a story this big, a lot can happen in a day. We should check it after dinner."
	Frobod with his empty plate says, "I'm ready now."
	I give him a look that calls him a savage, and say "Well I haven't eaten."
	A few minutes later Pehrom comes back in with a plate full of burger. It's hot, juicy, and overwhelming with the scent of fatty goodness. It even has a whole grain bun. The goblin says, "The bun's nothing because Haven bread's bullshit, but it has texture, and can soak up flavor but the meat's decent." He sets the plate down in front of me. "Bon app
tit."
	I say, "Well it smells good" and take my first bite. I wouldn't say I have the most sophisticated palate but I can taste hints of garlic, pepper, salt and onions mixed in the meat. I swallow and continue, "We've got to start you a garden. Herbs, vegetables, whatever you need."
	"That'd be awesome." Pehrom says.
	I call out, "Hey Alfred!"
	The butler appears in his customary poof of smoke. "Yes?"
	I say, "We need to give Pehrom some space outside for an herb and vegetable garden. See what you can do to make that happen. If you have to take space away from the flowers or jousting strip that's fine. This new food is important."
	"As you wish." He says with a bow. "Is there anything else?"
	"Not now," I say. "I'll let you know if I think of something." The butler disappears as abruptly as he came.
	There's a little dish of red sauce beside my plate that I hadn't noticed before. I ask, "What's this?"
	Pehrom says, "It's a tomato paste, not quite ketchup. Not quite what I wanted, but considering what I had to work with on my first day, it's not bad. I need to make vinegar from scratch before I can get the real flavor."
	I remove the bun and shake a bit of the sauce from the saucer on to the meat. I give it a try. It's like a pizza burger without cheese. I say, "This is the best thing I've tasted in Freeworld or Haven ever."
	Pehrom huffs, "Thanks, it doesn't take much."
	I say, "It's good that Mirae recruited you. We're glad to have you."
	My burger disappears faster than I'd like to admit. I'm a regular Hoover. Just over a day without real food, and I'm sucking down every last reminder of the real world. Wiping the corner of my mouth with a napkin, I look to Frobod and say, "Now we can go watch the TV." I half expect him to say something like "Finally." but he keeps his mouth shut, and leads our parade of well fed adventurers into the lounge.
	It doesn't take long for him to find a channel with a newscaster, the same woman we saw last time, delivering updates. "The death toll rises as over one hundred players of the game Freehaven Online have died."
	The screen cuts to the face of a bewildered man in a Chicago Bulls hat, and wet eyes. The screen cap names him Timothy Buck
Father of Freehaven victim. "They said that if we didn't touch him.... Didn't take the gear off, he'd be okay." His voice breaks, "... but now Jerry's gone."
	Frobod's face grows hard upon seeing this, back to the newscaster, "Freehaven Online continues to provide life support for those who reach out to them, and continues to investigate the whereabouts of all players of accounts that are currently signed in."
	Cut to a company man in a suit at a press conference. "We believe that many of the deaths, unrelated to equipment tampering or failure are linked to Archangel's introduction of digitization."
	A reporter in the crowd says, "According to your most recent statement 56 characters have gone 'offline' since the update, and yet as of that time there had only been 51 instances of intentional removal of a player's hardware, or equipment failure. How do you explain that?"
	The man raises his hand and says, "I have nothing more to say at this time." as he covers the mic with his hand. His tone is now brisk in a way that it hadn't been before. The clicks and clacks of media cameras sound louder on television has he turns to go. He's having a very bad day.
	Frobod says, "Sounds like digitization isn't all what it's cracked up to be."
	"Still," Mirae says, "More people are making it through the process than not."
	Frobod says, "How can you even be sure it's what you want? What if you get digitized and can't feel anything. What if you're whole life is spent experiencing a world like the regular haven food?"
	"It won't be like that." Mirae says.
	"How do you know?" Frobod counters.
	"Because we have two guild members that have already done it and I asked them! They say it's just the same except... sometimes things seem a little more vibrant by comparison to what we have now."
	"What about your family?" Frobod asks.
	Mirae shouts, "They can visit me if they want!"
	I take a deep breath and put a hand on Mirae's arm. "I don't want to lose you." I can feel her tense muscles beneath my fingers. "I don't have a lot of friends Haven side. The people in this room are pretty much all of them."
	She gives me a little hug and says, "I'm not going anywhere." The contact of her body against my own feels good. Something I can trust not to disappear.
	I say, "I don't know about you guys, but I'm beat." It's hard to say whether this is a side effect of being signed in for so long, or if this is a new element of realism imparted by Archangel's update, but I've never felt so tired in a game. My whole body feels the kind of heaviness I used to get after playing in the snow as a kid, coming inside, and plopping down beside the fireplace.
	"I hear ya." Tako says, and looks to Mirae, offering her his hand. "Let's go."
	"Alright." She says, getting to her feet. I get the feeling they want a little quality time before bed.
	Frobod says, "I'm gonna watch TV for a while."
	Laurelade says, "Me too."
	"Suit yourselves." I say, "You have your rooms. What do you say tomorrow we get back to Windroost farming? We still have a chance at the golden title."
	"Whatever." Frobod says, "We'll all be here." The last carries the defeated tone that comes with a reminder that we're trapped in game.
	I look at the TV screen, still the news, and say, "Don't torture yourself. We'll find a way out."
	Laurelade asks, "What's our progress on the quest anyway? I'm too tired to check my VT."
	I pull out my own, and check out the quest interface.

	I say, "First objective complete, 5k out of 50 toward farming."
	"Not bad," Laurelade says, "Considering that we didn't do much killing at the tourney."
	The newscaster says, "In spite of the risks, or perhaps because of them, many continue to log into Freehaven Online, while governments around the world seek to confiscate unused hardware."
	"Take it easy." I say, "I'm going to bed."
	Back in my room, I peel off my battle leathers and set Bite against the wall. It disappears into my inventory as soon as I let go. Now I'm standing here in an oversized tunic like a night shirt. I'm tempted to take a bath, but my heavy body just says, "Go to bed."
	I slip under the covers.
You are in bed.
Would you like to sleep? Yes / No
Chapter 21 - The Lost Champion
	My eyes pop open. Darkness. Shouting in the hall.
	"Melpomine! Wake your ass up!" That's Tako. I turn my head and see the faint glow of blue mage lights outside my room. "Melpomine!" He's a silhouette standing in my door. Apparently I forgot to close it, and the keep won't let him in without permission.
	I groan, "What time is it?"
	"I don't know." He says, "Late. Mirae's missing."
	That catapults me to full alertness. "Missing? Where'd she go?" Probably not the wisest of questions, so I may not be as alert as I thought.
	"I don't know!" Tako says.
	"Well, when did you last see her?"
	"We stayed up late talking until, like, 20 minutes ago and said good night. I found something in my bag that I forgot to give her, but she didn't answer when I knocked on the door."
	"Maybe she's sleeping?" I ask.
	"Check your VT." He says. I do so, pulling up my friends list as he continues talking. "She told me she wouldn't go without telling me."
	"Fuck." I say, seeing what he's talking about. He and I are both listed as being at Cupcake Keep whereas Mirae's location is listed as Durolan. "You don't think she's going for digitization do you?"
	"That's what I'm afraid of." Tako says. "I don't get why she wants to do it so much."
	I sigh, an angry sound that reveals my fear, frustration, and understanding of where she's coming from. "Let's go." I say, "There's no time to rouse the others."
	I fast equip my gear using the VT instead of putting it on manually because every second counts. My heart's beating. Damn it, Mirae. How could you? Alfred appears in a poof as I leave my chambers.
	I say, "Going out Alfred, no time to chat, unless you have information on Mirae."
	Alfred says, "She went out the front door a few minutes ago."
	I say, "That's what I thought. Tako let's move."
	Now, in the courtyard, just on the other side of the draw bridge, I stop, look to Tako and say, "It's time to ride the lightning. Stand still and hold on to your butt." The spell may only save us three or four minutes of travel time over the Elechoppers, but three or four minutes is a hell of a lot when gambling with a friend's life.
	Woosh... woosh... woosh... Bite's spinning, each side like blades in a human sized fan, weaving a pattern of azure circles in the air around me. The energy rises, chi ready to explode from inside, I grab Tako's wrist as lightning reaches down and connects with Bite. Bright white, we're seeing spots, and then nothing as we're yanked into the sky. Now we're looking down on a blanket of night clouds with stars twinkling above us. My hair's flapping behind me like a war banner. It's taking too long considering the short distance we're going, and I curse that the length of the flying scene appears to be the same regardless of distance traveled.
	Our vision blacks, white light, we're seeing spots as we're roughly deposited in a thunderbolt ten paces in front of the stone igloo. Angelic guards stand ready.
	"Mirae!" Tako shouts. "Don't do it!"
	I say, "Let's talk about this."
	We're both running toward her. Tako reaches for her arm, but the angel guards lower their menacing spears. I say, "Don't block her!" knowing that if they attack, he'll die and possibly lose his chance to say goodbye.
	Mirae stops, but doesn't want to look at me. Her back's facing us. "You're not supposed to be here."
	I say, "We're here because we care about you. Toxic Muffins. You're are healer. We've got a good thing going."
	Mirae says, "I told you I'm not going anywhere. We'll still have a good thing when I come out."
	Tako blurts, "I'll be your boyfriend! Please, anything! I don't want you to die!"
	Mirae says, "You can still be... in a few minutes. When I come out of there."
	Tako says, "No." and grabs her.
	The two angels spring into motion, sinister statues infused with life. Not a heartbeat passes before the two crystal tipped weapons are buried in his chest. He's been one-shotted. The angel on the right pulls its spear free, a smear of crimson covering the tip. The angel to the right does the same. Tako's digital body shimmers and fades from the ground like a ghost. Just a dark blotch on the cobble stones to show that he had been.
	Mirae says, "He'll be back at the keep in a few minutes. He changed his bind point. We did it together so we'd always end up at the same place."
	"Mirae..." I say, "I understand why you're doing this, just... wait a little longer. At least until they have it fixed."
	Mirae says, "If I wait the whole thing could be disabled. As soon as they crack whatever defenses Archangel's set up they can get to rolling back all the stolen technology. This may be the only shot I have at..."
	"At?" I ask, hoping to keep her talking.
	"At being normal! I don't want to be someone's burden anymore."
	"I get that... I really do."
	"Do you?" She asks. "Everywhere I go, and in everything I do someone's got to take care of me. That's not even counting the financial aspects. Just time. They can't even go on vacation together because of little old me, or they could but someone has to sit in the hotel babysitting instead of snorkeling or parasailing. Can't go to the amusement park, because cripples can't ride roller coasters. I'm holding everyone back, and I couldn't change that until now. Once I transition, and the logout gets sorted, they can visit me any time they want."
	I say, "There's a one in five chance you won't come out of there."
	Mirae says, "Those are good odds, because it leaves me with a four in five chance that I'll be fine. Better than fine because I'll be where I'm happiest... with people like you. This could be a one in a lifetime shot, and considering what I have to go back to... I'm ready to make this place my home. I want to be a Pioneer."
	I say, "Mirae just..."
	"This is my life. Let me have this. For the first time since I was eleven years old, I have a say in my own destiny."
	I can already feel her slipping away, a tear rolls down my cheek. She turns, takes some steps toward me, and wipes it away with her thumb. "I'm coming back." She says, "When I get back here you'll be my family. Big sister with a cool job, okay?"
	I sniff, and pray for no more tears because I want to be strong... for her. "Okay." I say, "I'm the tank, but you've got more courage than all of us." I hate that I'm letting her do this. "Give me a hug."
	She wraps her arms around my body, and I rock the girl in my arms, never wanting to let go. I commit her smell to memory, earthy, and perfumed. Like a proper elf from any fantasy. I whisper, "I'll be waiting" into the side of her head. I let go with a heavy heart.
	She says, "I need to get on with this before Tako comes back and gets sent for another respawn. I don't need that on my conscience."
	"Alright..." I say.
	She smiles, the most beautiful smile I've ever seen her make, and says "Thank you." I take a snapshot for later viewing. "Just sit tight for a few minutes, and I'll be right back."
	She turns, and enters the igloo.
* * * * *
	"Mirae, no!" A tiger roars as Tako leaps off his mount, and comes sprinting at me. It's a black cat unlike anything in the real world, with even blacker stripes.
	I say, "She just went in."
	Tako punches me in the shoulder, hard, but not enough to send me flying. There's no health loss, and I assume that's why we're not surrounded by city guardians. "How could you let her go?"
	"It was her decision." I feel like another person as I'm talking. "I couldn't have stopped her if I wanted. I tried to talk, but if I'd raised a hand to her, I'd be sent for respawn the same as you." I take a deep shaky breath, "All we can do is wait, and hope she comes out."
	"How long does it take?" Tako asks.
	"I don't know." I say.
	"Hey angels! How long does it take?" They stand silent and stoic as ever. Tako says, "It's taking too long. It's taking too long! She should be out already. How long can it take?"
	"She just went in." I say, "Give her a few minutes before we start jumping to conclusions."
	Minutes pass, I enable the clock in my user interface to keep tabs on it. Each second ticks painfully. The more of them that pass, the more uncertain I become in my stomach. I whisper, "C'mon Mirae, you're the strongest."
	Tako says, "She's not coming back. She's not coming back. How could you let her go?"
	How could I? It had seemed like the right thing at the time. Should I have pressed harder and made her angry at me, resentful? What would that have accomplished, she'd have just walked through the door anyway. Would she have? Was there anything I could do?
	In a quiet voice, almost too soft to hear under the ambient sounds of Durolan at night, I say, "I don't know."
	I hate waiting, each breath makes my heart beat in my ears a little louder, Tako's physically shaking. This can't be happening. I peer down the tunnel toward whatever awaits adventurers seeking digitization and see nothing. There's a closed wooden door separating the living from the great beyond. This... can't... be... happening.
	"Mirae!" I call, into the building, but no one answers. The angelic guards loom with their spears.
	"How long has it been?" Tako asks, his voice rattled.
	"I say, I don't know." and call again at the building, "Mirae!"
Mirae has gone offline.
	"NO!!!!!" Tako shouts, in a voice that sounds more beast than human. He collapses into my chest, sobbing. I feel the burn of tears streaking down my face.
	I killed her.
	In a voice so rattled that I hardly recognize it as my own, I say "You said that you would come out..." I sob, and pull Tako closer to my breast desperately needing human contact. I can't even imagine the guild without Mirae, and every time I try, thick crystal tears drop off my face. "Come back..." I whisper, "You will never be a burden."
	I collapse to my knees feeling seared and hollow, staring at the tunnel of no return. Tako sits cross legged beside me, still sniffling, and wiping tears from his face. We just sit and stare for over an hour hoping for a change. Sometimes my mind plays tricks on me as I think I see something moving at the end of the tunnel, but it's just a lie. A lie of my subconscious to give me hope, and make me feel better. It's not working. Every time I think my tears have dried up, I remember the elf's smiling face as she rode her first Elechopper, or danced with Tako, and more come pouring.
	"God sucks," I sniffle. "He takes us young, and he takes the best of us. Mirae was good, she deserved a fucking chance."
	A hand on my shoulder startles me. I look back and see Frobod looming over me, over us.
	"You weren't in the keep, and I saw she was offline. I'm sorry." His voice is somber.
	I sniffle and wipe my eyes. "What are we going to do? It wasn't supposed to turn out this way."
	A pause from Frobod before he says, "We go back home and plan her funeral. I can't agree with her decision, but she was our friend."
	Tako moans, "She's not gone. I can't believe she's gone..."
	"Neither can I..." I say.
	Frobod just nods, and extends his hand, helping me up off the street.
* * * * *
	The next twenty-four hours are spent planning the funeral, and reaching out to all of Mirae's friends via the guild mates she recruited. Most of them had already joined, but those more interested in socializing in town than going on adventures received their invitations to Cupcake Keep. Everything comes together fast due to us living in a digital environment, though Pehrom laments that he hasn't had more time to round up viable ingredients to make the most of his craft before the feast in her honor.
	Now we're gathered around a special corner of the garden that's been trimmed into a presentable state. I went back to Dragonswood Keep to collect her custom bow, which now serves as her tombstone, set on display among the roses, upon a magical 'rack' that makes it hover in air surrounded by a column of green light
her favorite color.
	I say, "Mirae was my first new friend upon coming back to Haven. She rescued me from some bandits, and then joined us for our first run through the Windroost. It's hard to believe that was just a few days ago, but in our time together she left a deep impression. She was a force of good in the world, thinking of others before herself, and wanting to leave this place a little bit better than she found it. She succeeded, with me at least, because I feel that I am a better person for having known her." I motion to all of the people she recruited, as well as the friends she didn't. "You guys were what were most important to her. She almost didn't join the Toxic Muffins because she didn't want to leave you. You're the reason that Haven became her happy place, and why she made the choice that she did. Those of you who know about her personal life know that this was where the best of her shined brightest." A number of her friends nod their heads, even in death not outing her disability. "Before she went in she told me I'd be her big sister. I hope I can live up to that title. I also hope that she's happy, wherever she is now, because she deserves it."
	I step aside and allow her other friends to speak, some of which have known her since the launch of this game. It makes me feel good to know that they at least know what happened to her, but my heart grows heavier knowing that I'm still not any closer to discovering Jordan's old friends. I will in time.
	A moment of silence for Mirae, and we all turn to head back into the keep. I'm not hungry, but Pehrom has worked hard to prepare a tasty meal, even if it does consist of wild game steaks, and hamburger. The goblin catches my arm as I linger toward the back of the procession.
	"Hey ahh..." He starts, sounding awkward in his New Jersey accent, "I just want to say thanks for what you're doin' here. A lot of people ya know, they hear about a girl like her, start callin' us Feebs n' shit, but not you. You're a good guild leader."
	"She was my friend." I say.
	"Yeah I know." The goblin says. "But I'm glad she brought us together, and I'm glad to be one of your Muffins. I know it ain't the same, but my kid game sister's got some healin'. She can take care of you."
	"Thanks." I say having not thought about who'd be replacing Mirae's heals in our groups. I just figured that since she recruited so many members, there'd have to be at least some heals among them. "Pohrum, right?"
	"Nah, that's my game brother. Pahrem's my sister."
	"I've seen her." I say.
	"Yeah, well... I'll make the introduction proper over dinner. So you can talk about what your planning."
	"Planning?" I ask.
	"Fuckin' Dragonsbane man. Get with the program. We're all here for two reasons, Mirae, and the fuckin' Dragonsbane quest. She wanted it. Best thing I can think while we're all here is to roll hard, and get us some golden stars."
	"The title..." I say.
	"You're damn right the title. Let's do it for Mirae, she wouldn't have split up our old guild if she didn't want it. We've got to make us the world first." I reach out, bend over, and give the little man a hug. He says, "Hey naw... What is that?"
	"Just my way of saying thank you." I say, "Being digital and all with her inventory off limits, there's not much left to hold on to, but if we can get that title and dedicate it to her... It's something we can always remember."
	"I know." the goblin says, "That's what I've been sayin'. First thing tomorrow we've got to be hunting dragons. Can't let some other guild beat us to it."
	"Fuck tomorrow." I say, imitating his speech patterns. "We're farming the Windroost tonight."
	The goblin says, "That's what I'm talking about."
Epilogue - The Dragon Slayers
	It's a week later. No word on getting out, so I can only assume my body's hooked up in a hospital bed somewhere.
	I yell, "Nothgoran! Go!"
	Nothgoran health 2%. I slip out of the way of his massive axe, and counterattack with Bite. Electric worms squirm over his armor. Tako stabs from behind. Frobod crashes into him with his sword, and our big eared healer lobs an orb of sickly goop which she insists is not a grenade.
	I shout, "Take cover!"
	The ball explodes sending corrosive slime everywhere. It certainly seems like a grenade to me. A grenade of acidic Jell-O. I'm okay thanks to a resistance spell Pahrem stuck on me. The slimy projectiles slink right off, but Frobod and Tako both leap, duck and roll. I thought that was supposed to be for fires.
	Nothgoran takes the brunt of the impact and falls to his knees, streamers of caustic smoke rising from his armor. He then collapses on his face.
Congratulations!
You have completed Dragonsbane - Part One.
Visit merchants in the capitol cities to discover dragon slaying items.
You have obtained the title Dragonslayer.
Freehaven First!
	Cheers erupt from my guildies, as he goes down, and we all get the notice. Golden "Dragonslayer" titles appear next to Tako and Pahrem's names as they toggle their displays. I pull out my VT and do the same, in just a few seconds we're all golden Dragonslayers. I kiss Bite's shaft and raise it in the air, and say "This one's for you Jordan." I look into the sky beyond the balcony and say, "...and Mirae for you. You're the best siblings ever."
The following quest is now available.
Limited Edition - Guild Quest - Dragonsbane - Part 2
Difficulty: Variable
You have discovered the identity of the extra-planar dragon, and have practiced slaying its kin. Destroy Cimmerian and his dark cultists to prevent the rip between worlds from spreading.
Objectives:
Destroy Cimmerian 				0/1
Cultists Slain					3/450
Slay 100,000 dragons or dragon kin.	50,000/100,000
Rewards:
Title: 			Dragonsbane
Reputation: 	10,000 Reputation in All Playable Factions
Extremely rare reagents that can be used to make items of great power.
Do you accept? 	Yes / No
	I tap yes, and send out a guild announcement.
"You all rock! Thanks for all your hard work. It looks like we've got a new quest."
	Frobod breathes heavily and says, "Twelve runs today. I'm beat. I need a hot bath, and some goblin burgers."
	I say, "I think we could all use them."
	Pahrem is a cute goblin girl with green skin, purple hair, and cheeks flecked with darker green freckles. Her khaki robes with a faint dark halo somehow combine the pocket utility of cargo pants with the mystique of high wizardry. She says, "So whatta ya wanna do next? Dragonsbane Two, or The Lost Champion?" She has the same accent as her game brother, a common trait among Haven goblins.
	I pause and say, "I've got a guy to see in the morning about The Lost Champion. If it's what I think it is, we're all going to hell."
The End
Freehaven Online:
Dragonsbane
Book One of Freehaven Online
AUTHOR'S NOTE
Dear Reader,
Thank you for reading my novel. I hope you enjoyed it. It was a labor of love borne of long years of online, table top, and live action gaming. As a newer author, my ultimate goal is to be able to write books like this for you full time so that there will be more adventures for you to devour. If you enjoyed this book, the best way to make that happen is to post glowing reviews on Amazon or Goodreads to encourage more readers to experience this story. They mean a lot.
I'd also like to give special thanks to the
 HYPERLINK "http://www.facebook.com/groups/LitRPGsociety"
GameLit Society
 for their continued efforts in bringing fans and authors of LitRPG/GameLit together and nurturing our community.
You're the best,
Jun
Glossary
Term
Definition
Novel Specific
Adventurer
An in-game term used to reference player characters, with a strong association with hunting monsters, and taking foolish risks with their lives.
Agro
The hostile attention of an attacking monster. If a player is being attacked by a non-player character, they have agro.
Alias
An in game identity with a unique appearance, name, and reputation score.
Attributes
Stats that represent a character's basic capabilities prior to equipping magical items, or other forms of magical items. In Freehaven online, attribute scan only be raised via point breaks.
No - Includes novel specific implementation
Avatar
A player's in-game body
Bind Point
A place where a player character re-appears in the game after death
Boss
A powerful monster that must be defeated to complete a dungeon.
Buff
An in-game beneficial effect with a limited duration that enhances a property or properties of a character
Challenge Rating
A score given to PCs and NPCs to reflect how difficult they would be to defeat in single combat based on the number of points invested into attributes. PC (and pet) challenge ratings are displayed as "Easy," "Average," "Very Difficult." etc. Whereas NPCs display as question marks, the more of which are present the greater the difficulty becomes.
Class
Short for classification. A term that defines a character's play style and abilities they have access to. Freehaven online does not use a class system, however players often refer to themselves as having a class to reflect the abilities they've learned and preferred style of game play. Some classes in other games, such as Paladin, refer to social titles earned through game play.
No - Includes novel specific implementation
Cooldown
The period of time a player must wait before using the same spell or ability again. For example, a spell with a 30 minute cooldown can only be used once every 30 minutes.
Crafting
The in-game production of goods that can be used by players.
Debuff
An in-game harmful effect with a limited duration that weakens a property or properties of a character
Damage per second. Refers to either a character's damage output, or their role as damage dealers in a party
Drop
The act of a (usually non-player) character leaving behind items for players to collect after their defeat. When used as a noun, this refers to in-game objects that can be collected after defeating an opponent.
Dungeon
A special location with powerful monsters that can be fought with the promise of loot.
Enchantment
A permanent spell cast upon an item to enhance its properties
Farm
The act of playing the game in a repetitive manner with the intent of generating a high number of the same item, reputation, or statistic.
Gank
To be killed in player vs. player combat suddenly, without having had a chance to react or prepare for combat.
Guild
A player run organization that serves as an in-game community. Guilds often have themes such as social, raiding, crafting, etc.
Healer
A character that restores health to injured characters.
Hotfix
An update applied to a computer game while players are still connected.
Kite
A gaming tactic where a player does just enough damage to an enemy to hold agro, while continually fleeing, thus causing the enemy to chase them.
Loot
In-Game rewards for killing monsters, and or completing quests. When used as a verb, it is a reference to collecting these rewards from defeated opponents
Manna
Mystical energy that is used to power spells.
Name Plate
Text that appears above or beside a character's avatar in the user interface revealing their name, and any titles they choose to display. An expanded name plate also reveals their appearance and challenge ratings.
No - Includes novel specific implementation
Noob
A new/inexperienced gamer. Also used as an insult to experienced gamers to imply that they don't know what they're doing.
Non-Player Character
Player Character
Point Break
Also known as a "Ding." The equivalent to leveling, when a character receives additional points to invest in their attributes.
Player vs. Environment - Refers to playing the game against computer generated opponents
Player vs. Player - Refers to fighting against other players in the game as opposed to computer generated opponents
Derived from "own," to mercilessly defeat an opponent.
Raid
A large number of players (typically greater than five) assembled into a single group for a common purpose (typically completing a dungeon).
Raiding
The act of exploring dungeons and killing NPC monsters for loot, currency, and experience
Range DPS
DPS characters that can inflict significant damage at range (example: Archers, fireball tossers)
Reputation
A numerical value that measures how NPCs will react to you. Higher reputation leads to better treatment, and unlocking higher tiers of reputation often results in added benefits such as lower prices from merchants, preferential treatment, and access to exclusive in game items.
Short for "resurrection." A spell used for returning a fallen character back to life, without the in-game penalties for dying.
Respawn
The act of coming back to life after an in game death without the aid of a resurrection spell.
Roll
Typically refers to rolling a computer generated 10 sided dice to determine who will obtain loot. It can also refer to the conscious activation of a non-combative skill such as spotting.
No - Includes novel specific implementation
Skills
Learned abilities. In Freehaven Online, skills can only be advanced by training, and/or actively using the skill/ability in the game.
No - Includes novel specific implementation
Short for "Social Gamer." Players that are more interested in hanging out, chatting, and engaging in non-combative game play
Spells
Magical abilities that require manna to activate.
No - Includes novel specific implementation
Stats
Statistics - Numerical representations of a character or item's capabilities
Tank
A character designed to take/absorb significant amounts of damage and hold the attention of attacking enemies
Trainer
A non-player character that can teach player characters new abilities, and/or skills.
Vendor Trash
Useless items that can be sold to merchants for currency.
Wipe
When all characters in a party die.
